# Decisions

Short ADRs (date, decision, alternatives, reason). Agents and game sessions write fragments in `docs/decisions/<agent or game id>.md`; the lead merges platform fragments here. Game sessions keep theirs in `docs/decisions/<id>.md` only.

## From the concept phase (17-18 Oct 2026, moved here from the planning CLAUDE.md and README)

- Shortlist: Generator Siege, Tank Crew and Turbo Reel (fishing). Kart Rush stays in the backlog (BRIEF 3.12) as P2 notes.
- Dropped ideas, not to be revived without asking the project owner: Boss Raid (WATTGAMES already does interval boss fights), Ramp Tower and Tug-of-War (too thin), Paceline TTT (Zwift covers it), Pedal Survivors, Track Night and Stealth Heist.
- Packaging: one platform brief, then one session per game. Everything lives in this repo as one app with a game picker.
- Art direction (after concept round 1):
  - Generator Siege: a low-poly 3D miniature with simple primitives, in an alternate-history Roman fort powered by treadwheels driving a bronze flywheel generator with a watt readout. Enemies are realistic but not detailed; people are tiny, with no close-up riders. Four treadwheels, one per player.
  - Tank Crew: 2D pixel art in a dark, oily alternate 1940s, brutalist super-heavy tanks in x-ray cutaway with one crude engine per task (diesel, electric, steam), one tank layout each for 1, 2 and 3 players, enemy tanks only, no people.
  - Turbo Reel: 2D pixel art on one shared ocean, hand-authored in a muted, limited palette.
- 2D pixel art (Tank Crew, Turbo Reel): whatever is fastest and looks good. The platform's `tools/art` generates sprites on fal with GPT Image 2.5, then pixelates them to a fixed palette and animates them by parts; Turbo Reel draws its sprites as pixel grids in code instead.
- Concept art: round 2 is 7 images from GPT Image 2.5 Sunburst on fal (1536 x 864, high quality), reviewed in `docs/concept/NOTES.md`; every request is in `docs/concept/manifest.json`. About $2.83 of the $5 concept-art budget is left; the owner adds fal credits for the games' art.
- Art prompts describe original designs only: no brand names or logos (no Wahoo, Zwift or Strava), no existing characters or franchises, and no flags, insignia or national markings on the 1940s tank art.
- `docs/concept/` is reference material for the build, not shipped content; images are committed as JPEG.
- Toolchain choices baked into the brief (see `docs/research.md`, "Decisions baked into the prompt"): Godot 4.7.2 with GDScript and the Compatibility renderer; GDBLE for Bluetooth with a Python bridge as fallback; host-authoritative multiplayer through a small Node relay with room codes; % of FTP fairness by default; FIT export and optional upload to each rider's own Strava API app; GUT 9.7.1 with strict pass rules; procedural or pipeline-generated art, an OFL font and CC0 or synthesized audio.

## Platform build (W0-W4, from 21 Oct 2026)

Merged from the fragments in `docs/decisions/` (the fragments stay as the per-module source; game sessions keep `docs/decisions/<id>.md` and never edit this file).

### lead

#### 2026-10-21: Godot binary is the .NET 4.7.2 build

- Decision: run and test with the Godot 4.7.2 .NET (mono) console build, which reports `4.7.2.stable.mono.official.ed1daf0bf`. The wrappers resolve `$GODOT`, then `tools/godot_bin.local` (gitignored, machine-local) in this or the main checkout, then `godot` on PATH.
- Alternatives: download the standard build (BRIEF 2); require `GODOT` in every shell.
- Reason: the maintainer develops with this binary; BRIEF 2 allows a .NET build for running and testing. Exports need the standard build's export templates (BRIEF 7.10), documented in the README.

#### 2026-10-21: Frozen W0 stubs for cross-module classes

- Decision: besides `addons/wattlands/contracts/`, W0 wrote stub files with frozen public signatures for every class another module calls (governor, LocalRider, GameHost, NetSession, registry, HUD layer, pedal-scan list, pixel viewport, ...). Owners fill the bodies and may add API, never change it.
- Alternatives: contracts as abstract base classes only; let each W1 agent design its own API and reconcile in W2.
- Reason: 15 builders work in parallel; a frozen, documented call surface keeps W2 merges mechanical.

#### 2026-10-21: Autoload classes are named Wl<Name>

- Decision: autoload `X` is an instance of `class_name WlX` (`WlEventBus`, `WlNetSession`, ...).
- Alternatives: no class_name on autoload scripts.
- Reason: Godot refuses a script class with an autoload's name; named classes let tests and in-process simulations create private instances (one EventBus and NetSession per simulated peer).

#### 2026-10-21: Two clocks, fixed steps, no wall clock in rules

- Decision: one `SimClock` drives platform time (always runs: trainer, governor, mock riders, telemetry) and game time (stops on pause: `sim_step`, phase elapsed, directive expiry). Real-time play feeds wall deltas to `SimClock.advance`; headless runs call `step()`.
- Alternatives: `_process` deltas everywhere; `Engine.time_scale`.
- Reason: BRIEF 3.6 and 6 demand fixed 0.05 s steps and determinism; BRIEF 4.1 needs trainer I/O to keep running while the game is paused.

#### 2026-10-21: Game events go through GameModule.game_event

- Decision: `GameModule` gets an additive `signal game_event(event)` and `send_event(event)`; the GameHost batches events (at most 10 messages per second), sends them in order and calls `apply_event` on clients only. Every event has a `"type"` key, which `int_fields()` uses.
- Alternatives: `ctx.events` (the EventBus) for game events.
- Reason: BRIEF 3.6 names `apply_event` but no emitter; the game briefs need ordered, batched events.

#### 2026-10-21: PlayerSlot field names

- Decision: the FTP field is `ftp` (watts); the Autopilot flag is `autopilot`; the look fields are `color`, `shape`, `pattern`, `hollow`. Backticked BRIEF names are kept exactly (`ftp_verified`, `p_eff_per_w`, `rider_mass_kg`, `preferred_cadence_rpm`, `haptics_on`, `is_bot`, `options`); `haptic_intensity` is added.
- Reason: the game briefs refer to "the slot's FTP" and "Autopilot flag" without code names.

#### 2026-10-21: Window base size and stretch

- Decision: base viewport 1920 x 1080, `canvas_items` stretch with `expand` aspect, window override 1280 x 720 for windowed runs.
- Alternatives: stretch disabled with the HUD scaling itself.
- Reason: HUD sizes are specified at 1080p (BRIEF 3.9); a 640 x 360 PixelViewport stays integer-scaled at 720p, 1080p and 1440p.

#### 2026-10-21: Phase kinds, modes and rule names are strings

- Decision: `Wl.WARMUP`, `Wl.ERG`, `Wl.SOLO`, `Wl.CLAMP_*` and friends are String constants.
- Alternatives: GDScript enums.
- Reason: they appear verbatim in JSON content, network messages and run_game_sim reports.

#### 2026-10-21: W1 split sdk-runtime into sdk-runtime and sdk-tools (16 builders)

- Decision: BRIEF 8's W1 role sdk-runtime became two builders. sdk-runtime owns `addons/wattlands/sdk/` and `tests/sdk/` (GameRegistry, GameHost, PlanValidator, DirectiveSink, the conformance test). sdk-tools owns the template (`src/games/_template/` and its content, assets and tests), `tools/run_game_sim`, `tools/new_game`, `tools/sdk/`, `tests/tools/` and `docs/GAME_SDK.md`. W1 therefore had 16 builders (`docs/OWNERSHIP.md`); `GameSimRunner.parse_args` (sdk-runtime) stays the one flag parser both sides use.
- Alternatives: one sdk-runtime builder, as the brief lists it.
- Reason: it was the brief's broadest W1 role, and the tools, the template and the SDK guide need only the frozen W0 SDK stubs, so they could be built in parallel with the runtime (the template's unit tests run on recording fakes; its integration tests stayed pending until the runtime landed in W2).

#### 2026-10-21: W2 and W4 ran as the lead plus up to 3 helpers

- Decision: W2 (integrate) and W4 (fix and polish) both used BRIEF 8's W2 shape, "you plus up to 3 helpers": each round three area fixers, each in its own worktree and branch, merged into `main` by the lead (W2 rounds: `core`, `sdk`, `app`; W4 rounds: `trainer`, `session`, `app`; for example `merge: w4r2/trainer`). Two rounds each.
- Alternatives: one fixer per module or per finding (W4's "parallel fixers grouped by area" allows more); the lead alone.
- Reason: findings cluster by area, so three fixers own disjoint paths and the merges stay mechanical; more parallel Godot runs would only queue on the machine-wide lock (2 slots, 4 on a large machine).

#### 2026-10-21: The GUT gate fails on pending or risky tests (W3 QT-3)

- Decision: `tools/run_tests` (`tools/lib/gut_gate.mjs`) fails when any GUT test is pending or risky (a test with no asserts), counted from the GUT summary's "Risky/Pending" line and the JUnit file, in full and narrow (`--gdir`) runs alike. The only exceptions are environment guards listed in `tests/pending_allowlist.txt` (a test file, or `file::test_name`): today the GDBLE surface tests, which call `pending()` when the GDBLE library isn't loaded.
- Alternatives: GUT's own pass rule (exit 0 with pending tests); a pending-count threshold.
- Reason: GUT exits 0 for pending and risky tests and counts them in JUnit, so a regression that trips a `pending()` guard, or a test that lost its asserts, stayed green. An explicit allowlist keeps the real environment guards visible and reviewable.

#### 2026-10-21: --sim-speed only with MockTrainers (W3 SAF-3)

- Decision: a sim speed other than 1 applies only to riders on a `MockTrainer`. `WlTrainerService.effective_sim_speed()` (the menu clock) and `AppSessionLauncher.effective_sim_speed()` (the session clock) return 1 for any other device, and `GameHost.setup()` resets a `sim_speed` other than 1 to 1 when any local rider's device is not a MockTrainer, each with a `push_warning`.
- Alternatives: refuse to start with a real trainer; ignore the flag only above 1; check only in the app.
- Reason: every governor time rule (slew, write rate, rescue, coasting, warm-up, haptics) runs on platform time, so x4 meant 120 W/s slew and 8 writes a second on a real trainer and x0.25 stretched rescue and coasting (BRIEF 4.5, 4.6). The GameHost check also covers tools and tests that build their own GameHost.

#### 2026-10-21: A Windows export tool (W3 PKG-1, PKG-4, PKG-5)

- Decision: `node tools/export_windows.mjs` exports the Windows Desktop preset into `build/windows/` from a clean copy of the tracked files (`build/export_src`), so the working tree is never touched. With a .NET editor it points the copy's preset at the standard `windows_release_x86_64.exe` as a custom template (`--template`, or the installed `<version>.stable` export templates), because a .NET editor looks for .NET templates. It writes `LICENSE.txt`, `CREDITS.md` and `THIRD_PARTY_NOTICES.txt` next to the exe and fails when the GDBLE DLL is missing. `--with-vcruntime` copies `vcruntime140.dll` (and `vcruntime140_1.dll`) from System32 next to the exe, an app-local Visual C++ runtime for the GDBLE DLL. `--smoke` runs the exported console exe headless with a mock trainer for 8 s, locks set and a temp profile, and fails on a non-zero exit or a script error. Godot runs take the machine-wide lock.
- Alternatives: export from the working tree with the editor's own templates; install the standard editor only for exports; ship the VC++ redistributable installer or require it.
- Reason: BRIEF 7.10 needs a Windows build that ships the GDBLE DLL, and the development editor is the .NET build. The notices travel with the exe. A fresh PC without the VC++ runtime shows no trainers, so the README names the redistributable and the app-local copy stays opt-in. The smoke run checks the exported build itself, not the source tree.

#### 2026-10-21: Atkinson Hyperlegible and CC0 audio stay BRIEF 3.9 fallbacks

- Decision: v0.1.0 ships Godot's default font and synthesized sound, BRIEF 3.9's fallbacks (as the ui-hud ADRs chose in W1). The font is a drop-in: put `AtkinsonHyperlegible-Regular.ttf` and `AtkinsonHyperlegible-Bold.ttf` with `OFL.txt` in `addons/wattlands/ui/theme/fonts/`, record them in `docs/deps.d/` and the credits, and import; `WlTheme` switches with no code change (`addons/wattlands/ui/theme/fonts/README.md`). Recorded sounds can replace a built-in through `Sfx.register(name, stream)`, with a credit.
- Alternatives: download the font and a CC0 pack during the build.
- Reason: a file download needs explicit approval, which this build did not have; BRIEF 3.9 names both fallbacks.

#### 2026-10-21: The lead CI workflow runs the full tools/run_tests

- Decision: `.github/workflows/lead.yml` runs every BRIEF 7 gate in one job on every push and pull request, as `tools/run_tests` runs them locally: ubuntu-latest, Godot 4.7.2's Linux build, Node 22, gdtoolkit 4.5.0 from pipx, both locks set. That covers the import, the full GUT suite, the scaffold check, `check_scripts`, the game sims with `fit_check`, the Node suites and the 3-peer end-to-end test. The logs are kept as an artifact. The module workflows keep their narrower suites.
- Alternatives: the module workflows only; one job per gate.
- Reason: each module workflow runs only its own part, so no CI job ran the command that decides green. It installs gdtoolkit 4.5.0 so the static gate's gdlint and gdformat --check run, as they do locally.
#### 2026-10-21: gdformat and gdlint are part of the static gate, configured by ./gdlintrc

- Decision: every .gd under `addons/wattlands/`, `src/`, `tests/` and `tools/` is formatted with gdformat (gdtoolkit 4.5.0, its default 100 columns). The static step of `tools/run_tests` fails on any gdlint problem and on any file `gdformat --check` would change whenever gdtoolkit 4.5.0 is available (`gdlint` and `gdformat` on PATH reporting 4.5.0, else `uvx --from gdtoolkit==4.5.0`). Without it the step still passes with "gdtoolkit unavailable, skipped"; `--no-lint` skips lint; `--strict-lint` is a no-op kept for old command lines. `./gdlintrc` is gdlint's default config (`gdlint --dump-default-config`) with `class-definitions-order` disabled, `max-line-length` 120, `max-public-methods` 100, `max-returns` 25 and `max-file-lines` 3000; every naming rule stays, and findings are fixed in code, never with `# gdlint: disable`.
- Alternatives: keep lint reported but non-fatal (docs/decisions/tooling.md); gdlint's defaults (100 columns, 20 public methods, 6 returns, 1000 lines, the fixed member order); format only new files.
- Reason: a style gate that only reports never converges (2214 gdlint problems and 337 unformatted files when this landed), and game sessions copy the template, so it has to be clean. `class-definitions-order`: our files group members by role (a constant or signal next to the code that uses it, test helpers next to their tests), which a fixed order would scatter. 120 columns: gdformat wraps code at 100 but can't split comments, long string literals or regexes. 100 public methods: the SDK facades (GameHost, NetSession, SafetyGovernor, LocalRider, HudLayer, the app screens) have wide, documented APIs. 25 returns: validators and CLI parsers return one error per check, which reads best as early returns. 3000 lines: NetSession, GameHost, SafetyGovernor and the lobby screen are long and cohesive; splitting them for the linter would hurt.
- Consequences: gdformat 4.5.0 has bugs Godot exposes. A lambda too long for one line, nested in a call or collection, is wrapped into a form Godot rejects ("Unindent doesn't match the previous indentation level") and can duplicate the file's comments into it; a comment above a dictionary entry it expands can move into the entry; a string literal with a raw line break stops gdtoolkit. The formatting pass replaced such lambdas with methods or local variables, put moved comments back and wrote `\n`; CLAUDE.md lists the pitfalls. `tests/tools/test_template_size.gd` now counts logical lines (a statement wrapped inside brackets counts once): with game.json the formatted template is 452 physical lines but 296 logical ones (295 before formatting), so BRIEF 3.6's "under 300 lines" keeps measuring the code, not the formatter. A game fresh from `tools/new_game` needs one gdformat run, since its shorter names let gdformat join a few wrapped lines; new_game's "next:" line, CLAUDE.md and docs/GAME_SDK.md say so.

#### 2026-10-22: A final Definition of Done sweep before the v0.1.0 tag

- Decision: after W4's two fixer rounds, the lead ran one read-only sweep of every BRIEF 9 Definition of Done bullet (14 checkers: one per BRIEF area, one for the docs, one per game brief, with three skeptics trying to refute each reported gap). It confirmed 24 gaps. Three were the missing `v0.1.0` tag. The lead fixed seven doc mismatches itself and the gate coverage (the static step parses every CI workflow as YAML through `uv`; the node step runs every `tests/games/<id>/` folder's Node tests; the fit step decodes `build/core_stack_test`). Eight small fixes went to isolated worktrees, each reviewed by a fresh agent and fixed again where the review found a problem:
  - the rescue banner always shows (4.6.3);
  - a mid-ride control loss shows a HUD notice (4.4);
  - no client learns another rider's weight in any scaling mode (3.2);
  - offline uploads never count toward the retry cap (3.8);
  - scenario FITs go to `build/scenarios` for the SDK decode;
  - one shared not_pedaling detector, with edge tests (3.5, 4.6.3, 4.6.4);
  - bots follow a published cadence band (3.6);
  - a Reset to defaults row for bindings and per-session Autopilot scanning (3.5), with over-tall Settings pages scrolling to the highlighted row (3.9);
  - Workout clients interpolate other riders and blend their own toward the host (5.1);
  - an export-time default relay URL (`export_windows --relay-url`), so a joining friend types only the code (DoD 2).

  What stays open is listed under "Known gaps in v0.1.0" in `docs/BACKLOG.md`.
- Alternatives: tag after W4 round 2 and list every sweep finding as a known issue; run a full third W3/W4 cycle.
- Reason: BRIEF 8 caps W4 at two fixer rounds but leaves the lead's closing step: rerun every gate, finish the docs, then tag. Each fix here is small, tested and reviewed, and four of them close BRIEF 3.2, 3.8, 4.4 and 4.6.3 gaps, which shouldn't ship as known issues when they are hours of work. The rest are P1 or design follow-ups, recorded rather than built.

#### 2026-10-22: CI hardening after the first GitHub run

- Decision: the first GitHub run of v0.1.0 passed every gate on Linux except two `tools/tests` cases that found Godot only through the gitignored `tools/godot_bin.local` (fixed: they pass `GODOT`, or bring their own `godot_bin.local`). A read-only sweep of Linux-only differences then found five gaps, now closed:
  - The full GUT run's floor is the larger of `build/green_count.txt` and the tracked `tests/green_count_floor.txt` (2720), so CI and fresh worktrees enforce the count rule instead of "more than 0". `--min-count` still overrides. Raise the floor when a merge to main grows the suite.
  - `run()` stops a timed-out step with SIGTERM first: the wrappers exec into `godot_lock.mjs`, which kills Godot's own process group on SIGTERM. SIGKILL follows after 10 s, and `run()` returns 124 even if an orphan holds its pipes, so a hung sim fails its row instead of hanging the job.
  - The sim step passes `--godot-timeout=840`, so the wrapper's own timer fires first.
  - The full GUT run gets an explicit 3600 s wrapper timeout. Linux CI took 675 s of the 1200 s default at 2720 tests.
  - `sdk-runtime.yml` and `art-pipeline.yml` run their GUT step through `tools/run_tests --only=gut --gdir=...`, so the strict gate applies (Run Summary, JUnit count, forbidden lines from stdout and stderr, pending and risky tests). Before, sdk-runtime's JUnit write failed silently, because `build/` doesn't exist on a fresh runner.
  - `lead.yml` installs uv, so the workflow YAML check runs on GitHub.
- Alternatives: cache `build/green_count.txt` in CI (a cache miss silently brings back "> 0"); `--min-count=2720` in lead.yml (CI then differs from the local command); detach every child (Ctrl-C would no longer reach it).
- Reason: CI should apply the gates exactly as `tools/run_tests` does locally, and fail loudly rather than hang or quietly loosen.

#### 2026-10-31: the v1 run's rulings

- Decision: the Godot lock's cap rises to 16 for the run, by the cap file, and returns to its default before the final literal `tools/run_tests` (BRIEF 2's 4 is a resource limit, not BRIEF 4.6 or 7). Checkpoints run `tools/gate strict` plus the matrices; the final release and CI run the literal suite.
- Decision: rules-step budget tests keep 1000 us locally and allow 2500 us when `CI` is set: GitHub's runners measured 1229-1828 us on unchanged code, so main was red for a machine's speed. The floor and every other test are unchanged.
- Decision: siege 0.3.0's edit of `docs/games/siege.md` stays: reverting it would leave the brief contradicting a shipped HUD. The `never_edit` rule applies from `tools/gate check` on.
- Decision: workflows go from 20 to 7 in the cleanup, with `lead.yml` running everything.
- Alternatives: cap 4 (about 8 h longer); no CI allowance (CI stays red and hides real failures).
- Reason: the owner asked for quick, concurrent, bulletproof work; none of this touches BRIEF 4.6 or a BRIEF 7 assertion.

#### 2026-10-31: Tank matrix rows re-pinned

Decision: `tools/lib/gate_manifest.json` requires Tank's S14-S18 and 85 matrix runs, and the lead re-pinned `safety_pins.json`.
Alternatives: leave the old 37 runs required, which would let the difficulty rows go missing unnoticed.
Reason: a stricter gate; no threshold moved. The re-pin also took Tank v4's additive changes to `check_sims.mjs` (the Ambush laps, the Repairs engine code, the new rows).

### tooling

Short ADRs for `tools/godot.sh`, `tools/godot.ps1`, `tools/lib/godot_lock.mjs`, `tools/lib/wrapper_common.ps1`, `tools/env.sh`, `tools/env.ps1`, `tools/run_tests`, `tools/run_tests.ps1`, `tools/lib/run_tests.mjs`, `tools/lib/gut_gate.mjs`, `tools/check_scripts.gd` and `tools/tests/` (BRIEF 1, 2, 7).

- **2026-10-21: Lock state is files that are created or deleted, never rewritten.** Each request gets `t-<seq>.json` (pid, lease, label; created exclusively), and a grant creates `h-<seq>`. No `h-` file means "waiting". Ticket numbers come from a `seq` counter that is only touched under an exclusive-create `seq.mutex`, and a mutex left by a dead process (or older than 10 s) is broken. *Alternatives:* a state field rewritten inside the ticket; `flock`; a lock daemon. *Reason:* every state change is one exclusive create or one unlink, which is atomic on NTFS and POSIX, so readers never see a half-written state, and there's no Windows rename-over-open-file failure. `flock` doesn't exist in Git Bash, and a daemon would be one more process to babysit.
- **2026-10-21: Strict FIFO grants with whole leases.** A ticket is granted only when no lower-numbered ticket is waiting, and either `held + lease <= cap`, or `lease > cap` and nothing is held. *Alternatives:* best fit, or letting small requests overtake. *Reason:* BRIEF 2 requires it, and it's the only rule under which a waiting e2e lease (3 slots) can't be starved by a stream of 1-slot runs. Tested with a 4-slot lease at cap 4, a 3-slot lease at cap 4 behind 2 held slots, and a 3-slot lease above cap 2.
- **2026-10-21: Dead tickets are found by pid first, then by heartbeat.** A ticket whose pid is gone is reaped at once. Each ticket's mtime is touched every 5 s from a worker thread, and a ticket with a live pid but no heartbeat for 60 s is reaped once the waiter has seen it stale over 10 s of its own uninterrupted polling. *Alternatives:* pid only; heartbeat only. *Reason:* Windows reuses pids, so pid alone could block the queue forever behind a crashed run. The worker thread keeps beating while a library user blocks its main thread (for example in `spawnSync`). The polling-gap rule stops a machine waking from sleep from reaping live holders.
- **2026-10-21: The cap is re-read on every poll.** `machineCap()` returns `WATTLANDS_GODOT_LOCK_CAP` if set, else 4 with at least 8 CPUs and 16 GiB `os.freemem()`, else 2. *Alternative:* fix the cap once per process. *Reason:* free memory drops as Godot processes start, so later grants see the real headroom. Tests and harnesses read the same override.
- **2026-10-21: `WATTLANDS_GODOT_LEASE_TOKEN` (additive).** `acquire()` returns `handle.token`. Passing it to `tools/godot.sh` or `.ps1` runs Godot inside that held lease without queueing again, and an invalid or released token falls back to normal queueing with a warning. *Alternatives:* the e2e harness starts the Godot binary directly, or nests a second acquire. *Reason:* BRIEF 2 has the e2e harness hold one lease for its host and two clients. Starting Godot directly would duplicate the env, user-dir, `-d` and timeout rules, and a nested acquire deadlocks when the lease is above the cap. The harness must start at most `lease` Godot processes under one token.
- **2026-10-21: The lock CLI's exit codes.** It returns Godot's own code, 124 on the run timeout ("godot run timeout"), 75 on the lock wait timeout ("godot lock wait timeout", and nothing was started), 127 when the program can't be started, 2 on usage errors, and 128+n on a signal. The lock is released on every path, including SIGINT, SIGTERM, SIGHUP and SIGBREAK, and a waiting ticket is withdrawn.
- **2026-10-21: Window hiding.** The runner sets `windowsHide` only for `--headless` runs and for Godot's `_console.exe`, which starts the real windowed exe as its own child, so that window stays visible. *Reason:* on Windows, `windowsHide` also hides the child's first GUI window, and windowed runs (screenshots, Siege's windowed perf run) must stay visible. Checked by hand: a windowed run through the wrapper showed a visible window.
- **2026-10-21: Timeouts kill the whole tree.** `taskkill /T /F` on Windows. On POSIX the child gets its own process group and the group is SIGKILLed. A test checks that the real Godot process, the grandchild of the runner on Windows, is gone after `--timeout`.
- **2026-10-21: "This checkout" means the wrapper's own checkout.** Binary resolution reads `tools/godot_bin.local` next to the wrapper, then in the main checkout (`dirname $(git rev-parse --path-format=absolute --git-common-dir)`), then looks for `godot` or `godot4` on PATH. A `godot_bin.local` that names a missing file is skipped with a warning. *Reason:* this works from any cwd and in every worktree (tested with a real `git worktree`).
- **2026-10-21: The user dir.** It's `<--path or git toplevel or cwd>/.userdata`: `APPDATA` on Windows (converted with `cygpath -m` in Git Bash), `XDG_DATA_HOME` on Linux. The wrapper creates the folder. Runs with `--export-release`, `--export-debug`, `--export-pack` or `--export-patch` keep the real user dir.
- **2026-10-21: Git Bash keeps MSYS's default argument conversion.** POSIX paths such as `/c/x` or `/tmp/x` become Windows paths for Godot. URLs, `res://` paths, JSON and arguments with spaces pass unchanged (tested). *Alternative:* `MSYS2_ARG_CONV_EXCL='*'`. *Reason:* Godot is a native program and needs Windows paths.
- **2026-10-21: `godot.ps1` reconstructs arguments typed at a PowerShell prompt.** Started with `-File`, PowerShell passes arguments untouched. Called directly, it drops a bare `--`, splits `-gdir=res://tests` at the colon and turns `a,b` into an array. `tools/lib/wrapper_common.ps1` rebuilds the typed words from the parser's AST of `$MyInvocation.Line` (adjacent tokens are joined, and the result is checked against `$args`). When that isn't possible it falls back to `$args`, rejoins `-x:` pairs and warns about a lost `--`. *Alternative:* document "always quote '--'". *Reason:* agents and humans type the GUT command from CLAUDE.md as-is, and a split `-gdir` would silently run nothing.
- **2026-10-21: PowerShell starts native programs through `System.Diagnostics.Process`.** It applies exact Windows argument quoting, uses the caller's current location as the working directory, and makes temporary environment changes that are restored right after start. *Reasons:* PowerShell 5.1 drops embedded double quotes when it calls a native program. A direct call would otherwise leak `APPDATA` and the lock variables into the user's session. With PATHEXT missing (an emptied environment), `&` opens an exe like a document, with no output and no exit code. Program lookup also scans PATH itself when `Get-Command` finds nothing, and the Windows check uses `[Environment]::OSVersion`, not `$env:OS`.
- **2026-10-21: One Node core for both runners.** `tools/run_tests` and `tools/run_tests.ps1` are thin shims over `tools/lib/run_tests.mjs`, which holds a `STEPS` list with one function per step: import, gut, scaffold, static, sim, fit, node, e2e. Godot steps go through `tools/godot.sh` (bash shim) or `tools/godot.ps1` (PowerShell shim). *Alternative:* separate bash and PowerShell implementations. *Reason:* identical behaviour, one place for the lead's W2 steps (scaffold, sim, fit and e2e are marked SKIP "not wired yet" until then), and gate logic that can be unit-tested (`tools/lib/gut_gate.mjs`).
- **2026-10-21: The GUT gate.** A run passes on exit 0, "Run Summary" in the log, a JUnit count above 0 and at least the required count, and none of BRIEF 7's five strings in the log. The required count is `--min-count`, else `build/green_count.txt`. `build/junit.xml` is deleted before each run, so a stale file can't pass. The green count is written after every green full run, including one that `--min-count` lowered, and the runner prints when that happens. Narrow runs (`--gdir`, `--gut-arg`) neither compare nor update it.
- **2026-10-21: `import` is never a gate.** A non-zero exit shows as WARN in the summary. The summary rows are PASS, FAIL, SKIP and WARN, and only FAIL makes the exit code non-zero.
- **2026-10-21: gdlint and gdformat --check are reported, not fatal, unless `--strict-lint` is passed.** Detection tries `gdlint` on PATH, then `uvx --from gdtoolkit==4.*` (gdtoolkit 4.5.0 works here), with a 90 s probe. Without it the runner prints "gdtoolkit unavailable, skipped". `--no-lint` skips both. *Reason:* BRIEF 7.2 makes them conditional on Python, gdtoolkit can lag GDScript 4.7 syntax, and style findings shouldn't block P0. The findings are in the summary and in `build/gdlint.log` and `build/gdformat.log`. *Superseded 2026-10-21* by docs/decisions/lead.md: lint is fatal whenever gdtoolkit 4.5.0 is available, `--strict-lint` is a no-op, and `./gdlintrc` configures gdlint.
- **2026-10-21: `check_scripts.gd` also fails on logged load errors.** In Godot 4.7, a script with a parse error loads as non-null with `can_instantiate()` false. An `@abstract` script with a parse error would pass both of the brief's checks. So the checker registers an `OS.add_logger()` Logger (Godot 4.5+) and fails any script whose load logs an error (warnings excluded). It skips dot-folders and folders with a `.gdignore`, since Godot never imports them.
- **2026-10-21: The node step.** It runs `node --test` with each suite directory as cwd (node's default file patterns) and skips a directory without test files. `WATTLANDS_RUN_TESTS_NESTED` stops a Node suite that starts `tools/run_tests` from recursing. Each suite has a 30 min timeout with a tree kill.
- **2026-10-21: `tools/run_tests` removes `FAL_KEY` and `WATTLANDS_ALLOW_ART` for its own process tree** (docs/OWNERSHIP.md TL5), so no gate can make a paid fal call even in a session where a person has set them. `tools/env.*` and the Godot wrappers still never name either variable, and a test scans them for that.
- **2026-10-21: Tooling tests never touch the main checkout's Godot cache.** Real-Godot tests run in scratch projects under `build/` (which has a `.gdignore`) and queue on the real machine-wide lock. Lock-logic tests use temp lock directories. The "empty environment" for the wrapper tests is PATH (bash, node, git, System32, WindowsPowerShell), SystemRoot and TEMP/TMP. TEMP is kept so `os.tmpdir()`, and with it the machine-wide lock directory, stays the same.

### vendor

- 2026-10-21. Decision: vendor GUT 9.7.1 from the `v9.7.1` tag's source archive (commit `aeb5d4f3f7f0a6c9b5e178876d6c99b791fda605`), all of `addons/gut/` unchanged. Alternatives: the Asset Library, or a newer GUT from `main`. Reason: BRIEF 1 asks for pinned GitHub sources and BRIEF 7 names 9.7.1, the release for Godot 4.7.x. The vendored tree hash equals upstream's (`5d68938`), so the copy is verifiably unmodified.
- 2026-10-21. Decision: pin GDBLE to the tagged release v0.6.0 (`gdble-v0.6.0.zip`, tag commit `ecae959`, built from `690a833`). Alternatives: main at `13c49c7`, or v0.5.5 with the double first import. Reason: BRIEF 4.2 prefers a tagged release at or after `13c49c7` over main, and v0.5.5 aborts Godot 4.7.2's first headless import. v0.6.0 contains `13c49c7`, ships `BUILD_INFO.json` with `release_ready: true`, and its asset digest and `SHA256SUMS` verify. The first headless import on a fresh `.godot` cache exits 0 (see the import-check entry below).
- 2026-10-21. Decision: keep only the Windows x86_64 and Linux x86_64 GDBLE libraries; drop the macOS dylibs (8.6 MB), the Android AARs (3.0 MB), `gdble.gdap` and `permission_startup.gd`, and remove their `.gdextension` entries and `android_aar_plugin`. Alternatives: keep the whole release (21 MB). Reason: Windows is the target and CI runs on Linux; the rest would add 11.6 MB of binaries no target uses, and `.gdextension` entries for missing files would only fail later. Hashes of everything removed are in `docs/deps.d/gdble.md`.
- 2026-10-21. Decision: set `reloadable = false` in `addons/gdble/gdble.gdextension`. Alternatives: upstream's `true`. Reason: BRIEF 4.2; hot reload of a BLE extension would drop the trainer session.
- 2026-10-21. Decision: drop upstream's `addons/gdble/SHA256SUMS` and reproduce it verbatim in `docs/deps.d/gdble.md`. Alternatives: keep it in the addon. Reason: after the platform trim and the `.gdextension` edit it no longer verifies, and a failing checksum file in the tree would mislead; the record keeps the provenance.
- 2026-10-21. Decision: keep upstream's `README_EN.md` and `docs/API_CONTRACT.md` under `addons/gdble/docs/`. Alternatives: rely on GitHub. Reason: the ble module codes against them offline (event timing, `connect_device()` being a factory, no auto-reconnect); Godot does not import `.md` files.
- 2026-10-21. Decision: keep GDBLE v0.6.0 after the import check. On Godot 4.7.2 (.NET build) the first headless import of the main checkout on a fresh `.godot` cache exits 0 in about 4 s, with no error lines, and both classes load (`BluetoothManager`, `BleDevice`). Alternatives: main's prebuilt addon at `13c49c7` or later, or v0.5.5 with BRIEF 4.2's double import. Reason: neither fallback is needed. Two more imports (incremental, and fresh again) also exit 0 and change nothing. Logs: `build/vendor/import1.log`, `import2.log`, `import3_fresh.log`.
- 2026-10-21. Decision: commit `addons/gdble/gdble.gdextension.uid` (`uid://daj40y0ysg0l8`, written by the first import) as the addon's only added file; the `addons/gdble` tree to commit is `b9f97530f34f35c7290e35d06ed996b40ba1d78f`. Alternatives: delete it, or gitignore it. Reason: BRIEF 2 commits `*.uid`; without it every fresh clone writes a different random UID. Godot keeps it stable on later imports.
- 2026-10-21. Decision: record that `addons/gut/` stays byte-identical to upstream through imports and test runs (tree `5d68938`, no new `.uid` or `.import`), so it needs no local modification entry. Alternatives: none. Reason: checked by hashing the working copy after three imports and four GUT runs.
- 2026-10-21. Decision: the GDBLE surface to code against is the verified ClassDB list in `docs/deps.d/gdble.md`. Every member BRIEF 4.2 lists exists with the same argument count; action methods return an `int` operation id; the rest is additive. `BleDevice.disconnect()` and `BleDevice.is_connected()` clash with `Object.disconnect(signal, callable)` and `Object.is_connected(signal, callable)`, so on a `RefCounted`-typed device they must be called as `call(&"disconnect")` and `call(&"is_connected")`. Alternatives: hold devices untyped. Reason: a probe showed the direct calls are parse errors on `RefCounted` or `Object` variables ("Too few arguments ... Expected at least 2"), the `call()` forms compile and reach GDBLE's methods, and CLAUDE.md keeps static typing (`Object`/`Node`/`RefCounted`) for GDBLE objects.
- 2026-10-21. Decision: `tools/run_tests` must not trust GUT's exit code for an empty run. Alternatives: rely on the exit code. Reason: with no tests GUT 9.7.1 prints `[GUT ERROR]:  Nothing was run.`, prints no "Run Summary" and exits 0, writing a JUnit file with `tests="0"`. The gate's string and JUnit checks (BRIEF 7) catch it; the real logs were run through `tools/lib/gut_gate.mjs` to confirm.

### platform

Short ADRs for `WlSettings`, `WlApp`, `WlTrainerService`, `LocalRider`, `RidePause`, `HrSensorManager`, `FtpTest` and `AutoloadLookup` (BRIEF 3.2, 3.5, 4.1, 4.6.9, 6).

- **2026-10-21: A missing settings file is a normal first run; a corrupt one warns.** Decision: a missing `profile.json` or `settings.json` gives the defaults silently; unparsable JSON or a non-object gives the defaults with `push_warning`; single values of the wrong type are dropped and numbers out of range clamped (`WlSettings.PROFILE_RANGES`), each with `push_warning`. `set_value` refuses a wrong type for a documented key with `push_error`; other modules may store their own JSON keys. Files are written to `<name>.tmp` and renamed over the old file. Alternatives: warn on missing files too; refuse to start on a corrupt file. Reason: every first launch and every test temp dir starts without files, and a hand-edited or half-written file must never block riding (an FTP of 0 or a weight of 5000 would reach the governor).
- **2026-10-21: `--strava-base=<url>` is a server root.** Decision: the API base becomes `<url>/api/v3` and the OAuth base `<url>/oauth` (a trailing `/api/v3` or `/oauth` is stripped first), for this run only, never saved; `--relay` likewise. Alternatives: use the URL as-is for both. Reason: the real Strava layout is `https://www.strava.com` + `/api/v3` and `/oauth`, and `tests/core/test_locks.gd` already uses `http://localhost:8899/api/v3/uploads`, so `server/mock_strava` can mirror the real paths. Rides reads `Settings.strava_api_base()` and `strava_oauth_base()`.
- **2026-10-21: Recent rooms and the last trainer live in `settings.json`.** Decision: `recent_rooms` (`{code, at, role}`, normalized code, `HOST`/`CLIENT`, newest first, one per code, at most 8, UTC `at`) and `last_device` are settings keys as the W0 stub documents; `TrainerService` copies `FtmsConnectionManager.last_device()` into `last_device` on every `trainer_ready`. `Paths.recent_rooms_path()` stays for compatibility; ble keeps its own `last_device.json`. Alternatives: separate files. Reason: one file, one loader, one corruption rule; the app shell reads both through `Settings`.
- **2026-10-21: One time base per rider.** Decision: `LocalRider` keeps its own platform time (integer microseconds, from 0 at each `setup()`), queues device samples when they arrive and processes them in the next `tick()` as copies stamped with that time. The governor, gestures, recorder and `Telemetry.t` all use it. Alternatives: pass the device's `sample.t` through. Reason: a MockTrainer or FtmsTrainer created before the rider (menus, reconnects) has another clock origin, and the governor's rescue, coasting and 3 s power rules must compare sample times with its own tick time.
- **2026-10-21: Raw trainer values in telemetry; the speed model drives the course.** Decision: `Telemetry.power_w`, `cadence_rpm` and `speed_kmh` are the trainer's, `distance_m` integrates the trainer's reported speed (the `Telemetry` contract and the recorder use that), and the speed model (P_eff on the applied SIM grade, 0 outside SIM) feeds only `SafetyGovernor.set_course_distance()`. A sample older than 3 s counts as gone (power, cadence and speed 0). W' balance steps every tick on the latest raw power; the recorder gets the latest values every tick and keeps 1 Hz itself. Alternatives: speed model speed in telemetry; W' and the recorder per sample. Reason: BRIEF 3.4 asks for W' every sim tick, per-tick feeding can't skip a second when notifications jitter to 1.1 s, and games integrate their own speed model (BRIEF 3.11).
- **2026-10-21: Exactly one ack per governor record.** Decision: every `directive_applied` record the governor emits is re-emitted by the rider and queued for `take_acks()` as `{id, kind, applied, clamped_by, refused, reason}`; `apply_directive()` queues the returned record itself only if the governor didn't emit during the call. Alternatives: ack only what `apply_directive` returns. Reason: course grades and other governor records also need acks, and the stub doc lets the governor both return and emit.
- **2026-10-21: A process-wide live-rider list for the exit paths.** Decision: `LocalRider.live_riders()` (weak references, filled by `setup()`, emptied by `shutdown()` and freeing) is what `WlTrainerService.shutdown_all()` walks on quit, window close, crash and tree exit. Alternatives: a register/unregister API on TrainerService that GameHost and GameSimRunner must call. Reason: BRIEF 4.6.9 must cover session and bot riders too, and a frozen GameHost stub can't be asked to call a new API in W1.
- **2026-10-21: The App boots only in the real app.** Decision: `WlApp._ready` defers `boot()` only when the SceneTree has no script; under GUT, `check_scripts.gd` or a tool's `-s` driver the App autoload stays in `boot` and never starts the trainer. A scripted peer (`--host`/`--join`) goes to `lobby` with `launch_options()`; the app shell drives it from there. Alternatives: boot always and stub Bluetooth in tests; an explicit `--no-boot` flag. Reason: no Bluetooth activity in tests (BRIEF 1) without asking every test runner to pass a flag.
- **2026-10-21: quit() order.** Decision: `goto("quitting")`, emit `quitting(code)` (the app shell saves rides and writes `--report` with the stack still up), save any ride still recording, `TrainerService.shutdown_all()` (SIM flat, then Stop), write a minimal scripted-peer report if `--report` was given and nobody wrote one, then quit, after 0.5 s of real time when a real trainer is connected so the safe commands can leave. A window close request runs the same path (auto-accept-quit is off in the real app); `NOTIFICATION_CRASH` and leaving the tree after boot shut the riders down at once. `--run-seconds` counts the App's own SimClock at `--sim-speed` and quits with `run_seconds_exit_code` (default 0). Alternatives: write the full report in the platform. Reason: the report's contents are session data the app shell owns; the harness still always finds a JSON file.
- **2026-10-21: The menu-time rider.** Decision: `TrainerService` builds its `LocalRider` (peer 1, profile slot in the saved scaling mode, solo, not recorded) as soon as a trainer exists, attaches it to the InputRouter, and ticks it on its own SimClock (`--sim-speed`) until `set_external_tick(true)`. Profile and scaling changes outside a session set the rider up again (a mock whose FTP, CP, W', cadence or weight changed is rebuilt); `hold_rider(true)` defers that, and releasing sets the rider up afresh. `EventBus.panic_requested` calls the rider's `panic()`. Switching trainers shuts the old rider down first. Alternatives: create the rider only for sessions. Reason: pedal gestures drive every menu (BRIEF 3.5), and the FTP test's cool-down must not be rebuilt by its own profile save.
- **2026-10-21: FTP test details.** Decision: `FtpTest` is pure logic (`start`, `step(dt, telemetry)`, `back`) plus `run_on(rider)`. The steps go up to min(1000 W, trainer max), at least one, at most 120. The power rule needs an ERG target above 0; the cadence rule ignores a cadence older than 3 s, so a trainer without cadence can finish. Rescue and coasting end at once and the cool-down phase starts in the same tick. The 10 s save countdown runs at the start of the cool-down, which continues; `back` cancels it. A test ended in the ramp-in or with `back` gives no result. Saving sets FTP, CP = FTP and `ftp_verified`. Alternatives: count down after the cool-down; save results from the ramp-in. Reason: the rider sees the result while still pedaling easily, and an estimate from a 50-65% ramp is meaningless.
- **2026-10-21: `AutoloadLookup` instead of autoload globals.** Decision: platform classes find `EventBus`, `Settings`, `TrainerService`, `Rides`, `InputRouter` and `App` through `/root` at run time and every class takes an injected instance first. Alternatives: name the autoloads as globals. Reason: the same classes run in GUT, in tools and in in-process multi-peer simulations, where each peer has its own bus (OWNERSHIP 3.1 C28).
- **2026-10-21: Handing ticking back makes the session rider the menu rider again.** Decision: `TrainerService.set_external_tick(false)` after `true` sets its rider up afresh (peer 1, the profile's slot, solo, no plan, no recorder, a fresh governor) and applies any profile or scaling change made during the session; while that rider's ride still records, the reset waits until `finish_ride()` (checked every menu step, `is_menu_reset_pending()`). Profile changes during a session wait for the hand-back instead of being dropped. `App.quit()` shuts the riders down before handing ticking back, so quitting never builds a menu rider and the shut-down governor keeps being ticked for its safe commands. Alternatives: leave the session's governor and plan in place in menus; reset at once and drop an unsaved ride. Reason: menus must ride free in SIM flat with solo gestures (coast never fires in ERG), and the results screen must still be able to save the ride.
- **2026-10-21: A ride is never lost to a rebuild.** Decision: `LocalRider.setup()` on a rider whose ride still records stops and saves that ride first (`push_warning`), and `TrainerService` does the same before switching trainers. Alternatives: drop it silently; refuse the setup. Reason: a saved ride is the one artefact a rider keeps (BRIEF 3.8), and the exit path already saves open rides.
- **2026-10-21: A new trainer object mid-session is rebound, not rebuilt.** Decision: `LocalRider.rebind_device(d)` keeps plan, phase, time, queues and recorder and calls `governor.setup(d)` again; `TrainerService` uses it when `trainer_ready` hands over a different device object while a GameHost ticks the rider (menus still rebuild the rider). Alternatives: free the rider and build a new one. Reason: the GameHost holds the rider; freeing it mid-session would crash the session. trainer-control's `setup()` must accept a second call that rebinds its controller.
- **2026-10-21: Setup clears a reused MockTrainer.** Decision: `LocalRider.setup()` clears the mock's cadence and power bands and sets its phase to -1. Alternatives: rely on the cool-down to clear bands. Reason: the app's `--mock-trainer` mock lives across sessions, and a session left before its cool-down would otherwise steer the next session's mock rider.
- **2026-10-21: FIT lap names are text.** Decision: lap names from phase labels and `lap_marker` labels go through `TranslationServer.translate` (strings keys become text, plain text passes through); unlabelled lap phases are `CORE_LAP_PHASE` ("Phase {n}") and the cool-down without a label `CORE_LAP_COOLDOWN`. Alternatives: the raw label. Reason: PL11 allows a strings key as a label (Tank), and Strava shows the lap name.
- **2026-10-21: settings.json holds JSON values only.** Decision: `set_value` refuses anything that doesn't survive JSON as itself (vectors, colors, non-string keys, NaN) with `push_error`; `set_last_device` first round-trips its dictionary through JSON (packed arrays become arrays). Alternatives: store whatever `JSON.stringify` makes of it. Reason: a value that comes back as a different type after a restart is a latent bug in another module.
- **2026-10-21: A dropped FtpTest lets go.** Decision: an `FtpTest` freed while driving a rider (the screen dropped it without `stop()`) puts the governor back on its normal profile and releases the TrainerService hold from `NOTIFICATION_PREDELETE`. Alternatives: rely on `stop()`. Reason: a forgotten hold would freeze every later profile change for the menu rider.
- **2026-10-21 (W2): Same-object reconnects resync the governor.** Decision: `FtmsConnectionManager` keeps one `FtmsTrainer` per address, so a reconnect hands `TrainerService` the same object (`trainer_ready`, then `reconnected`). `TrainerService` now listens to `reconnected` and calls the additive `LocalRider.trainer_reconnected()`, which calls `SafetyGovernor.on_trainer_reconnected()` (restore the last safe mode through the ramped path, BRIEF 4.4). A new object still goes through `rebind_device` (in a session) or a rebuilt rider (menus), and its `reconnected` then resyncs too. `rebind_device` with the same object stays a no-op. *Alternative:* make `rebind_device(same)` resync. *Reason:* the reconnect is a distinct event from a rebind, and the tested W1 contract of `rebind_device` stays.
- **2026-10-21 (W2): LocalRider fires timed gestures every tick and records absent values as absent.** Decision: `tick()` calls `GestureRecognizer.advance(t)` after the samples, so coast and stop_5s fire on the tick they are due rather than with the next ~1 Hz sample; the recorder gets -1 (absent) for a cadence or speed the trainer doesn't report or a stale sample's (power 0 W), and RideRecorder keeps its 1 Hz thinning of the per-tick calls. *Reason:* sim's integration note; no invented 0 rpm cadence in the FIT.
- **2026-10-21 (W2): Acks carry `refused` as a bool and `dropped`.** Decision: `take_acks()` entries are `{id, kind, applied, clamped_by, refused (bool), reason, dropped: [{channel, reason}]}`. *Reason:* trainer-control's records carry a bool and the dropped haptic channel; the lead fixes the Telemetry.acks comment.
- **2026-10-21 (W2): `Paths.get_override()` and `RiderProfile.show_wkg`.** Decision: additive. `get_override()` returns the normalised override ("" when none) so run_game_sim can restore it; `show_wkg` (default false, in to_dict/from_dict) is the rider's opt-in to see W/kg on the HUD and results, independent of `share_weight` (W/kg is computed locally). *Reason:* sdk-tools and ui-hud requests.
- **2026-10-21 (W2): setup() restarts a MockTrainer's profile.** Decision: besides clearing the bands and phase, `LocalRider.setup()` calls `MockTrainer.restart_profile()`, so `cadence_drop:at_s`, surges, ramps and intervals count from the session's setup (the rider's platform time 0) even when the `--mock-trainer` mock rode through the menus first. *Alternative:* leave it to each caller (trainer-core's W1 note); nobody called it. *Reason:* a profile scripted for a session must not depend on how long the menus took.
- **2026-10-21 (W2): `TrainerService.set_menu_ticking(on)`.** Decision: an additive alias of `set_external_tick(not on)`, which the app shell already calls duck-typed at the start (false) and end (true) of a session and of the FTP test. *Reason:* without it the service kept ticking the rider from its own clock while the GameHost ticked it too (two ticks per frame in the windowed app).
- **2026-10-21 (W2): A scripted peer's hard stop and report belong to the app shell.** Decision: following docs/ARCHITECTURE.md "Scripted peers" (lead, after W1), `WlApp` ignores `--run-seconds` and writes no fallback `--report` when `is_scripted()`; the shell stops with exit 2 and writes the full report after `App.quit()`. Non-scripted runs keep the `--run-seconds` quit and the fallback report. This supersedes the W1 "quit() order" ADR for scripted peers. *Reason:* WlApp's own clock started at boot, so it reached `--run-seconds` before the shell's and quit with 0 and a minimal report, hiding the shell's exit 2 and report from the e2e harness.
- **2026-10-21 (W2r2): LocalRider.tick() ends when a handler sets the rider up again.** Decision: `setup()` bumps a generation counter; `tick()` reads it at the start and returns as soon as it changed (after `device.tick`, each sample, the timed gestures and `governor.tick`), and `_queue_gestures` stops emitting the rest of a batch. *Alternatives:* defer `setup()` calls made during a tick to its end (callers expect the new setup at once, the session start reads the rider right after). *Reason:* a menu pick made from a gesture handler (a pedal-scan pick that starts a session) set the rider up mid-tick, and the rest of the tick fed the old platform time into the new governor, gestures and ride (a FIT record at the menu's time).
- **2026-10-21 (W2r2): The menu reset re-attaches the rider, drops the plan at once and keeps the connection.** Decision: `_rebuild_menu_rider()` attaches the rider to the InputRouter again (GameHost.end_session detaches it); when the reset must wait for a ride still recording, `LocalRider.clear_plan()` (additive) drops the session plan at once so the governor rides SIM flat meanwhile; `LocalRider.setup()` on the same device object calls `SafetyGovernor.adopt_connection()` on the governor it replaces, so the warm-up continues from the menus into a session and back, and the step from the cool-down's ERG to the menus' SIM flat is ramped from the trainer's real state. *Alternatives:* leave the re-attach to the app shell's AppSessionInput (only the session screen does it); keep the plan until the ride is saved (the trainer would hold the interval's ERG target in the menus). *Reason:* BRIEF 4.6.8 counts the warm-up from the first pedal stroke after the trainer connects, and the menus ride free at SIM flat.
- **2026-10-21 (W2r2): `--mock-keyboard` turns on MockTrainer's keyboard control.** Decision: `WlTrainerService.use_mock()` passes `keyboard: CmdLine.get_bool("mock-keyboard", false)` to every mock it builds (the `--mock-trainer` one, a rebuilt one after a profile change, one picked in Settings). Without the flag the mock never reads a key. *Alternative:* turn it on with every `--mock-trainer` (W/S/A/D would move the rider whenever a keyboard user navigates the menus). *Reason:* BRIEF 4.2's keys are also BRIEF 3.5's movement keys, so the overlap must be asked for; the lead adds the flag to CLAUDE.md's list.
- **2026-10-21 (W2 round 2, app): An FTP result needs one completed step; `FtpTest.save_now()`.** Decision: `FtpTest` counts a result only when at least one one-minute step was completed (`steps_completed >= 1`) and the test wasn't ended with `back()`; before, reaching the steps was enough, so a stop a few seconds into step 1 saved 0.75 x a ramp-in minute. The additive `save_now()` saves during the countdown at once (the page's Save item). The cadence rule stays as it was: it counts only a fresh cadence (`Telemetry.cadence_age_s` <= 3 s), which includes the CadenceEstimator's speed-based estimate, so a trainer without cadence (and nothing to estimate from) never ends the test on it. `FtpTest` is now the one implementation; the app's `AppFtpTest` is gone. *Alternatives:* keep the app's copy; count the steps as reached. *Reason:* BRIEF 3.2 (one-minute steps, best 1-minute power) and the lead's round-2 item: a test stopped in the ramp-in or before one step was done has no FTP to save.
- **2026-10-21 (W4, SAF-1): The local human rider's safety values come from the rider's own profile.** Decision: `LocalRider.setup()` given both a profile and a slot for a human (not a bot) overwrites the slot's `ftp`, `ftp_verified`, `haptics_on` and `haptic_intensity` from the profile before configuring the governor, with push_warning when they differed. A CLIENT's slot comes from the host's session_start, so a modified or buggy host can no longer set the client's watt basis, lift the 0.85 unverified factor or turn opted-out haptics back on. A null profile keeps the slot as given (tests, in-process riders); bots keep the lobby's bot FTP. The app's session launcher is fixed separately (app fixer). *Alternatives:* copy the slot instead of changing it (the session's `ctx.players` would then show the host's numbers for the local rider while the governor used others); fix only the launcher (every other caller would stay exposed). *Reason:* BRIEF 3.3 "ERG targets always come from each rider's own FTP", 4.6.7 "each client validates and clamps them locally".
- **2026-10-21 (W4, SAF-3): `--sim-speed` only with a MockTrainer.** Decision: `WlTrainerService.effective_sim_speed()` (additive) is the flag with a MockTrainer (or before any device when `--mock-trainer` is given) and 1 with a real trainer (push_warning once). The menu clock follows it whenever the device changes; the app's session launcher uses the same helper for the GameHost's clock (app fixer). *Alternatives:* forbid the flag in the app (scripted mock runs use it); scale nothing on platform time (the mock rider and the sims need it). *Reason:* every governor time rule (30 W/s slew, 2 writes per second, rescue, coasting, warm-up) must hold in wall time on a real trainer.
- **2026-10-21 (W4, SAF-8): Disconnect, switch and "use mock" make the trainer safe before the link drops.** Decision: `WlTrainerService.disconnect_trainer()` and `connect_trainer(address)` (additive; Settings calls them, app fixer) and `use_mock()` shut the rider down first (its governor sends SIM flat, then Stop, through the controller) and release it, then run the link action (`FtmsConnectionManager.disconnect_trainer()`, `connect_to()`, or dropping and freeing the old manager) once the FTMS control point has sent both (queue empty and nothing in flight, or the client not in control), or after `SAFE_UNLINK_TIMEOUT_S` = 3 s (push_warning). Without a pending write (a mock, a fake manager, the hardware lock) it happens at once, so `use_mock()` still switches synchronously. After a disconnect `device()` is null until a trainer is ready again, which sets a new rider up. The connection manager also releases the old `FtmsTrainer` from the shared client on a switch (docs/decisions/ble.md). *Alternatives:* a fixed 0.5 s wait like `App.quit` (too short for a retried write, too long when nothing is pending); shut down inside the manager ("the manager never writes to the trainer itself"). *Reason:* BRIEF 4.6.9 "on exit, crash paths and disconnect: try SIM flat, then Stop"; W3 found that none of the three paths sent Stop, and a switch sent the old rider's SIM flat and Stop to the new trainer.
- **2026-10-21 (W4, NET-6): A host quitting the app leaves its room.** Decision: `WlApp.quit()` calls `NetSession.leave()` (after the quitting signal, so rides and reports go first) when this peer hosts a room (role HOST, not the offline lobby, state not offline or error), and then waits `QUIT_GRACE_S` of real time before the tree quits so the relay transport sends it. A client never leaves on quit: its saved reconnect token lets a restarted client rejoin. `WlApp.net_session` (injection) and `AutoloadLookup.net()` are additive. The client-side notice is the app fixer's. *Reason:* BRIEF 5.3 sends host_left "only on an explicit leave", and 5.5 says "If the host leaves, end the session gracefully"; a window close looked like a drop, so clients sat 120 s in the dropped-host hold.
- **2026-10-21 (W4, RS-1): The network lock refuses ambiguous URL authorities.** Decision: `Locks.network_allows()` (and the trainer bridge's loopback rule, through the new `Locks.is_loopback_url()`) refuse a URL whose authority (after "scheme://", up to the first "/") holds "@", "?", "#", "\", "%", whitespace or a control character, whatever `url_host()` reads. `url_host()` keeps its browser-style reading. *Alternatives:* make `url_host()` follow Godot's `String::parse_url` (two parsers can't be kept in step for every input, and the existing browser-style cases would flip). *Reason:* `HTTPRequest` cuts the authority only at "#" and "/" and then drops everything up to the first "@", so "http://127.0.0.1?@HOST/" reached HOST while locked (BRIEF 1, 7.5). The app's `scripted_config.gd` should use `Locks.is_loopback_url()` too (requests for lead).
- **2026-10-21 (W4, QT-5): `WlTrainerService._pending_change` removed.** It was only ever written: the release, the hand-back and the pending menu reset always set the rider up afresh from the current profile.
- **2026-10-21 (W4r2, NET-2): `LocalRider.resume_phase(index, elapsed_s)` for a rejoin into a running phase.** Decision: additive; GameHost calls it when a client enters a phase that already runs on the host. It forwards to `SafetyGovernor.resume_phase`, follows the MockTrainer's phase and clears the bands in the cool-down like `start_phase`. FIT laps: none when the rider already rides that phase or rode no phase of the plan yet (a restarted client's new FIT starts at the rejoin, BRIEF 3.6, so its first lap is that phase); a real phase change, the phase having moved on while the socket was down, laps as `start_phase` does so every interval keeps its lap (BRIEF 7.5). For a "course" phase the governor has no distance record of (a new rider), `estimate_course_distance` rides the speed model over the phase's course for `elapsed_s` at 75% FTP as P_eff, from that power's steady speed, with the rider's mass, difficulty and grade range, in 1 s steps. A rider without a governor (not set up, a test double such as the sdk's FakeRider) enters the phase through `start_phase()`. *Alternatives:* no lap ever (a socket rejoin after a phase change would lose that interval's lap); start the course at 0 (the trainer's road and the game's would be minutes apart); the game's own course position (game state the platform can't read). *Reason:* the estimate keeps a restarted rider near the right part of the course; it only steers SIM grade, which every 4.6.6 limit still bounds.
- **2026-10-21 (W4r2, SAF-1 range): every profile write is clamped, as loading is.** Decision: `WlSettings.save_profile()` first clamps the profile into `PROFILE_RANGES` (new static `clamp_profile(p)`, additive: a value out of range takes the nearest bound, NaN or infinity the default, each with push_warning), and `FtpTest` limits a valid result to the profile's 30-2000 W before showing and saving it. *Alternatives:* clamp only at load (W4r2: a Strava-imported or tested FTP below 30 W reached the rider stack, whose governor then kept its 200 W default); a setter on `RiderProfile.ftp_w` (a frozen contract field, and tests build out-of-range profiles on purpose). *Reason:* the profile is the source of the rider's watt basis (SAF-1), so it must hold only values the governor accepts.
- **2026-10-21 (W4r2, RS residual): a loopback URL needs a known scheme.** Decision: `Locks.is_loopback_url()` (the network lock and the trainer bridge's loopback rule) also requires the URL, exactly as given, to start with `http://`, `https://`, `ws://` or `wss://` (any case; new `has_known_scheme()`, additive), and `authority_ambiguous()` also counts "[" and "]". *Alternatives:* strip a leading scheme-like prefix as before (url_host() read "127.0.0.2:8765/x://127.0.0.1" as 127.0.0.1 while WebSocketPeer, which treats a missing scheme as ws://, connected to 127.0.0.2); parse the scheme like Godot's String::parse_url (another parser to keep in step). *Reason:* the lock's callers only ever build http(s) and ws(s) URLs, so anything else is refused rather than interpreted; brackets only ever hold an IPv6 host, which is never loopback here.
- **2026-10-21 (W4fin, DoD 2): A build can ship its default relay URL.** Decision: `WlSettings.default_relay_url()` (additive) is the project setting `wattlands/net/default_relay_url` when it holds a ws:// or wss:// URL with a host, else the built-in `ws://localhost:8787/v1` (an unusable value gets push_warning at load). `project.godot` leaves it unset; `node tools/export_windows.mjs --relay-url=<url>` writes `override.cfg` next to the exe (`[wattlands]` / `net/default_relay_url="<url>"`), which Godot reads from the folder of the exe and .pck at startup, so a friend's copy reaches that relay and the friend types only the room code. Precedence: `--relay=<url>`, then a relay URL the rider chose, then the default. settings.json keeps `relay_url` (with `relay_url_custom: true`) only while the rider's choice differs from the default; a blank value or the default's own value follows the default again, and a saved `ws://localhost:8787/v1` without the marker (earlier builds wrote the default on every save) follows it too; a marker that is not a bool (a hand-edited `"yes"` or `1`) is dropped with push_warning, like any wrong-typed value. `load_all` prints "Settings: build default relay URL <url>" once, only for a default other than the built-in one, and `--smoke` checks that line; so `--relay-url=ws://localhost:8787/v1` (the built-in value) writes no `override.cfg`, prints a note that it already is the default, and `--smoke` then expects no line. *Alternatives:* a reachable hosted relay URL in `DEFAULTS` (every dev run and test would point at a real internet service, and no hosted relay URL exists to ship); LAN fallback from join-by-code to LanTransport (larger, and the app never hosts a LAN room yet); keep writing the default into settings.json (a copy that ever saved settings would keep localhost after an upgrade to a build with a relay URL). *Reason:* DoD 2, a joining friend types only the code; the shipped default stays localhost.
- **2026-10-24: One heart rate sensor per local rider, subscribe only, on the trainer's backend.** Decision: `HrSensorManager` (trainer/ble) scans for 0x180D or a heart-rate-like name, subscribes to 0x2A37 and never writes; it remembers the sensor in `Paths.last_hr_sensor_path()` and reconnects with the trainer's backoff. `WlTrainerService` starts it with the FTMS path and hands its transport the trainer's backend: a `GdbleTransport` that shares the trainer transport's `BluetoothManager` (`share_manager_of`), or a second `BridgeTransport`. *Alternatives:* a second `BluetoothManager` (two btleplug sessions on one adapter, and a second `initialize()` would restart nothing useful); multi-device support inside one transport (every BleTransport signal would need a device address, a frozen contract). *Reason:* GDBLE manages several `BleDevice` handles on one manager, and a transport per device keeps the contract and `FtmsConnectionManager` unchanged.
- **2026-10-24: Shared GDBLE scans.** Decision: transports on one manager share its scan: `start_scan()` restarts a running scan to cover the longer window (GDBLE refuses a second start), and `stop_scan()` leaves it running while another transport's window lasts. A sharing transport's `initialize()` reports `adapter_ready` from the primary and never calls `initialize()` again, and it reports the adapter lost when its primary leaves the tree. *Reason:* GDBLE's `stop_scan()` cancels every scan on the manager, and a second `adapter_initialized` would re-run the trainer manager's start-up.
- **2026-10-24: The trainer bridge serves two clients.** Decision: `serve()` gives each socket its own `BridgeServer` and backend (the first from the caller, more from `backend_factory`; the CLI passes `BleakBackend`), at most `MAX_CLIENTS` = 2; without a factory a second client is still refused as busy. *Reason:* the bridge's protocol is one device per socket, so a heart rate sensor gets its own socket and bleak client; the write lock applies to every session.
- **2026-10-24: Pause on not pedaling is a rider timer layered on the governor.** Decision: `RidePause` (core) ride-pauses a human rider after 10 s (`ENTER_S`, the governor's 4.6.4 solo pause) of the shared `not_pedaling` detector, in every mode, and resumes after 1 s at >= 30 rpm; `LocalRider` stops the recorder's timer while ride-paused or game-paused, emits `ride_paused_changed`, and in SOLO `pause_requested("stop_5s")`; the GameHost resumes a non-user pause when the ride pause ends (also with `platform_pause` off). In multiplayer `Telemetry.paused` is the rider's own pause only; the host pauses the shared clock on the edge where every human rider is paused (bots ignored, a dropped rider counts as paused), resumes on the edge back, and tells clients through a `paused` key on the roster, so a client stops its governor's game time and phase clock too. *Alternatives:* lower the governor's 10 s pause to 5 s (a governor rule; BRIEF 4.6 stays as written); keep the `stop_5s` gesture as the trigger (outside ERG only, and `pedal_start` needs 3 s); level-triggered shared pause (a resume from the menu would be undone the next tick). *Reason:* the user's decision (5 s, every mode); the governor's coasting floor (3 s), AFK (10 s) and ramp-back stay untouched.
- **2026-10-24: The pause symbol replaces the pause banner.** Decision: `HudPauseBadge` (two bars, `UI_PAUSE_TITLE`, `UI_PAUSE_HINT`) sits on the HudLayer root at 15% of the screen height, centred, drawn last, and shows whenever the local rider is paused; no HudSpec flag hides it. HudBanner's paused state is gone. *Reason:* one clear symbol above every game, also for a multiplayer rider whose session runs on.
- **2026-10-24: Heart rate stays local and rides in the power gauge.** Decision: `LocalRider.heart_rate_source` (TrainerService's sensor) or a MockTrainer `hr` profile fills `Telemetry.heart_rate_bpm`; the GameHost zeroes it in every `frame.players` entry and in the telemetry a client sends; the power gauge shows it next to the watts while above 0; `RideRecorder.set_heart_rate()` records it and the FIT gets record, lap and session heart rate fields only when the ride has one, so rides without a sensor encode byte for byte as before. *Alternatives:* a separate HUD widget (every game's layout would need a place for it); always writing the fields (invalid values in every FIT). *Reason:* every game already shows the power gauge, and BRIEF 3.8 keeps each rider's data on their machine.
- **2026-10-25: the mock trainer's keyboard control moves to free keys, adds presets, and gets a Settings toggle.** Decision: T/G power and Y/H cadence (tap = step, held = ramp), a numpad-style block `M , . / J K L / U I O` (and the numpad) for 50 to 250 % FTP presets, N for stop, Shift held for a sprint; a Settings > Trainer toggle (`mock_keyboard`, only with the mock active) next to `--mock-keyboard`; a one-line `HudMockKeys` readout in the HUD's bottom-right corner. Presets and the sprint put the power on its value at once. *Alternatives:* keep W/S/A/D, digits 1-9 and Space (they are the movement, ability 1 and 2 and confirm bindings, so every press also moved menus or fired abilities); a game-drawn readout. *Reason:* the keys must not collide with the input map; the readout belongs to the platform so every game shows it the same way. The mock only produces telemetry, so the governor and BRIEF 4.6 are untouched (`tests/arch/test_no_bypass.gd` unchanged).
- **2026-10-26 (v0.3.0): A heart rate sensor is ready when heart rate flows.** Decision: `HrSensorManager` reports ready on the first 0x2A37 notification, not when the subscription is sent; no notification within `NOTIFY_TIMEOUT_S` (10 s) after subscribing or while ready, or a GATT reset, discovers and subscribes again on the same link once, and a second miss, a failed discovery or subscription (also a refused Windows pairing) or a link loss reconnects with the 1-2-4-8-15 s backoff. GDBLE's structured subscribe success (and the bridge's subscribe reply) become an additive `subscribed(char_uuid)` signal, used for a log line only. Every status change is printed once. A sharing GdbleTransport already out of the tree when its primary leaves (the app quitting) reports nothing. *Alternatives:* ready on subscribe success (a subscription can succeed and still deliver nothing after Windows bonds the strap); reconnect on every miss (slow, and a GATT reset only needs discovery). *Reason:* the v0.2.2 ride showed READY for 45 minutes with no heart rate in the FIT.
- **2026-10-26 (v0.3.0): Pause on not pedaling after 10 s.** Decision: `RidePause.ENTER_S` is 10 s (owner decision), the same moment as the governor's 4.6.4 solo pause, which is unchanged with the 3 s coasting floor; the BRIEF 7.4 assertion reads "paused (solo) or AFK no later than 10 s". *Alternatives:* keep 5 s. *Reason:* the owner's feedback after a 45-minute ride; 10 s is the ceiling 4.6.4 already sets.
- **2026-10-26 (v0.3.0): Fullscreen is a setting with F11 and Alt+Enter.** Decision: `WlWindowMode` applies the `fullscreen` setting at boot (`WINDOW_MODE_FULLSCREEN` or windowed), Settings > Audio and display toggles it, and F11 or Alt+Enter toggles and saves it on every screen (main scene). `WlInputRouter` never maps either key to an action (Alt+Enter is not `confirm`) and a key capture never binds them. *Alternatives:* a separate Display page (one more root row on a full Settings page). *Reason:* a maximized window is not fullscreen and drops the pixel scale.
- **2026-10-26 (v0.3.0): PixelViewport flex mode.** Decision: an optional `game.json` `pixel_flex: {"min": [w, h]}` (validated, 2D only, 1..pixel_base) sets `PixelViewport.flex_min`: k is the largest whole scale with window / k >= min, the SubViewport is floor(window / k) (never below min), centred, at most k - 1 px of border; `art_size()` and `art_size_changed` tell the game, `anchor_rect()` keeps HudSpec rects laid out on pixel_base with their nearest edge, and HudLayer maps every rect through it. Without the key the letterbox is unchanged. *Alternatives:* fractional scaling (blurry or uneven pixels); per-game anchoring in HudSpec (a contract change). *Reason:* the owner wants the picture to fill the screen with crisp pixels.
- **2026-10-26 (v0.3.0): A HUD rect too small for its widgets scales the stack.** Decision: when a rect slot's widgets need more than the rect (`min_rect()` above it, e.g. at a low art scale), HudLayer draws the slot's stack at a uniform scale below 1 (fonts included) so it stays inside its rect. *Alternatives:* let it overflow (it covered the neighbouring rect: watts over rpm in Turbo Reel). *Reason:* overlapping numbers are unreadable; the rect is the game's layout.
- **2026-10-26 (v0.3.0): No on-screen panic button.** Decision: the session screen drops its panic button and the corner it reserved; panic stays on Backspace held 1 s, gamepad View + Menu and the pause overlay's Panic item, and the overlay names the keys (`APP_SESSION_PANIC_HINT`). `HudLayer.reserve_corner` stays (games' tests use it). *Alternatives:* keep the button (it covered game art and HUD in every session). *Reason:* BRIEF 4.6.5 requires the governor's panic and the keys, not a button; owner decision.
- **2026-10-26 (v0.3.0): Lobby cards wrap.** Decision: a rider card's options line and trainer line (180 px) wrap, at most two lines each with an ellipsis, so long option labels no longer widen the card and push the menu column off screen. *Alternatives:* one column of cards (8 riders would not fit). *Reason:* a one-bot Turbo Reel lobby put Start off screen.
- **2026-10-26 (v0.3.0): The ERG haptic pull, the one owner-approved BRIEF 4.6 amendment.** Decision: `EffortDirective.haptic_pull` (% FTP) is a governed channel in `SafetyGovernor`: pull × haptic intensity, at most +50 % FTP, × 0.85 while FTP is unverified, on top of the governed ERG target for at most 3 s, then back at once; the target with the pull never exceeds min(150 % FTP, 1000 W, trainer max) nor a latched 105 %/120 % band, and its time above 105 %/120 % counts toward the budgets (they count `applied_target_w`, which includes it). It is exempt from slew and the long-phase 105 % cap only. Off (dropped and logged, or refused as the request's only effect) in SIM or a SIM block (`haptic_pull_in_sim`), for opted-out riders, in warm-up, rescue hold and ramp-back, coasting, AFK, pause, panic and the ramp_test profile (`haptic_pull_unsafe`); a running pull ends at once when any of those begins. One 15 s cooldown per rider for SIM haptics and pulls together, from the end of the previous one. `TrainerController.command_erg` takes the pull as an additive term on top of the slewed target (never during a SIM → ERG entry); while the trainer holds a pull, a write that keeps it on waits rather than close the 0.5 s write slot past the pull's end, so the drop-back is written by the end and the trainer never holds a pull longer than 3 s within 2 writes per second. Reported as `haptic_pull` in status/telemetry `clamped_by` (so the 1 Hz trace shows it) plus `haptic_pull_s/_w/_pct` and `target_with_pull_w` in the applied record. The new names are `Wl.CLAMP_PULL`, `Wl.REFUSE_PULL_SIM` and `Wl.REFUSE_PULL_UNSAFE`. *Alternatives:* a short ERG target directive (slew turns a 2 s +30 % step into a 2 s ramp nobody feels, and the 105 % long-phase cap blocks it in fish fights); a SIM block (10 s minimum, two ramped mode switches); separate cooldowns (simpler shared accounting, and a rider is in ERG or SIM, never both); a pull added before the floor and rescue (it would then need every rule's exemption reasoning; adding it last keeps every other rule unchanged). *Reason:* the owner wants to feel a hooked fish turn and pull; the bounds keep the tug brief, bounded by the absolute ceiling and the duration limits, and absent whenever the rider is unsafe.
- **2026-10-26 (v0.3.0): The help overlay and the `help` action.** Decision: `HudSpec.help` becomes `HudHelp`, a platform overlay in `HudLayer` (above the HUD, below the pause badge): one panel per entry centred on `at` and kept on screen, an optional arrow to `arrow_to` (art pixels drawn on the art grid with `grid_px` in a 2D game, else a screen-space line), the game's palette, the title `UI_HELP_TITLE`, closed after 20 s of the session's platform time. The GameHost owns its input: `help` toggles it (an empty help shows the toast `UI_HELP_NONE`), and while it is open `back` and `confirm` close it and are consumed; every other action passes and the sim keeps running. The app's session screen leaves a `back` to the GameHost while the overlay is open instead of opening its menu. `auto_help` (GameHost config, the app passes true) opens it once per profile and game id, remembered in `Paths.help_seen_path()` by `HelpSeen` only when there was help to show; the screenshot tool marks its game seen so shots show the game. `help` is an additive contract action in `Wl.ACTIONS` with F1 and the left stick click in `project.godot`'s input map (no other binding uses either), rebindable and saved in input.cfg like the others, listed in Settings > Controls (whose list uses the small font to keep every row on a 1080p screen) and in `WlInputRouter.PLATFORM_ACTIONS`. *Alternatives:* a code-only platform action outside the contract (two sources of defaults); let the session screen own the overlay (headless tools and other hosts of a GameHost would lose it, and two owners would race on `back`). *Reason:* the owner wants every game to explain its screen the same way, without stopping the ride.
- **2026-10-27 (v0.3.0): Rides page: a ride's actions, copies and the rides folder.** Decision: picking a ride on Settings > Rides opens its actions (upload or re-upload, needing Strava; "Copy title" and "Copy description", which copy exactly `WlRides.activity_name` / `activity_description` of the saved summary and work without Strava), so ride rows are no longer disabled without Strava. The page shows once, small and dim, "Rides are saved in: <ProjectSettings.globalize_path(Paths.rides_dir())>" with a "Copy folder path" item.
