Copies go through the screen's `clipboard_set` Callable (`DisplayServer.clipboard_set`; tests swap it) and confirm with a notice under the status line for 3 s (the app has no toast outside a session). *Alternatives:* two more rows per ride (triples the scan list); copy buttons beside each row (not pedal-scan selectable). *Reason:* the owner uploads rides by hand and wants Strava's exact title and description.

### trainer-core

Short ADRs for `addons/wattlands/trainer/` (FtmsCodec, FtmsClient, FtmsTrainer, MockTrainer), `tests/trainer/` and `tests/vectors/ftms.json` (BRIEF 1, 4.1-4.6).

- 2026-10-21. Decision: `FtmsCodec` is a static, pure class; parsers return a Dictionary of named fields, or `{}` after a `push_error` when bytes are short or malformed. Scaled values decode as `raw / 10^n` (3050 -> 30.5 exactly as JSON writes it), unscaled ones as ints. Alternatives: typed record classes; returning `{ok, error}`. Reason: Dictionaries cross into `bike_data` signals, reports and JSON vectors unchanged, and dividing by an exact power of ten gives the same double in GDScript, Python and JS, so the golden vectors compare exactly.
- 2026-10-21. Decision: encoders round half away from zero, then clamp to the field's range (sint16, uint8 ...); Set Target Power clamps to 0..32767 (never negative); NaN encodes as 0. Safety limits (grade -10..+16 %, the ERG ceilings) stay in the governor, not the codec. Alternatives: banker's rounding; clamping to KICKR limits in the codec. Reason: one rule every language can reproduce (the vectors pin it with exact binary ties such as 0.125 % -> 13 and -0.125 % -> -13), and BRIEF 4.6 keeps every safety rule in one place.
- 2026-10-21. Decision: Indoor Bike Data reassembly merges parts until one has More Data clear; a part that repeats a field already pending flushes the pending record as incomplete (`more_data` true), and 8 parts flush it too. A malformed part is dropped without discarding the pending record. Alternatives: drop incomplete records; reset on every error. Reason: some FTMS devices set More Data on every notification; with the repeat rule they still deliver a record per notification (one notification late) instead of none.
- 2026-10-21. Decision: `tests/vectors/ftms.json` holds `encode`, `parse` and `assemble` lists with lower-case hex and a `_doc` array that defines every key, op, rounding and error rule. A parse case is either `valid: true` with the complete expected output, or `valid: false`. Alternatives: one file per kind; only valid cases. Reason: ble's Python tests reuse the file; a self-describing format and explicit malformed cases let them reach byte parity without reading GDScript. The vectors were cross-checked with an independent JS implementation before commit.
- 2026-10-21. Decision: `FtmsClient` waits `SUBSCRIBE_SETTLE_S` (0.25 s) after subscribing (0x2AD2, 0x2ADA, then 0x2AD9 last) before Request Control. Alternatives: write at once. Reason: `BleTransport` has no subscribe acknowledgement, btleplug writes the CCCD asynchronously, and an indication missed because the CCCD write lost the race would cost a 2 s timeout.
- 2026-10-21. Decision: reads use the control point's timing (2 s, two retries) one at a time; Feature is required, the ranges and Device Information are optional and keep KICKR defaults (0..1800 W, 0..100 resistance). Alternatives: fail on any missing read. Reason: KICKR firmware versions differ in what they expose, and the defaults are the trainer the brief targets.
- 2026-10-21. Decision: under the hardware lock the flow stops after subscribing: state `read_only`, a `hardware control locked` warning and `client_ready`, so telemetry flows; `is_ready()` stays false and every write (`write_control`, `request_control`, the flow's own Request Control and SIM flat) is refused and logged. The lock is checked at entry and again right before the transport write. Alternatives: fail the flow; never emit `client_ready`. Reason: BRIEF 1 allows scan, read and subscribe; a read-only client keeps the probe-style data path testable, and the second check covers a lock set while writes were queued.
- 2026-10-21. Decision: control-flow tests use `tests/trainer/unlocked_ftms_client.gd`, a test-only subclass that overrides `_is_hw_locked()` for that instance and only talks to the fake KICKR. The process keeps `WATTLANDS_NO_HW_CONTROL=1` throughout; lock tests use the real `FtmsClient` with the variable set to `1` explicitly and restored. Alternatives: set the variable to `0` around each test. Reason: no test ever lifts the process-wide lock (BRIEF 4.6.10), and the production class has no bypass: it always reads the environment.
- 2026-10-21. Decision: queued (unsent) Set Target Resistance/Power/Simulation writes are replaced by a newer write with the same op code; Request Control, Reset, Start and Stop are never coalesced. Alternatives: plain FIFO. Reason: BRIEF 4.5's "drop superseded commands; always send the newest", without ever reordering or dropping a state change.
- 2026-10-21. Decision: a control-point write still unanswered after its two retries (6 s) emits `failed()` and drops the queue; result 0x05 or Machine Status 0xFF emits `control_lost("another app may hold control")`; `request_control()` (added) re-requests control and returns to ready through SIM flat. Alternatives: skip the timed-out write and continue. Reason: three silent attempts mean the link is gone, and the connection manager's reconnect (BRIEF 4.4) is the right recovery.
- 2026-10-21. Decision: Cycling Power fallback is active when the Feature lacks power measurement (machine bit 14) and the trainer offers 0x1818/0x2A63; the latest CPS power fills `power_w` (`power_source: "cps"`, `TrainerSample.POWER_FROM_CPS`) into bike data records that lack it. Alternatives: emit separate CPS samples. Reason: one sample stream per device keeps LocalRider unchanged.
- 2026-10-21. Decision: a client inside the tree ticks itself from `_process` (PROCESS_MODE_ALWAYS); a client outside the tree is ticked by `FtmsTrainer.tick(dt)`. Alternatives: only `_process`; only the trainer. Reason: the connection manager runs the flow before an `FtmsTrainer` exists, while tests and headless runs need deterministic ticks.
- 2026-10-21. Decision: `FtmsTrainer` does not estimate cadence. A record without cadence becomes a sample without `HAS_CADENCE`; `CadenceEstimator` (trainer-control), which LocalRider runs on every sample, fills it. Alternatives: a second estimator inside FtmsTrainer. Reason: the W0 contracts put estimation in `CadenceEstimator`; two estimators would disagree.
- 2026-10-21. Decision: MockTrainer fatigue is a hard W' model: the follower holds any target while W' (Skiba, CP = FTP unless `cp_w`, W' 20 kJ unless `wprime_j`) lasts, then gives 95 % of CP until 10 % of W' has come back. Scripted profiles ignore fatigue. Alternatives: power fading with W'. Reason: time to failure is exactly W' / (P - CP) (150 % FTP fails after about 160 s, sub-CP targets never do), Workout's VO2 session never exhausts a compliant rider, and the analytic ramp-test model in BRIEF 7.4 stays valid.
- 2026-10-21. Decision: every surge lasts at least until two notifications carried it (it is extended past `for_s` if needed), no dropout touches a surge or the 10 s before one, and gaps never chain. Alternatives: align surges to the notification schedule. Reason: docs/OWNERSHIP.md C30: ten surges must give ten ERG surge gestures; the test runs five seeds with and without dropouts.
- 2026-10-21. Decision: `band` uses a first-order lag (tau 2 s) and an Ornstein-Uhlenbeck noise (sd 0.6 rpm, tau 4 s) clamped to +-1.5 rpm, so samples (rounded to 0.5 rpm) stay within 2 rpm of centre + offset. Alternatives: white noise. Reason: smooth like a rider and bounded, so Fishing's +-18 rpm offsets hold 10 rpm outside a 16 rpm band.
- 2026-10-21. Decision: `cadence_drop` rpm 0 means the rider stops (0 W); any other rpm leaves power to the ERG follower. `at_phase` counts `after_s` from entering the phase and re-arms if the phase ends first; `at_target_pct` compares the commanded ERG target (what the governor applied, after the unverified factor) and ignores SIM. Each instance fires once; overlapping drops use the lowest rpm. Alternatives: drops that also cut power. Reason: ERG holds power at low cadence on a real trainer; the rescue rule's cadence branch is what these scenarios exercise.
- 2026-10-21. Decision: the follower sprints outside ERG at >= 120 % FTP: cadence +10 % (not for `band` riders) and a reported cadence that lags its fall for 5 s (tau 1.5 s); the ERG flywheel speed follows the true cadence, SIM speed comes from a `SpeedModel` (rider `weight_kg` + 8 kg) at the commanded grade and wind, seeded from the flywheel speed on entry. Alternatives: speed from cadence in SIM too. Reason: BRIEF 4.5's "cadence ... lags after sprints" and 3.11's speed model, without false ERG surge gestures (the lift never applies in ERG).
- 2026-10-21. Decision: test doubles (`fake_kickr_transport.gd`, `unlocked_ftms_client.gd`) have no `class_name` and are loaded by path. Alternatives: `FakeBleTransport`. Reason: other W1 modules may write doubles with the same obvious names; script classes are global.
- 2026-10-21 (hardening, supersedes the coalescing entry above). Decision: a new Set Target Resistance/Power/Simulation write replaces only the queue's last (unsent) write, and only when that write and the one going out just before it (queued or in flight) have the same op code; otherwise it is appended. Alternatives: replace a same-op write anywhere in the queue (the first W1 rule); plain FIFO. Reason: the first rule could reorder commands ([ERG 200, SIM 0] + ERG 180 sent ERG 180 before SIM 0, leaving the trainer in SIM while the controller meant ERG) and could drop the entry write of a ramped mode switch (SIM grade 0, the low ERG entry, BRIEF 4.5). Tail-only replacement inside a same-op run keeps the order and every mode's first write, and still drops superseded targets.
- 2026-10-21 (hardening). Decision: service discovery that has not answered after `FtmsClient.DISCOVER_TIMEOUT_S` (10 s) fails the flow with `failed("service discovery timed out after 10 s")`. Alternatives: wait forever; retry discovery inside the client. Reason: without it a lost `services_discovered` left the client in `discovering` with no signal, so the connection manager never reconnected; recovery belongs to its backoff (BRIEF 4.4).
- 2026-10-21 (hardening). Decision: `MockTrainer.restart_profile()` restarts profile time (at_s drops, surges, ramp, intervals) without touching the rider, the commanded state or the notification schedule; `profile_time_s()` reports it. Alternatives: rebuild the mock at session start; count at_s from the first phase. Reason: with `--mock-trainer` the TrainerService creates the mock at launch and ticks it through the menus, so at_s and surges would otherwise count menu time; rebuilding would swap the device under the governor.
- 2026-10-21 (W2r2). Decision: MockTrainer's keyboard control keeps BRIEF 4.2's keys (W and S ±10 W, A and D ∓/±5 rpm), reads them with `Input.is_physical_key_pressed` only while enabled and while the app window has focus (`Window.has_focus()`; false outside the tree, so headless runs never read keys), and counts a key when it goes down; the first poll after enabling or after the window regains focus only notes the held keys. `key_reader` and `focus_reader` (additive Callables) let tests inject both; `is_keyboard_enabled()` is additive. The app enables it only with `--mock-keyboard` (platform), and W/S/A/D then also move menu focus and the games' up/down/left/right, because they are the InputRouter's WASD bindings (BRIEF 3.5). Alternatives: other keys free of the bindings (I/K, J/L or the numpad), which would contradict BRIEF 4.2's W/S/A/D; filtering the keys out of the InputRouter while the flag is on (ui-hud's file, and it would take WASD movement away from a keyboard-only tester). Reason: BRIEF 4.2 names W/S/A/D; the overlap only matters for a developer who asks for it with an explicit flag, and the focus check keeps typing in another window from moving the simulated rider.
- 2026-10-21 (W4, ftms-ble-1). Decision: a control-point write the transport rejects inside the write call itself (GDBLE's synchronous BUSY or QUEUE_FULL arrive as `operation_failed("write")` during `write_characteristic`) is retried on the client's next tick instead of from inside that call; an asynchronous failure is still retried at once. A send with no transport left (teardown) drops the queue with a warning. Alternatives: a fixed retry delay for every failure (slower for the common async case). Reason: three synchronous rejections recursed into `failed()` within one write; one tick between tries lets the backend drain.
- 2026-10-21 (W4, SAF-8). Decision: `FtmsTrainer.release_client()` (additive) disconnects the trainer object from the client: its writes are dropped with push_warning, its status reads disconnected and no more bike data arrives. The connection manager calls it when another trainer's link replaces this one.
- 2026-10-21 (W4, ftms-ble-5). Decision: the bridge's Python codec follows the GDScript codec's rules (round half away from zero, clamp each field to its range, target power to 0..32767, NaN to 0, and the sint16 resistance encoding too), its control-response and machine-status parsers add the GDScript keys (`request_opcode`, `params`, the per-code values), and `tools/trainer_bridge/tests/test_ftms.py` runs every shared encode case and every shared parse case of a kind it implements from `tests/vectors/ftms.json` (the energy keys keep the bridge's names, mapped in the test). Reason: BRIEF 4.3 "reuse them in the Python tests"; W3 found 7 mismatches that the Indoor-Bike-Data-only reuse never saw.
- 2026-10-21 (W4, QT-5). Decision: `MockTrainer._options` removed; it was only written.

### trainer-control

Short ADRs for `addons/wattlands/trainer/control/`, `tests/governor/`, `tests/control/` and `tests/arch/test_no_bypass.gd` (BRIEF 4.5, 4.6, 7.1).

- **2026-10-21: The controller owns slew, the switch entries and coalescing; the governor owns every other rule.** Each tick the governor computes the governed ERG target (every 4.6 rule except slew) or the stacked SIM state and hands it to `TrainerController.command_erg` / `command_sim`; the controller slews increases at 30 W/s (decreases at once), runs the ramped ERG <-> SIM sequences and decides when to write. *Alternatives:* slew in the governor. *Reason:* BRIEF 4.1 lists slew under the controller and 4.6.12 applies it last; the SIM -> ERG entry must start slewing only once ERG is actually written, which only the controller knows.
- **2026-10-21: Coalescing is one write per 0.5 s slot, computed from the newest command when the slot opens.** Superseded commands are never written, the newest is always sent, and an unchanged state (SIM compared at FTMS resolution: grade 0.01 %, wind 0.001 m/s, crr 0.0001, cw 0.01) writes nothing. *Alternatives:* a sliding 2-per-second window (allows bursts). *Reason:* even spacing suits the KICKR's one-in-flight control point. `status.applied_target_w` is the slewed target, at most one slot ahead of the trainer.
- **2026-10-21: Panic and shutdown write at once, bypassing the slot.** `emergency_safe()` sends SIM grade 0 and wind 0 in the call; `shutdown()` sends SIM flat, then Stop (0x08 0x01), back to back. *Alternative:* wait for the slot. *Reason:* 4.6.5 says "immediately"; safety outranks the write rate. The next ordinary write still waits 0.5 s after them. A trainer bound with `setup()` while panic holds gets the SIM flat write at once.
- **2026-10-21: Ramped switches (4.5) exactly as written.** ERG -> SIM: ERG at max(floor, min(applied target, 3 s power)) when that is below the written target, then SIM grade 0 / wind 0, then the stacked state, each in its own slot. SIM -> ERG: SIM grade 0 / wind 0 when not already flat, then ERG at max(floor, min(3 s power, governed target)), then slew. The first ERG command after connecting uses the same entry (the trainer was left at SIM flat by the connection flow); after a panic resume the entry is the floor (W0 stub doc). The 3 s power is the time-weighted mean of the samples of the last 3 s, each held until the next; `setup()` clears it, so a reconnect enters from the floor.
- **2026-10-21: Samples take effect on the next tick.** `on_sample` queues; `tick` advances platform time first and then processes the queue at that time. *Reason:* with LocalRider's order (device.tick, on_sample, governor.tick) every rule is timed from the tick in which the sample arrived, which makes "3 s (+1 tick)" and "10 s (+1 tick)" exact in tests.
- **2026-10-21: not_pedaling counts an absent cadence as low.** `power < 15 W and (no cadence or cadence < 20 rpm)`. *Reason:* a trainer with no cadence and 3 W is not being pedaled; a rider producing >= 15 W is pedaling whatever the cadence field says.
- **2026-10-21: Rescue triggers are timed while armed.** Cadence < 45 rpm for 3 s or power < 75 % of the applied target for 5 s must hold for the whole window while rescue is armed (in ERG, not paused, governed target >= 60 % FTP after the unverified factor and the 4.6.1 caps, before the warm-up cap and slew); the window starts at the later of the first qualifying sample and the moment rescue armed. "Applied target" for the power test is the target last written to the trainer. *Alternatives:* timers independent of arming (a 70 %-compliant rider coming out of a 50 % recovery would be rescued on the first tick of the next interval); the slewed target (a few watts ahead of the trainer). *Reason:* the most literal reading of "in ERG with ... if ... for 3 s", and no carry-over of a power test made against another phase's target.
- **2026-10-21 (hardening review): Rescue arms on whole watts.** Rescue is armed when the whole-watt governed target is at least floor(60 % FTP), the same whole-watt comparison 4.6.1 uses for the caps and the duration counters. At FTP 201 a 60 % target governs to 120 W (60 % FTP is 120.6 W) and arms rescue; a 59.5 % target (119 W) does not. *Alternative:* compare against the exact 120.6 W, which left a target written at exactly 60 % unarmed at most FTPs. *Reason:* a target exactly at a threshold behaves as at it (4.6.1), and arming errs on the protective side.
- **2026-10-21: Rescue details.** The rescue target is min(governed target, 50 % FTP) (it never raises a lower target), then the floor; the hold ends when cadence >= 70 rpm for 3 s from the first such sample, which emits `rescue_changed(false)` and starts a linear 10 s ramp from 50 % to the channel's current governed value (slew still applies); a SIM phase ends rescue at once (SIM load is self-selected); a new trigger during the ramp restarts the hold. In the ramp_test profile the trigger ends the test (`test_ended`, signal `ramp_test_ended`): 50 % stands until the next `set_plan`, never ramping back.
- **2026-10-21: Warm-up runs on platform time from the first pedaling sample.** It counts 300 s of elapsed platform time from the first sample that is not not_pedaling, and resets once no pedaling sample has arrived for 1200 s. It survives `setup()` (a BLE reconnect does not restart it). Before the first stroke it is active and pending (`warmup_remaining_s` 300). The SIM clamp -2..+3 % applies to the total grade.
- **2026-10-21: Duration limits use the controller's applied ERG target on platform time.** A tick counts above 105 % / 120 % when the whole-watt applied target is above floor(105 % / 120 % FTP); the reset run counts ticks with the applied target <= 90 % FTP in ERG and resets both counters and latches at 120.0 s inclusive; any tick not in ERG (SIM, panic, a switch in progress) restarts the run and adds nothing. The latch takes effect from the next tick, so the time above is exactly 300 s / 60 s.
- **2026-10-21: Directive validation: whole-request refusals and haptic drops.** No phase, the cool-down, bad fields (unknown mode, non-finite values, negative duration, more than one target field, nothing set), `erg_in_sim_phase` (mode ERG or an ERG target in a SIM phase), `haptic_in_erg` (a haptic to a rider not in a SIM phase or block, even with a valid block request: never a mode switch), `outside_range_pct`, SIM block length / unsafe / spacing and `mode_grade_outside_sim` refuse the whole request. A haptic dropped for opt-out, warm-up or cooldown is listed in `record.dropped` and the rest of the request applies; with nothing else set the request is refused with that reason. *Reason:* Siege S10 needs "no dropped channel" as a separate notion from refusal. `haptic_in_erg` refuses the whole request rather than dropping only the haptic because games send haptics as `{mode: SIM, haptic, duration_s}` (T2.7, F2.7): dropping only the haptic would let such a request with a 10-30 s duration open a SIM block for an ERG rider, which is exactly the "haptic turned into a mode switch" 4.6.6 forbids. Opting out (`configure({haptics_on: false})`) also ends a running haptic at once.
- **2026-10-21: Channel details.** `mode: SIM` while a block runs (or in a SIM phase) confirms without changing the block's expiry, so a block can never be extended past 30 s. `mode: ERG` in an ERG phase ends a running block (the exempt closing switch) or confirms. The end of a SIM block takes its mode grade and haptic with it. A factors request replaces the whole `crr`/`cw` channel (an unset factor goes back to 1.0). Expiry counts game time snapped to whole ticks; `duration_s` 0 holds until the phase ends; every channel resets at `start_phase` and `set_plan`. Switch spacing is measured on platform time from the last ERG <-> SIM change of the governor's mode, plan phases and panic included.
- **2026-10-21: Haptic bounds.** Grade x intensity bounded to -3..+3 % (positive bound from 4.6.6, the symmetric negative bound to keep haptics small); duration min(duration_s, 8 s) in game time with a hard 8 s platform-time cap, so a pause can never stretch a haptic; a new haptic while one is active, or within 15 s (platform time) of the end of the last applied haptic or haptic pull (one shared cooldown), is a cooldown drop. Haptic wind x intensity is bounded so that cw x ((v + w)^2 - v^2) stays within the force of +3 % grade minus what the haptic grade already used, at the latest reported speed (20 m/s assumed when unknown, the conservative case); grade and wind share one +3 % budget. `haptic_intensity` names the scaling, `haptic_limit` only the +3 % / 8 s bounds (OWNERSHIP C5).
- **2026-10-21: Course grades.** `set_course_grade` is accepted only in `grade_source: game` phases (else `grade_source_not_game`) and the applied value moves at most every 0.5 s to the newest value (2 Hz). `course` phases follow `course_profile` by the distance from `set_course_distance` minus the distance at the phase start, linear between points, holding the first/last grade outside them, through the same 2 Hz coalescer. Every `set_course_grade` call is reported as a `course_grade` record.
- **2026-10-21: Virtual gears are fixed grade offsets tuned at a reference speed.** Gear g adds the grade that multiplies resistance at 30 km/h (83 kg, crr 0.004, cw 0.51) by 1.07^(g - 12) relative to the base grade (flat-road force for descents); gear 12 is neutral; gear 1 is about -2.5 % and gear 24 about +6.0 % on the flat. At 30 km/h every step is exactly 7 % at any base grade >= 0; at constant flywheel speeds elsewhere the step drifts: 8-17 % at 25 km/h, 4-6 % at 35 km/h, 2-4.5 % at 45 km/h (lower gears at low speed reach zero resistance like any descent). *Alternative:* offsets that track the rider's measured speed (7 % at every speed, but the grade then moves with the rider and costs extra writes). *Reason:* BRIEF 4.5 asks for a grade offset and allows a documented approximation; fixed offsets are deterministic and predictable. Shifts count only while the rider rides SIM; the gear survives phase changes.
- **2026-10-21: status() reports what the trainer is doing.** `mode` is the controller's written mode (so a 30 s SIM block shows about 30 s of SIM on a 1 Hz trace), `applied_target_w` the slewed target (0 outside ERG), `applied_grade_pct` the stacked grade while in SIM; `desired_mode` (`ERG`, `SIM`, `SAFE`, `OFF`), `governed_target_w`, `written_target_w` and `written_grade_pct` are added. `clamped_by` names `switch_ramp` while a switch runs or the ERG target climbs from a switch entry.
- **2026-10-21: Bad input.** `start_phase` outside the plan, `set_plan(null)`, a non-positive FTP and an unknown profile are `push_error`s and keep the previous state; out-of-range numeric configuration is clamped with `push_warning`; refused directives are `push_warning`s. `set_plan` keeps the current phase index when the new plan has it (a replan between phases).
- **2026-10-21: CadenceEstimator.** Present cadence passes through and teaches the km/h-per-rpm ratio (exponential average, alpha 0.1, only at >= 40 rpm and >= 5 km/h, and not from a repeated value while the speed moved > 5 %); an absent cadence, or one unchanged for more than `stale_s` while the speed moved > 15 %, is replaced by speed / ratio with `CADENCE_ESTIMATED`; a 10 s calibration sets the ratio from the mean speed at the target cadence. Added `cadence_age_s(now)` (for `Telemetry.cadence_age_s`) and `reset()`.
- **2026-10-21: The no-bypass scan lexes GDScript.** `tests/arch/test_no_bypass.gd` strips comments and string contents with a small GDScript lexer (normal, triple-quoted, raw, `&` and `^` strings), then flags any code reference to a guarded name that is not a `func` definition, and any string literal that is exactly a guarded name (dynamic `call`, `callv`, `Callable`, `call_deferred`, `has_method`). The allowlist is per file and per name. Fixtures prove it flags a bypassing script in a temp folder and passes comments, definitions and messages. Names built at run time can't be seen statically; review covers them.
- **2026-10-21 (hardening review): The scan also guards `request_stop`.** Besides the five names of BRIEF 4.6.11, `TrainerDevice.request_stop` (Stop, 0x08 0x01) may be called only by `trainer_controller.gd`: the TrainerDevice contract lists it with the setters, and the exit path (4.6.9) reaches it only through `SafetyGovernor.shutdown()`. `FtmsTrainer` and `MockTrainer` implement it (definitions are allowed) and never call it. *Alternative:* the five BRIEF names only. *Reason:* a Stop from anywhere else would bypass the governor's SIM-flat-then-Stop sequence; no current module calls it.
- **2026-10-21 (W2): A reconnect of the same device resyncs through the ramped path.** Decision: `SafetyGovernor.on_trainer_reconnected()` (additive) drops the stale 3 s power and calls `TrainerController.resync(crr, cw)`, which sets the written state to SIM flat (what `FtmsClient.begin()` leaves), forces the next SIM command to be written once even if it equals that guess, and counts the flow's own write for the 0.5 s slot. The current mode then comes back exactly like a first command: ERG through the SIM -> ERG entry max(floor, min(3 s power, governed target)) and 30 W/s slew, SIM by writing the stacked state. Plan, phase, warm-up, limits, rescue, coasting, gears and channels are kept; a reconnect is no directive switch for the 30 s spacing. Panic re-sends SIM flat at once; after `shutdown()` nothing is sent (only `setup()` revives). A new device object still goes through `setup(device)`. *Alternatives:* `setup(device)` again with the same object (resets to OFF and loses the slot accounting); re-sending the last written command as is (could jump straight to a high ERG target from SIM flat, against 4.5 and 4.6.2). *Reason:* BRIEF 4.4 "restore the last safe mode" with the 4.5 switch rules and 4.6.2 slew; the trainer really is at SIM flat after the flow.
- **2026-10-21 (W2): The no-bypass scan admits GdbleTransport's single hand-off by count.** Decision: `ALLOW_SITES` admits at most n call sites of a name per file (gdble_transport.gd: one `write_characteristic`, the `&"write_characteristic"` in `_device_write`); `bridge_transport.gd` and the transports no longer have a whole-file allowance (they only define `write_characteristic`, and definitions were never call sites). A second hand-off is flagged. *Alternative:* the whole-file allowance ble's W1 branch used. *Reason:* ble asked for exactly the one hand-off; a count keeps the scan as strict as possible for real call sites.
- **2026-10-21 (W2r2): With no phase the governor rides SIM grade 0, wind 0.** Decision: with no plan, a plan before its first `start_phase()`, or after the new `clear_plan()`, `_command_controller` sends `command_sim(0, 0, crr, cw)` instead of `command_off()`; `desired_mode` stays `OFF`, so it is no ERG/SIM switch for the 30 s spacing (as the connection flow's own SIM flat never was). From ERG the controller ramps there (4.5 step-down first). `clear_plan()` (additive) drops plan, phase and every channel and ends a rescue; warm-up, limits, gears and panic stay. *Alternatives:* keep `command_off()` (the trainer keeps whatever was written last: the cool-down's 50 % or an interrupted interval's target in the menus); count it as a switch (would refuse SIM blocks in the first 30 s of every session for no physical reason). *Reason:* platform's ADR says menus ride free at SIM flat, and after a session the trainer must not hold an ERG target nobody plans.
- **2026-10-21 (W2r2): Warm-up and the trainer's written state belong to the trainer connection.** Decision: the 4.6.8 warm-up state moves into `GovernorWarmup` (start, done, last pedal stroke on its own clock, advanced by the governor's ticks). `SafetyGovernor.adopt_connection(previous)` (additive) copies it, `TrainerController.adopt()` copies the written state and the time since the last write, and the rider's 3 s power history and latest speed carry over, when both governors are bound to the same device object. LocalRider calls it from `setup()`. Plan, phase, channels, rescue, coasting, duration limits, gears and panic stay per governor (per session). A new device object starts afresh; a reconnect keeps everything (same governor). *Alternatives:* keep one governor across sessions and reset its session state (its platform time, switch history and injected test governors would all need special cases); move only the warm-up (a fresh controller after a session would jump from the cool-down's ERG straight to SIM flat without the 4.5 step-down and could write within 0.5 s of the old controller's last write). *Reason:* BRIEF 4.6.8 counts warm-up "from the first pedal stroke after the trainer connects", and 4.5 ramps every switch from what the trainer really holds.
- **2026-10-21 (W2r2): No cadence is estimated from the flywheel while the rider coasts.** Decision: `CadenceEstimator` never estimates when the sample's power is below 15 W (BRIEF 3.5 not_pedaling): a reported cadence is kept even when it stays unchanged while the speed falls (a crank at rest reads 0), and an absent one stays absent. A frozen 0 rpm reading at 15 W or more is still stale. *Alternatives:* keep the W1 rule (a 0 rpm reading unchanged for 3 s while the flywheel spins down counts as a frozen estimate and becomes speed / ratio, so a coasting rider reads as pedaling for as long as the flywheel spins). *Reason:* found by the W2r2 warm-up tests: in SIM the mock's coasting speed made a stopped rider "pedal" for about 70 s, which would hide the coasting floor, pause and AFK (4.6.4), stop_5s and the warm-up's 20-minute reset (4.6.8) on a real trainer's spinning-down flywheel too.
- **2026-10-21 (W4, SAF-2): Duration limits belong to the trainer connection too.** Decision: `SafetyGovernor.adopt_connection()` also copies the time above 105% and above 120% FTP, both latches and the run of ERG at <= 90% FTP toward the 120 s reset, so they survive `LocalRider.setup()` on the same device object (every session and every menu reset builds a new governor). They reset only as BRIEF 4.6.1 says (120 consecutive seconds of ERG at <= 90% FTP) or with a new trainer connection (another device object). Supersedes the W2r2 line "duration limits ... stay per governor (per session)". *Alternatives:* keep them per session (W3: End ride while latched, then a session whose first 300 s are SIM, gave a fresh 60 s / 300 s budget with no reset run); one governor for the whole connection (its platform time, switch history and injected test governors would all need special cases). *Reason:* 4.6.1 names one release condition, and ending a session is not it.
- **2026-10-21 (W4, SAF-6): The menus' SIM flat counts as SIM for the 30 s spacing.** Decision: with no phase the governor commands SIM grade 0 (W2r2), so for switch tracking OFF is SIM: ERG -> OFF (`clear_plan()`) is a switch, and OFF -> ERG is one when the controller has the trainer at SIM (the menus, the connection flow's SIM flat after a resync, a cool-down that already stepped down). A governor whose controller has written nothing yet knows no previous mode, so its first ERG is no switch (in-process simulations and the governor rig start that way). `adopt_connection()` carries the last switch time, rebased onto the new governor's clock. The sdk's `PhaseTracker` still starts at NO_SWITCH; the rider's governor refuses such a block either way (requests for lead). Supersedes the W2r2 reason "would refuse SIM blocks ... for no physical reason". *Alternatives:* count OFF -> ERG always (a fresh rig's first ERG would refuse every early SIM block although nothing was written); keep OFF as no mode (W3: SIM -> ERG -> SIM within 1 s at a session start). *Reason:* BRIEF 4.5 counts from "the rider's previous switch" at the trainer, and since W2r2 the menus really hold SIM flat.
- **2026-10-21 (W4, SAF-7): A stale-sample watchdog.** Decision: while the device reports a link (status not disconnected, scanning or connecting), no trainer sample for more than `STALE_SAMPLE_S` (3 s, LocalRider's `STALE_S`) counts as one not_pedaling sample (0 W, no cadence) at the moment the stream went stale, fed through the same per-sample path as a real one: the coasting floor follows 3 s later, pause/AFK 10 s after that, and the rescue power trigger sees 0 W (the coasting floor wins over rescue, 4.6.12). It fires once per stale stretch; the next real sample ends it. The watchdog counts from `setup(device)`, a reconnect (`on_trainer_reconnected`) or the latest sample, and `adopt_connection()` carries it. Status gains `samples_stale` (and `reset_run_s` for SAF-2), both additive. *Alternatives:* inject the synthetic sample in LocalRider (the governor is the safety authority and also runs without LocalRider in tests and tools); count from the last sample itself (coasting 3 s after a rider was last seen pedaling, before the stream is even stale); also while the link is down (a link drop is the reconnect flow's job, and the re-entry already starts at the floor). *Reason:* BRIEF 4.6.4 times coasting from samples, but a KICKR that stops notifying left the ERG target at the interval's value while the telemetry already showed 0 W. Hardware check: whether the KICKR CORE keeps sending Indoor Bike Data at about 1 Hz with zeros when the flywheel stops, and how long notifications can stall on Windows/GDBLE while the link stays up (requests for lead: a HARDWARE_TEST_PLAN item).
- **2026-10-21 (W4, SAF-1): `configure()` rejects an FTP outside 30-2000 W.** Decision: `GovernorClamps.FTP_MIN_W` / `FTP_MAX_W` (the range Settings clamps a profile into); `configure({ftp_w})` outside it push_errors and keeps the previous value (the default 200 W for a new governor). *Alternatives:* clamp into the range with a warning (an absurd FTP from a hostile session_start would still set a 2000 W basis); keep "> 0" (W3: 5000 W from a host gave a 1500 W floor). *Reason:* BRIEF 4.6.7, the client validates locally; the rider's own FTP comes from the profile (LocalRider, docs/decisions/platform.md).
- **2026-10-21 (W4, SAF-4): The 4.6.11 scan also holds games to ctx.directives.** Decision: under `src/games/`, `tests/arch/test_no_bypass.gd` also fails on references to the trainer stack's classes and autoload (TrainerService, LocalRider, SafetyGovernor, TrainerController, the devices, FtmsClient, the connection manager and transports, DirectiveRouter, HudRouter), on `.governor`, `.configure_governor`, `.emergency_safe`, `.resume`, `.local_rider`, `.trainer_service`, `._riders`, `directives.riders` and `hud.riders`, on bare calls of `configure_governor(`, `emergency_safe(` and `resume(` (definitions excepted), and on those names as exact string literals; `.tscn` and `.tres` files are scanned as text everywhere for the guarded setters and, under `src/games/`, for those names and for script paths into the trainer stack. A bare `.riders` is not flagged because Workout's own `WorkoutState.riders` is a game field; the routers' maps go private in the sdk (session fixer). *Reason:* W3's fixture game reconfigured the governor to ramp_test, set an unvalidated 200% plan and called resume() with 0 violations.
- **2026-10-21 (W4r2, SAF-4 recheck): The scan also closes the GameHost route.** A GameModule is its GameHost's child, so `get_parent()` reached the host's rider map and `LocalRider.setup({governor_profile: "ramp_test", skip_warmup: true})`, `set_plan()` and `start_phase()` without a guarded name. Decision: under `src/games/` the scan also fails on `GameHost`, on any member access whose name holds `local_rider` (`.local_riders`, `._local_riders`, an accessor), on `governor_profile` anywhere in code or as a string, on a string naming a stack script's file (a preload or load by path), on the exact strings `set_plan`, `start_phase` and `resume_phase`, and on calls of `.setup(`, `.set_plan(`, `.start_phase(` and `.resume_phase(` on a receiver that is not rooted at `ctx`, `self` or `super`, unless the receiver is a plain variable that the file declares, in every declaration, with a type that can't hold a LocalRider, SafetyGovernor or GameHost (anything but untyped, Variant, Object, Node or a stack class). *Alternatives:* flag every `.setup(` on a non-ctx receiver (Siege's `MultiMeshScatter.setup(mesh, capacity)`, a 3D-kit API in GAME_SDK 18, and every game view's own `setup` would fail the gate, which games can't change); flag a bare "setup" string too (a common state name; a dynamic call needs a rider reference, which the other rules guard). *Reason:* a typed GDScript variable can't hold an object of an unrelated class at run time, so a typed receiver is provably not the rider, and the static-typing rule (BRIEF 6) already asks games to type their variables.
- **2026-10-21 (W4r2, NET-2): `resume_phase(index, elapsed_s[, phase_distance_m])` enters a running phase.** Decision: additive `SafetyGovernor.resume_phase` does what `start_phase` does (directive channels revert to the plan, a "game" course grade waits for the game, a SIM phase ends rescue) but starts the phase clock at `elapsed_s`, so a ramp continues at the host's point. A "course" phase follows its profile from the distance already ridden in it: the caller's `phase_distance_m` when it knows it, else the governor's own record (one (game t, distance) entry per game second from `set_course_distance`, six hours at most; `distance_ridden_since(game_t)`), else 0. Every 4.6 counter keeps its state: the connection's warm-up (the skipped elapsed time never counts toward its 300 s), the 105% / 120% times, latches and reset run, the switch spacing, the haptic cooldown, coasting and rescue arming; the long-phase cap and every ceiling apply as for any phase. A non-finite or negative `elapsed_s` is a push_error and changes nothing. *Alternatives:* `start_phase(index, elapsed_s)` (changes a frozen signature); fast-forwarding the governor through the missed time (it would count the rider's absent minutes toward the duration limits and the warm-up). *Reason:* BRIEF 3.6, a rejoin resumes the phase; 4.6 counts what the trainer applied, which a resume doesn't change.
- **2026-10-21 (W4r2, SAF-1 range): an FTP below 30 W gives 30 W, never the previous value.** Decision: `configure({ftp_w})` below `GovernorClamps.FTP_MIN_W` (zero, negative, -inf) push_errors and uses 30 W; above `FTP_MAX_W`, +inf or NaN it still push_errors and keeps the previous value (200 W for a new governor). Supersedes the W4 line "rejected ... the previous value stays" for low values. *Alternatives:* keep the previous value (W4r2: a 25 W profile FTP rode 100 % at 200 W); clamp both ways (a 5000 W FTP would become a 2000 W basis with a 600 W floor, where the previous value is always in range and lower). *Reason:* every FTP-relative rule scales with the FTP, so the protective reading of an invalid FTP is the lower of the two candidates; WlSettings now also keeps the profile in range on every save (docs/decisions/platform.md).
- **2026-10-21 (W4 final, BRIEF 3.5): One shared not_pedaling detector; the stale-sample watchdog is a stricter governor rule on top of it.** Decision: `GestureRecognizer.is_not_pedaling(power_w, cadence_rpm)` (power < 15 W and cadence < 20 rpm, both strict; -1 for an absent cadence, which counts as low) is the predicate the governor and the GestureRecognizer share, and `GestureRecognizer.NOT_PEDALING_W` / `NOT_PEDALING_RPM` are the only copy of 15 W and 20 rpm in the governor, `CadenceEstimator` and the GestureRecognizer. Not yet the only one in production code: `GameHost`'s rejoin "shows pedaling" check (`PEDAL_POWER_W`, `PEDAL_CADENCE_RPM` and the inline `power >= 15 or cadence >= 20` in `addons/wattlands/sdk/game_host.gd`, owned by sdk-runtime) still writes both numbers. It gives the same answer as `not is_not_pedaling(...)` for every number (`Telemetry.cadence_rpm` defaults to 0), a parity test in `tests/governor/test_coasting.gd` fails if its constants drift, and calling `not GestureRecognizer.is_not_pedaling(tm.power_w, tm.cadence_rpm)` there is requested from the lead; once that lands, the shared constants are the only place. `SafetyGovernor._take_sample` calls it (coasting 4.6.4, the 10 s pause/AFK, the warm-up's pedal strokes) instead of its own comparison; `SafetyGovernor.NOT_PEDALING_W` / `NOT_PEDALING_RPM` and `CadenceEstimator.COAST_POWER_W` stay as aliases of the shared constants (frozen API, no number of their own). Behaviour is unchanged: the old copy gave the same answer for every input (`not has_cad` is now cadence -1). The stale-sample watchdog (SAF-7) is unchanged and stays a separate, stricter governor-only rule layered on the shared detector: its synthetic 0 W, no-cadence sample goes through the same predicate, but only the governor sees it, so with the link up a dead stream still coasts, pauses or marks AFK and feeds the rescue power trigger, while coast and stop_5s never fire from samples that did not arrive. The rescue power trigger still compares against `TrainerController.written_target_w()`, the target on the trainer, which equals the applied target once its 0.5 s write slot has passed. *Alternatives:* a new platform class holding the predicate (one more class for a single static, where BRIEF 3.5 already puts the detector in the GestureRecognizer); keep both copies plus a parity test (the drift risk the DoD sweep found; it stays only as GameHost's stop-gap); change GameHost from this branch (sdk-runtime's file, which only its owner or the lead edits); feed the watchdog's sample to the GestureRecognizer too (a dropped stream would fire "back" and "pause" gestures; the governor's own pause/AFK already covers a stopped rider). *Reason:* BRIEF 3.5 asks for one detector that drives coast, stop_5s and 4.6.4. The trainer layer already uses sim classes (MockTrainer uses SpeedModel and SeededRng), so the dependency direction is not new. Tests pin every edge: exactly 15 W or exactly 20 rpm is pedaling, and 14.99 W with 19.99 rpm (or no cadence) coasts at 3 s and pauses at 10 s, in the governor and the recognizer, which agree on an edge grid; exactly 45 rpm never triggers rescue and 44.9 rpm does at 3 s; exactly 70 rpm ends the hold at 3 s and 69.9 rpm never does; exactly 75 % of the target never triggers and 119.99 W or 119 W of 160 W does at 5 s; a target of exactly 60 % FTP arms rescue (already pinned).
- **2026-10-21 (W4 final, lead): GameHost's rejoin check uses the shared detector.** Decision: `GameHost` drops `PEDAL_POWER_W` and `PEDAL_CADENCE_RPM`; its rejoin "shows pedaling" check is `not GestureRecognizer.is_not_pedaling(tm.power_w, tm.cadence_rpm)`, the exact negation of the old `power >= 15 or cadence >= 20`, so behaviour is unchanged. `tests/governor/test_coasting.gd` now asserts GameHost keeps no thresholds of its own. Alternatives: keep the pinned copy. Reason: BRIEF 3.5 asks for one shared detector, and a copy pinned by a test is still a copy.

### sim

Short ADRs for `addons/wattlands/sim/` (BRIEF 3.3-3.7, 3.11, 4.6.1, 4.6.8).

- **2026-10-21: "For N s" in gestures is timed from the first sample of the stretch.** pedal_start (3 s), coast (3 s) and stop_5s (5 s) fire on the first sample at least N s after the stretch's first sample; the optional `advance(t)` fires them on time between samples, holding the latest sample's state for at most 2.5 s. *Alternatives:* counting samples; timing from the latest sample only. *Reason:* the governor's coasting rule (4.6.4, BRIEF 7.4) is timed the same way, so the gesture and the governor agree; `advance` keeps the ~1 Hz jitter out of the timing for callers that tick.
- **2026-10-21: A surge is two consecutive qualifying samples against a frozen 10 s baseline.** "For 1.5 s" means two consecutive ~1 Hz samples at least 0.5 s apart (a duplicate notification never counts twice). Both compare against the mean of the samples in the 10 s before the streak's first sample (power outside ERG, flywheel speed in ERG), so the surge never raises its own bar. One surge per streak; a sample that doesn't qualify re-arms it. *Alternatives:* a rolling average that includes the surge (a marginal 1.3 x surge then fails on its second sample); a time-weighted average. *Reason:* the threshold the brief states holds for the whole 1.5 s, and the sample mean equals the time-weighted mean at ~1 Hz.
- **2026-10-21: Starting to pedal is never a surge.** The surge baseline counts only samples after the latest not_pedaling sample and needs 5 s of that history, and a surge needs pedal_start to have fired since the last stop. *Alternatives:* include stopped samples (a restart from 0 W then looks like +40 W); no minimum. *Reason:* pedal-scan menus would otherwise pick on every restart.
- **2026-10-21: coast and stop_5s are used up per stop where they aren't allowed.** A stop that reaches 3 s (5 s) during ERG (or, for stop_5s, in multiplayer) never fires that gesture later in the same stop, even if ERG ends. *Alternative:* fire as soon as the stop is outside ERG. *Reason:* a "back" arriving seconds after a phase change would surprise the rider; the arbitration in the W0 stub says each fires once, at its time, where allowed.
- **2026-10-21: not_pedaling is a state, not a fired gesture.** `not_pedaling()`, `not_pedaling_s()`, `not_pedaling_s_at(t)`, `not_pedaling_since()` and the `not_pedaling_changed` signal expose it; `on_sample` only returns `Wl.GESTURES`. *Reason:* GM8 lists the gestures games get as pedal_start, surge, coast and stop_5s; not_pedaling feeds the governor and coast/stop_5s.
- **2026-10-21: SprintRule treats each update as the power since the previous one.** A 1 Hz sample at t covers (t - 1, t]; `held_s()` runs from the start of the first holding update's interval; with a window, `holds()` needs updates covering the whole window and compares its time-weighted average. *Alternatives:* forward hold; held_s from the first holding update. *Reason:* the same reading serves sim-step feeds and 1 Hz samples, and "over the last 3 s" (Tank's Ram) then means exactly 3 s of data.
- **2026-10-21: The importer keeps a .zwo's own warm-up.** A prefix (300 s ERG 50 -> 65%) goes first unless the first block is a Warmup or every ERG phase starting in the first 300 s already stays <= 65%. An ERG phase that starts inside 300 s and has a value above 65% is split at 300 s (the second part continues the lap), then the inside part is clamped to 65%. *Alternatives:* always prefix (two warm-ups); clamp the whole crossing block to 65% (a 10-minute warm-up ramp loses its top half). *Reason:* riders import workouts with warm-ups; the split keeps the file's ramp where 4.6.8 allows it, and the PlanValidator decides warm-up membership by phase start.
- **2026-10-21: Short ERG/SIM runs are extended to 30 s.** Any run of SIM (or ERG) phases between two switches shorter than 30 s gets its last phase lengthened to 30 s; the appended ERG cool-down counts as a switch after a final SIM run. *Alternatives:* merge a short FreeRide into its ERG neighbours or convert a short ERG gap to SIM. *Reason:* extending keeps every block's mode and target and changes the session length by seconds; the PlanValidator then has no spacing warning to report.
- **2026-10-21: Duration limits are checked per phase by its peak, and the whole offending phase is clamped.** A phase whose peak is above 120% (105%) accrues its full length; >= 120 s of consecutive ERG phases with peaks <= 90% reset both counts (inclusive); a SIM phase restarts that count. A phase that would push the 120% count past 60 s is clamped to 120%, then one that would push the 105% count past 300 s to 105%; exactly 60 s and 300 s are allowed. *Alternatives:* split the offending block at the limit; integrate ramps exactly. *Reason:* it is never less strict than the PlanValidator's reading (PL17: accrued at the phase's range top), so an imported plan is never rejected, and "clamp the offending block" is what the session preview lists.
- **2026-10-21: Rejected .zwo files use push_warning.** `parse_zwo` returns `ok: false` with `error` for empty, non-XML, wrongly rooted, truncated or blockless files and logs it with `push_warning`. *Alternative:* `push_error`. *Reason:* a .zwo is user content, so a bad one is an expected, non-fatal condition like a refused directive; push_error would make every game test that imports a bad file need `assert_push_error`.
- **2026-10-21: Phase kinds come from the file's values.** Kinds follow the tag and the block's own power (SteadyState >= 76% WORK, Ramp by its upper end), before any clamp, so a block clamped in the warm-up keeps its kind. *Reason:* the kind describes the coach's intent; the target shows the clamp.
- **2026-10-21: Importer text is translated from content/strings/sim.csv.** Phase labels ("Steady 88%", "Interval 2/5 110%"), clamp lines and warnings are built from `SIM_ZWO_*` keys with `{named}` placeholders at parse time; labels show the clamped values. *Reason:* labels are plain text the HUD and FIT laps show as-is (PL11), and platform strings live in the module's CSV.
- **2026-10-21: XML details.** Godot 4.7's XMLParser decodes entities (named and numeric) in text and attribute values itself and keeps CDATA content in `get_node_name()` (`get_node_data()` errors on CDATA), so the importer adds no decoding. Tag and attribute names match case-insensitively; a power above 5.0 is read as a percentage with a warning; IntervalsT without OnPower/OffPower uses the mean of PowerOnLow/PowerOnHigh (PowerOffLow/PowerOffHigh) with a warning.
- **2026-10-21: A part split off at 300 s keeps its block's length for the long-phase cap.** When the warm-up split cuts a block longer than 300 s into a part inside the warm-up and a part after it, the 105% cap for phases longer than 300 s still applies to the second part, even if that part alone is 300 s or shorter. *Alternative:* judge each part by its own length (the PlanValidator would accept that too). *Reason:* the split exists only to keep a file's warm-up within 4.6.8; it must not lift the cap the block had as written ("the 105% cap for blocks longer than 300 s").
- **2026-10-21: Guards against malformed .zwo files.** A leading byte-order mark is ignored; an IntervalsT `Repeat` above 200 is limited to 200 with a warning; a workout whose parsed blocks add up to more than 24 h is rejected (`ok: false`); a missing file returns the full result shape with a translated error and the same `push_warning` as any rejected file. *Alternatives:* no limits; skipping the oversized block. *Reason:* a .zwo is user content, and a typo like `Repeat="10000"` would otherwise build tens of thousands of phases on the host and on every client.
- **2026-10-21: Importer and gesture tests include seeded property sweeps.** 300 random .zwo files must import, satisfy an independent reading of the validator's rules, never raise any block's target, keep laps and import identically twice; 120 random gesture streams in each ERG/solo combination must fire only what BRIEF 3.5 allows, and every stop exactly what it should. A PlanValidator test over the fixtures and 100 random files is `pending` while PlanValidator is the W0 stub, and becomes a real check (no error, no warning, only the cool-down appended) once sdk-runtime's validator is merged. *Reason:* the fixtures pin each rule; the sweeps catch interactions between the importer's passes and between gesture rules.
- **2026-10-21: .zwo durations are whole seconds.** `Duration`, `OnDuration` and `OffDuration` are rounded to the nearest second; one that rounds to 0 makes the block invalid (skipped with a warning), and an `OffDuration` that rounds to 0 gives on phases only. *Alternative:* keep fractions. *Reason:* Zwift writes whole seconds, converters leave artefacts like 29.999999 that would otherwise trigger a spurious 30 s spacing clamp, and a sub-tick phase (under 0.05 s) would end inside a single sim step.
- **2026-10-21 (W4, ZIH-2): The surge baseline counts from pedal_start.** The 10 s average a surge compares against counts only samples from the moment pedal_start fired and from the start of the current unbroken pedal_start-condition streak (a soft-pedal dip below 40 rpm and 30% FTP restarts it), and still needs `SURGE_MIN_HISTORY_S` (5 s) of that history. *Alternatives:* keep counting from the last stop but wait 5 s after pedal_start (a first-order spin-up with tau 3 s still surged: its low ramp samples stay in the window); require a full 10 s window after pedal_start (every surge 13 s after starting). *Reason:* BRIEF 3.5 "surge picks" in every pedal-scan menu, so starting to pedal must never pick; W3 saw the app's first-run title pick "Ride now" 7 s after a plain start, and 9 of 20 seeded mock riders surged from rest. Two gesture tests moved their late surges accordingly (the second start's surge at 38 s instead of 34 s; a 320 W streak where the 300 W pair after pedal_start now sits in the baseline).
- **2026-10-21 (W4, QT-6): `WorkoutImport` is `ZwoImport`.** Platform classes never carry a game prefix (BRIEF 6, CLAUDE.md), and Workout owns the Workout prefix; the file keeps the path BRIEF 3.7 names (`sim/workout_import.gd`). `tests/arch/test_class_prefixes.gd` fails on any class_name under `addons/wattlands`, `src/app` or `tools` that starts with Workout, Siege, Tank, Fishing (either case) or Template. The Workout game kept a deprecated `WorkoutImport` alias (statics inherited) only until qa's `tests/scenarios/workout/test_zwo_import.gd` used `ZwoImport`; both changes landed in W4 (1835250), so no alias is left and every caller names `ZwoImport`.
- **2026-10-21 (W4 final, DoD 3.6): Bots follow a published cadence band.** `Bots.RIDER_SPEC` is `"erg:compliance=1.0+band"`: the erg follower at compliance 1.0 plus MockTrainer's `band` cadence policy at offset 0. A bot's cadence goes to the centre of the band the game publishes for it (`ctx.directives.cadence_band`) after the 2 s lag, with seeded noise within ±1.5 rpm, and rides its preferred 90 rpm while no band is published or after it is cleared. Every bot is built from the constant (the app's `AppSessionLauncher.make_bot_rider` and `run_game_sim`'s GameSimRunner; e2e runs have no bots), and the noise stream derives from each bot's mock seed, which derives from the session seed, so the same seed still gives the same `deterministic_hash`. Power and trainer load are unchanged (a band never changes load). Side effects, all within the BRIEF: without a band a bot's cadence carries the ±1.5 rpm noise (88.5-91.5 rpm instead of exactly 90); its cadence no longer rises 10% in an outside-ERG sprint (the band policy steers it); `run_game_sim` hashes for runs with bots differ from earlier builds; and in ERG, a band step up of more than about 12% makes a bot fire the BRIEF 3.5 `surge` gesture, as a human following the band would. In ERG the surge compares flywheel speed (cadence times a fixed gear) with 1.12 x its 10 s average, and the 2 s lag also lifts that average, so in practice the step must be larger: over 40 seeds a bot never surged on a step up to 16%, surged on 15 seeds at 18% and on nearly all at 20% or more (no band to 100-120 rpm is a 22% step). No game in the repo publishes such a step for a bot today (Workout publishes no cadence band; the template's 80-100 rpm band is centred on 90), but a game that does and reads a bot's gestures in `frame.gestures` gets that surge. *Alternatives:* a bot-only MockTrainer option that follows the band without noise (a change to trainer-core's frozen surface for a cosmetic difference); leaving bots at 90 rpm and changing the docs (BRIEF 3.6 names bots as followers, and a band that excludes 90 rpm, such as 100-120, left every bot out of band). *Reason:* W4 DoD sweep, BRIEF 3.6 `cadence_band` ("bots and MockTrainer's band-tracking cadence policy follow it").

### ble

- 2026-10-21. Decision: `GdbleTransport` and `BridgeTransport` refuse every write while `WATTLANDS_NO_HW_CONTROL=1` (push_warning "hardware control locked", `operation_failed("write", ...)`), and the Python bridge refuses `write` under the same variable in its own environment. Alternatives: rely on `FtmsClient`'s lock alone. Reason: defence in depth for BRIEF 1. Under the lock `FtmsClient` never writes anyway, so nothing changes for the app; a bug above the transport still can't reach a real trainer. Scan, read and subscribe stay allowed.
- 2026-10-21. Decision: backend choice skips GDBLE only when its library isn't loaded; when GDBLE loads but reports no adapter, the bridge is tried next; the bridge is always tried, because its reachability is known only after `initialize()`. With neither, the status is `STATUS_DISCONNECTED` with the translated detail "No Bluetooth adapter found" (GDBLE loaded, no adapter) or "Bluetooth is unavailable ..." (nothing loaded or reachable), plus push_warning. Alternatives: `STATUS_ERROR`; stop after GDBLE's answer. Reason: the stub says no adapter is never an error; a PC without Bluetooth is a normal setup that plays with the mock trainer, and the bridge may work where btleplug doesn't.
- 2026-10-21. Decision: one backoff schedule for every failed link: `backoff_delay(n)` = 1, 2, 4, 8, then 15 s, for a lost link, a failed connect, a connect that takes over 30 s (the bridge times out its own connect at 25 s, so the transport normally answers first) and a setup (FtmsClient `begin()`) that fails or takes over 30 s. It resets when the trainer is ready again. Timers advance only in `tick(dt)`; `_process(delta)` calls it unless `manual_clock` is set. Alternatives: retry only after a loss; separate first-connect logic. Reason: BRIEF 4.4 gives one schedule; a KICKR's first connect on Windows sometimes fails, and a single state machine is easier to test.
- 2026-10-21. Decision: a reconnect reuses the same `FtmsClient` (attached once per transport) and the same `FtmsTrainer` (created once per trainer address), runs `FtmsClient.begin()` again (rediscover, resubscribe, indications, Request Control, SIM flat), then emits `trainer_ready(device)` followed by the additive `reconnected(device)`. A GATT reset (GDBLE `EVENT_OVERFLOW` on notifications or `SERVICES_CHANGED`) does the same without dropping the link. Alternatives: a new device per reconnect. Reason: the governor keeps its device reference, and `reconnected` is the one hook it needs to restore its last safe mode (begin() leaves the trainer in SIM flat).
- 2026-10-21. Decision: `connect_to()` for the trainer that is already linked does nothing when it is ready or setting up, and runs `begin()` again after a control loss or a failed setup; both transports answer `connect_device()` for the device they already hold with another `device_connected` instead of a second connect. Alternatives: always reconnect. Reason: GDBLE answers a second connect with `already_connected` and may not repeat its `connected` signal, which would leave the manager waiting for a timeout.
- 2026-10-21. Decision: control lost (Request Control 0x05 or Machine Status 0xFF) sets `STATUS_CONTROL_LOST` with "Another app may hold control of the trainer (Zwift, the Wahoo app). Close it, then retry.", emits the additive `control_lost(detail)` and never retries on its own; `retry_control()` runs `begin()` again when asked. Alternatives: retry with the backoff. Reason: grabbing control back every few seconds from a Zwift session the rider started on purpose would fight the other app and could change resistance under the rider.
- 2026-10-21. Decision: under the hardware lock, a setup failure keeps the read-only link (`STATUS_ERROR`, "Trainer setup failed (...). Hardware control is locked (read-only).") and stops; without the lock it drops the link and retries with backoff. Alternatives: retry in both cases. Reason: under the lock Request Control is refused by design, so a retry loop would never end.
- 2026-10-21. Decision: GDBLE events. Device signals and the manager's `device_connected`/`device_disconnected` are both connected and deduplicated, so each link change is reported exactly once (`reason` "local" after `disconnect_device()`, else "remote"; a disconnect before the link came up is a failed connect). `ble_event` is used only for unsolicited events: a non-terminal initialize failure (adapter powered off) becomes `adapter_ready(false, code)`, and the manager then re-runs `initialize()` before its next attempt. Every `start_scan()` re-announces GDBLE's discovery cache, because GDBLE deduplicates discovery over the adapter's lifetime. Alternatives: the structured events only; the legacy signals only. Reason: API_CONTRACT.md recommends device events for device work, and the legacy signals carry BRIEF 4.2's 0.5.x surface; the cache replay keeps a rescan useful.
- 2026-10-21. Decision: GDBLE device handles are held as `RefCounted` and called only through `call(&"...")`; signals are connected and disconnected through `Signal(obj, name)`. The test double's zero-argument `disconnect()` overrides `Object.disconnect` with default arguments under `@warning_ignore("native_method_override")`. Alternatives: untyped variables; a different method name in tests. Reason: `docs/deps.d/gdble.md` (the name clash); `Signal(...).disconnect` reaches Object's own method even on a fake that defines `disconnect()`.
- 2026-10-21. Decision: the bridge protocol is JSON text frames with integer request ids, `{ok, result, error}` responses, `{event}` messages and lower-case hex payloads; UUIDs expanded and lower case on the wire. The bridge binds to 127.0.0.1 only, serves one client at a time, runs requests in order (scans in the background) and releases the trainer when the app disconnects. `BridgeTransport` accepts loopback URLs only and has open (3 s) and request (10 s, connect 25 s) timeouts on `tick(dt)`. Alternatives: binary frames; base64. Reason: readable logs, trivial on both sides (`PackedByteArray.hex_encode`, `bytes.hex`), and nothing ever leaves the machine.
- 2026-10-21. Decision: the probe and the bridge tests use their own Python FTMS parsers and `tools/trainer_bridge/tests/vectors_local.json`, transcribed from BRIEF 4.3, until trainer-core's `tests/vectors/ftms.json` lands; a tolerant reader then checks every Indoor Bike Data case in that file whose field names it can map. The bridge itself forwards raw bytes and never parses. Alternatives: wait for the shared file. Reason: W1 builders run in parallel; the GDScript `FtmsCodec` stays the codec the app uses.
- 2026-10-21. Decision: `bleak` is imported lazily and only by `BleakBackend`; the server, the probe and the parsers are tested against a fake backend. Here bleak could not be installed: Python 3.15.0 is the only interpreter, the `winrt-*` wheels bleak 3.0.2 needs don't exist for it, and building them needs Microsoft C++ Build Tools (BRIEF 2: treat Python as absent for those parts). websockets 17.3 and PyInstaller 6.22.3 installed fine. The probe never ran against hardware in this run. Alternatives: download another Python (BRIEF 1 forbids it). Reason: the proxy, the protocol and the probe's safety rules are still fully tested; only the bleak calls themselves are unverified.
- 2026-10-21. Decision: the app doesn't start the bridge itself yet; a user starts `trainer_bridge serve` (or the PyInstaller exe) and the manager finds it on its next `start()`. Alternatives: `OS.create_process` from the manager when GDBLE has no adapter. Reason: P1 asks for the bridge and the spec; launching a child process belongs with app settings (a path to the exe and an opt-in), so it is a backlog note.
- 2026-10-21. Decision: status details are translated text from `content/strings/ble.csv` (`BLE_STATUS_*`, filled with `String.format`), and JSON from the bridge or the last-device file is parsed with `JSON.new().parse()`. Alternatives: English literals; `JSON.parse_string()`. Reason: BRIEF 6 strings rule; `parse_string` logs an engine error on bad input, which GUT counts as a failure and which bad input from an external process shouldn't cause.
- 2026-10-21 (hardening review). Decision: `FtmsConnectionManager` treats a backend that doesn't answer `initialize()` within `INIT_TIMEOUT_S` (10 s) as unavailable and tries the next one; `connect_to()` during start-up waits for the backend instead of initializing it twice; an adapter lost while no trainer is linked shows "No Bluetooth adapter found", and `scan()` or `start()` initializes it again before scanning. Alternatives: rely on GDBLE always answering; require a full `start()` after a loss. Reason: without these, a connect requested during start-up stalled for 30 s, a hung backend left the status at "Starting Bluetooth" forever, and a lost idle adapter could only be recovered by restarting the app.
- 2026-10-21 (hardening review). Decision: while auto-connect waits for the remembered trainer and nothing is linked, scans repeat in `AUTO_SCAN_S` windows; `stop_scan()` and `disconnect_trainer()` end the search until the next `start(true)`. Alternatives: one 30 s scan at launch (the W1 build). Reason: BRIEF 3.5's zero-input start; a trainer switched on after the app was otherwise never connected without someone pressing scan.
- 2026-10-21 (hardening review). Decision: `GdbleTransport` reports a live link down (`device_disconnected(address, "adapter lost")`) before `adapter_ready(false, ...)` when the adapter goes away. Alternatives: wait for GDBLE's own disconnect events. Reason: GDBLE cancels device work on power-off but need not report the link; the transport then believed it was still linked and the reconnect took the "already linked" shortcut without calling `connect_async()`.
- 2026-10-21 (hardening review). Decision: a GATT reset (`SERVICES_CHANGED`, notification overflow) runs `FtmsClient.begin()` only on a ready trainer, not after a control loss. Alternatives: also after a control loss (the W1 build). Reason: `begin()` requests control, so it would take control back from the other app, which the control-loss ADR rules out; `retry_control()` rediscovers and resubscribes when the rider asks.
- 2026-10-21 (hardening review). Decision: `BridgeTransport.disconnect_device()` also cancels a connect in flight (its late answer is ignored), and a connect that times out sends a `disconnect` so the bridge releases a link that completes late; the bridge answers `connect` for the trainer it already holds without opening a second link, and releases the old link before connecting a different trainer. Alternatives: leave late links to the next connect. Reason: the bridge runs requests in order, so a late link otherwise stayed open on the bridge while the app retried, and a second `BleakClient` to the same trainer leaked the first.
- 2026-10-21 (W4, ftms-ble-1). Decision: `FtmsClient.failed` while the link is READY or CONTROL_LOST (a control-point write unanswered after its two retries: "the link is presumed dead") drops the link, backs off (1 s first), reconnects and runs `begin()` again, then emits `trainer_ready` + `reconnected`, so the governor restores its last safe mode (BRIEF 4.4). It does so under the hardware lock too: a ready client there is read-only and never writes, so this failure can't come from the lock (a setup failure under the lock still keeps the read-only link). Alternatives: `retry_control()` (keeps a link that stopped answering); let `FtmsClient` drop the link itself (the manager owns the link). Reason: W3 found the stack stuck READY with every write dropped, so panic (4.6.5), rescue and the exit SIM flat + Stop (4.6.9) never reached the trainer.
- 2026-10-21 (W4, ftms-ble-3). Decision: every scan the manager starts after an earlier one first calls `transport.stop_scan()` (tracked by `_scan_issued`; a stop resets it). GDBLE's `stop_scan()` cancels every unfinished scan request and is harmless when none runs. Alternatives: one untimed `start_scan(0)` for the whole auto-connect search (changes the windows the status text and tests rely on). Reason: GDBLE refuses a duplicate start with BUSY, and the manager's window and GDBLE's scan timer are two clocks, so a restart at the window's end could lose the next 30 s window (BRIEF 3.5 zero-input pickup). Hardware check: on a real adapter, auto-connect picks up a trainer switched on 60-90 s after launch with no BUSY in the log.
- 2026-10-21 (W4, SAF-8). Decision: `connect_to()` another address while a trainer is linked releases the old `FtmsTrainer` from the shared `FtmsClient` (`FtmsTrainer.release_client()`) before the link drops, and `_on_client_ready` does the same for a replaced device before `trainer_ready`; linking a released trainer again makes a new `FtmsTrainer`. `linked_address()` (additive) lets `WlTrainerService` tell a switch from a reconnect. Reason: one client serves every trainer, so the old rider's shutdown writes reached the NEW trainer (a Stop right after it became ready). Making the old trainer safe before the switch is `WlTrainerService`'s job (docs/decisions/platform.md).

### relay

Short ADRs for the multiplayer relay (BRIEF 5.3). Date for all of them: 2026-10-21.

- **The protocol core is socket-free, and two WebSocket layers sit under it.** `src/relay.ts` gets a `RelayConn` per socket and is fed text. `src/ws_package.ts` (the `ws` package) and `src/ws_builtin.ts` (an RFC 6455 server on `node:http` upgrade and `node:crypto`) only frame bytes. `RELAY_WS_IMPL=auto` (the default) tries `import('ws')` and falls back to the built-in server. `ws` and `builtin` force a choice, and `ws` fails if the package is missing. The server checks the handshake (`src/handshake.ts`) before it hands the socket to either layer, so both refuse bad upgrades the same way (405, 400, 426 with `Sec-WebSocket-Version: 13`, 404). The `ws` layer is set to match the built-in one: `maxPayload` 16384, no permessage-deflate, no subprotocol. *Alternatives:* the built-in server only, or the protocol logic written twice. *Reason:* BRIEF prefers `ws` and requires a zero-dependency fallback. One core lets the same protocol and frame suites prove both layers.
- **Every time rule runs on an injected clock, applied by `sweep()`.** The idle timeout, the token bucket, the join window, reconnect-token expiry, the host hold and the room lifetime all read `Clock.now()`, which is monotonic `performance.now()` in production. `startRelay()` calls `relay.sweep()` every second. Tests use a `FakeClock` with `sweepMs: 0` and sweep by hand, so the 60 s, 120 s and 4 h cases run in milliseconds and are deterministic. A message that arrives for an expired seat or room sweeps that room first, so nothing depends on sweep timing.
- **`hello` comes first.** `create`, `join` and `msg` before `hello` get `bad_request` ("send hello first"); `ping` is allowed at any time. `hello` has no reply. A `proto` other than 1 gets `error {code: "version_mismatch", proto: 1}`, and the socket closes with 4002. *Alternative:* assume protocol 1 when `hello` is missing. *Reason:* the version check only works if every client sends its version, and BRIEF's protocol lists `hello` first.
- **A malformed message is answered, not fatal.** Invalid JSON, a non-object, a missing or unknown `t` (including server-only types like `created`), a missing or mistyped field, or a binary message gets `error {code: "bad_request", detail, re}`, and the connection stays open. Only WebSocket-level faults close the socket: 1002 for a protocol error, 1007 for invalid UTF-8 and 1009 for oversize. *Reason:* a client bug shouldn't drop a rider from a session, and `detail` and `re` make the bug easy to find.
- **The 16 KB limit is enforced in the WebSocket layer, as close code 1009.** It applies to the whole message across fragments. The built-in server checks it from the frame header before buffering the payload. *Alternative:* accept larger frames and answer with `bad_request`. *Reason:* that's standard WebSocket behaviour (`ws` `maxPayload`), it bounds memory per socket, and NetSession never sends more than 16 384 bytes (OWNERSHIP C9), so an oversize message is a client bug.
- **The rate limit is a token bucket of 60 a second, burst 120, per connection, over every message.** That includes `ping`, `hello` and `join`. It is checked before JSON parsing, so floods are cheap to drop. Each dropped message is counted, and at most one `rate_limited` error goes out per connection per second. *Alternative:* an error per dropped message. *Reason:* an error per drop would double a flood back at a misbehaving client.
- **The join limit is a sliding window of 10 attempts a minute per IP.** Only attempts that pass validation count, and refused attempts don't, so a client retrying in a loop gets back in after a minute. Behind Fly or Render, `RELAY_TRUST_PROXY=1` reads `Fly-Client-IP` or the first `X-Forwarded-For` entry. Without it the socket address is used, because forwarded headers can be forged.
- **Seats, peer ids and held seats.** The host is peer 1, and peer ids grow and are never reused in a room. A dropped peer's seat is held 120 s and counts toward `max`. `create.max` is capped at 8, and `created.max` reports the cap. On a drop, every other connected peer gets `peer_left` with reason `dropped`. An explicit leave sends reason `left` and frees the seat at once. Seat expiry sends nothing: the peer already got `peer_left`, and NetSession leaves the slot AFK (OWNERSHIP NET6).
- **A seat keeps one reconnect token until it is freed, and a reconnect may replace a live socket.** (Revised in the hardening review; the first version rotated the token on every reconnect.) A valid token returns the same peer id and the same token (`joined.rejoin: true`), and every other peer gets `peer_joined {rejoin: true}`. The 120 s hold restarts at every drop, and expiry is checked when a `join` arrives, not only by the sweep. If the old socket still looks open (a killed client whose TCP connection the relay hasn't noticed), the new socket takes over: the old one closes with 4001, and the others get `peer_left` (`dropped`) and then `peer_joined` (`rejoin`), the same transitions as a drop. An unknown or expired token joins as a new peer instead of failing, and the host then decides (`session_in_progress`). *Alternatives:* rotate the token on every reconnect, or refuse a token while its socket is open. *Reason:* with rotation, a reconnect whose `joined` reply is lost (the network drops again, which is exactly when reconnects happen, or the app dies before it saves the new token) leaves the client holding a dead token, so it comes back as a new peer and loses its slot for the rest of the session. A stable token makes every reconnect retryable, matches BRIEF 5.3 ("a reconnect token stays valid for 120 s and keeps the same peer id; clients save it in their profile dir") and works whether NetSession saves the token once or after every `joined`. Rotation's gain, a leaked token working only once, is small: tokens go only to their owner, over `wss` in production, and are never logged. Takeover makes the e2e restart (BRIEF 7.7) work even when the old socket lingers. A client closed with 4001 should not reconnect by itself, or two copies of one profile would take the seat from each other in turn.
- **The slow-consumer limit is also checked on every frame received.** Both WebSocket layers answer WebSocket pings themselves, outside the relay's rate limit, so a client that floods pings and never reads would otherwise grow its socket's backlog without bound. On every incoming frame the relay closes a connection whose unsent output is over 1 MiB (4003, a drop), the same rule it applies before each send. *Alternative:* count WebSocket pings in the message rate limit. *Reason:* BRIEF's 60 a second counts messages, and the backlog rule bounds memory directly in both layers with one check in the core.
- **`node src/main.ts` refuses unknown options, and a blank `PORT` means the default.** Options are parsed by a pure function (`src/options.ts`) with unit tests. Flags are `--name=value` and win over the environment. A typo such as `--prot=0` or `--port 0` exits with code 1 and the usage text instead of silently binding port 8787. A blank `PORT` or `HOST` (for example `PORT=` in a shell or compose file) counts as unset, where `Number("")` used to turn it into port 0, a random port.
- **The host drop and `host_left`.** A host drop sends `peer_left {peer: 1, reason: "dropped"}`, and the room is held 120 s. Messages to the host are dropped while it's away, and new peers may still join. `host_left` carries a `reason`: `left` (explicit leave), `timeout` (the host's token expired) or `expired` (the 4 h lifetime; the host gets it too). After `host_left`, peers stay connected outside any room and may create or join again on the same socket.
- **`joined.peers` lists the connected peers.** A host that reconnects after a drop learns who is still there, including peers that joined while it was away, whose `peer_joined` it missed. Nothing sent while a peer was away is replayed; NetSession resends state on `rejoin` (OWNERSHIP NET6).
- **`d` is forwarded byte for byte.** After `JSON.parse` validates the message, `src/json_span.ts` finds the raw text of the top-level `d`, and the relay sends `{"t":"msg","from":N,"d":<raw>}`. *Alternative:* `JSON.stringify(parsed.d)`. *Reason:* "never looks inside d" also means "never rewrites it". Integers above 2^53 (64-bit seeds) and spellings like `1.0` survive. It's also cheaper than re-encoding a 16 KB snapshot ten times a second.
- **Non-hosts may only address the host.** A client's `msg` with `to` other than 1 (including `to: 0`) gets `bad_request` and is dropped. A `msg` to a peer that isn't connected, or to the sender itself, is dropped silently, because a peer leaving is a normal race.
- **Slow consumers are closed.** A connection with more than 1 MiB of unsent output is closed with 4003 and treated as a drop. *Reason:* one stalled client must not grow the relay's memory without bound while the host streams 10 Hz snapshots.
