- **Logging and `/health`.** Logs carry only room serial numbers and counts, never codes, tokens or payloads (a unit test checks this). `/health` returns `{status, proto, impl, uptime_s, rooms, connections, peers, held}`, with 503 `closing` during shutdown. A shutdown closes every socket with 1001. The entry prints `WATTLANDS_RELAY <ws url>` once it listens, so harnesses can start it on `PORT=0`. It binds all interfaces by default (dual stack, so `ws://localhost` works whether localhost resolves to ::1 or 127.0.0.1).
- **TypeScript without a toolchain.** The sources use erasable syntax only (no enums, namespaces or parameter properties; type imports use `import type`), with explicit `.ts` import extensions, so Node runs them directly. `package.json` scripts pass `--experimental-strip-types` for Node 22.12–22.17. `tsconfig.json` is for editors. A scratch check with TypeScript 6.0.3, `@types/node` 26.6.4 and `@types/ws` 8.18.2 (`strict`, `erasableSyntaxOnly`, `verbatimModuleSyntax`) passed with no errors. TypeScript isn't a dependency.
- **Tests are `.test.mjs` files that import the `.ts` sources, and their helpers live in `testkit/`.** `tools/lib/run_tests.mjs` detects Node suites by `.js`, `.mjs` and `.cjs` names, and `node --test` runs every file under `test/`. Without `npm ci`, the `ws` halves are skipped with a reason, so the lead's full run stays green on a fresh checkout. CI sets `RELAY_REQUIRE_WS=1` after `npm ci`, so the skip becomes a failure there.
- **The Docker image.** It is `node:24-alpine` (LTS, type stripping on by default), runs as user `node` with root-owned read-only files, runs `npm ci --omit=dev` only when `package-lock.json` exists, and has a `HEALTHCHECK` that uses Node's `fetch` against `/health` (no curl in the image). Built locally on 2026-10-21 with Docker 29.9.1 (Node 24.22.0 in the image). The container answered `/health` with `impl: "ws"`, served a create, join and msg smoke test, reported `healthy`, and exited 0 on `docker stop` (SIGTERM shutdown). The hardening review rebuilt it after its fixes (Node 24 in the image, `ws` layer): `/health` answered, a create, join, msg and same-token reconnect smoke test passed, it reported `healthy` and exited 0 on `docker stop`. The image was never pushed.

### net-client

#### 2026-10-21: One relay core for the loopback hub and the LAN server

- Decision: `RelayRooms` implements the relay's room semantics (hello check, codes, ids, routing rules, tokens, held host rooms, limits) without sockets. `LoopbackHub` drives it in-process with JSON text frames; `LocalRelayServer` drives it over `TCPServer` + `WebSocketPeer`. `LoopbackTransport`, `RelayTransport` and `LanTransport` share the client side (`RelayClientTransport`: hello, create/join, error codes, signals).
- Alternatives: a loopback that only forwards dictionaries; a separate GDScript test server for the RelayTransport tests.
- Reason: tests and in-process simulations then see the relay's real behaviour (codes, `to: 0`, non-host routing, tokens, `peer_left` reasons) and the same JSON float/int effects as the network; one implementation to keep right. LoopbackTransport now extends RelayClientTransport (still a Transport); its public API is unchanged.

#### 2026-10-21: Int conversion rules

- Decision: top-level `peer, host, from, to, seq, proto, max, v` and every `*_id` key become ints only when they hold a whole number; declared paths (schema and `int_fields()`) round any number; peer-keyed dictionaries convert only keys that spell an integer and never inside a snapshot's `state`, an event's `events` or `tm.intents`. `lobby_state.settings.bot_ftp/max_players/min_players` are converted too (requested as an additive NetMessages change).
- Alternatives: convert every number at those keys; convert peer keys everywhere.
- Reason: no data loss on unrelated fractional values; option ids under `player_options` stay strings; games keep their own payload keys as written; intents are cast by games (OWNERSHIP PR4).

#### 2026-10-21: encode() writes insertion order at full precision

- Decision: `Protocol.encode()` is `JSON.stringify(msg, "", false, true)`.
- Alternatives: Godot's default (sorted keys, 14 significant digits).
- Reason: 14 digits loses information for ordinary floats; insertion order keeps dictionaries (rosters, lobbies) iterating the same on every peer. Godot's parser is still not correctly rounded for long decimals, so PROTOCOL.md promises exact round trips for ints and short decimals only.

#### 2026-10-21: NetSession time comes from tick(dt), or one step per poll

- Decision: `tick(dt)` advances the session's platform time and polls; `set_time(t)` sets it; a session that is only polled counts each poll as `Wl.SIM_DT`. The autoload ticks from `_process`. Reconnect backoff, clock pings and coalescing use this time; only `RelayTransport`/`LocalRelayServer` timers (connect timeout, pings, silence, 250 ms linger) read the wall clock, with an injectable `time_func` for tests.
- Alternatives: wall clock everywhere; a SimClock reference in NetSession.
- Reason: in-process HOST simulations stay deterministic (OWNERSHIP C29) whatever the GameHost calls; real sockets are I/O and need real time.

#### 2026-10-21: NetSession owns reconnect; transports report drops

- Decision: transports emit `disconnected("dropped")` and keep `room_code` and `reconnect_token`; `WlNetSession` re-joins at once, then after 1, 2, 4, 8 and every 15 s, and gives up after the token's 120 s with `refused("dropped")`. The autoload saves the token to `Paths.reconnect_token_path()` on every `created`/`joined`; private instances keep it in memory unless `persist_token` is on (with their own `token_path`), so in-process peers sharing one profile never pick up each other's token (OWNERSHIP C28).
- Alternatives: automatic reconnect inside RelayTransport.
- Reason: one policy for every transport, testable over loopback; hosts reclaim peer 1 the same way.

#### 2026-10-21: A refused ready-up is not a session refusal

- Decision: the host answers a `ready` from a client without the game, or with another version, with `refused {reason, scope: "ready", game_id, game_version}`; the client emits `ready_refused(reason, message)` and stays in the lobby. Clients also check locally (`ready_block_reason()`) and never send such a ready. Game versions compare as strings (whole floats as ints).
- Alternatives: a new message type (contract change); reusing `refused` without a scope (would end the session).
- Reason: BRIEF 5.5 wants a clear message without kicking the player; `refused` already exists in NetMessages.

#### 2026-10-21: Schema version mismatches are refused both ways

- Decision: a game message with another `v` is dropped and reported (`protocol_mismatch`); the host refuses that client with `refused version_mismatch`, a client refuses itself on a host message with another `v`. `refused` is accepted on every version.
- Alternatives: silently drop mismatched messages.
- Reason: BRIEF 5.5 "show a clear error on mismatch"; the relay's `hello.proto` check does not cover the game-message schema.

#### 2026-10-21: Lobby slots and drops

- Decision: slots follow join order, then bots. Before the start a dropped peer leaves the lobby but keeps its place in the join order if it comes back; an explicit leave forgets it. `mark_started()` freezes the slots; dropped riders stay with `connected: false`. Changing the game resets clients' ready flags (not the host's); changing content or options does not.
- Alternatives: re-slot on every rejoin; reset ready on every lobby change.
- Reason: stable looks for a flaky connection; scripted peers (e2e) re-ready only when the game changes.

#### 2026-10-21: Coalesced directives are rate-limited per kind, flushed before other messages

- Decision: host `directive` messages of kind `course_grade`, `cadence_band`, `power_band` to one peer are sent at once unless one of the same kind went to that peer in the last 100 ms; then the latest waits and is sent when the window ends or before any other message to that peer. The window is measured on the session's clock, whatever the poll rate; only a private session whose clock stands still (polled without `tick()` after `set_time()`) releases a waiting directive three polls after it started waiting; the autoload, whose clock moves in `_process`, never does.
- Alternatives: batch everything per tick; no coalescing; a fixed two-poll window (the first W1 version: at 60-144 fps it let 20 or more a second through per kind, against C11).
- Reason: OWNERSHIP C11 keeps the host under 60 frames a second without delaying the common 2 Hz case and without reordering `request` or `phase`.

#### 2026-10-21: Game manifests from GameRegistry, overridable

- Decision: `set_game()` looks the manifest up in the game registry (`WlNetSession.registry`, default the `GameRegistry` autoload; duck-typed `manifest(id)`/`games()`) for `min_players`, `max_players`, `bots` and option defaults; `set_game_manifest()` registers one explicitly (tests, in-process runs). Player info without `games`, and `set_local_profile()` with empty `games`, get the registry's `{id: version}`.
- Alternatives: change `set_game()`'s frozen signature; leave defaults to the app shell.
- Reason: the lobby needs the limits and defaults (OWNERSHIP REG4, PS14) and the frozen API has no place for them.

#### 2026-10-21: Sockets linger 250 ms before closing

- Decision: after a leave (client) or a relay-initiated close (server) the WebSocket stays open 250 ms (wall clock) before `close()`, and both ends read every pending frame before handling a close.
- Alternatives: close at once.
- Reason: on Godot 4.7.2 a text frame sent right before `close()` never reaches the other side (measured with a probe); the relay would see a drop instead of a leave, and clients would miss the final `error`.

#### 2026-10-21: One reconnect token per peer in RelayRooms

- Decision: the in-process relay (`RelayRooms`, behind `LoopbackHub` and `LocalRelayServer`) keeps one token per peer while the peer is in the room and returns the same token on every rejoin; a join with that token supersedes a socket still open for the peer. Clients still save whatever token each `created`/`joined` returns.
- Alternatives: a fresh token on every join (the first W1 version).
- Reason: with rotation, a second drop during the rejoin handshake loses the new token with the answer, and the client comes back as a new peer (after the start: `session_in_progress`). BRIEF 5.3 says a token "stays valid for 120 s and keeps the same peer id"; clients that save the latest token work with either relay.

#### 2026-10-21: Leaving cancels what is in flight

- Decision: `WlNetSession.leave()`, refusals, the session's end and `start_offline()` call the transport's `leave()` even before the room exists; `RelayClientTransport.leave()` then sends `leave` (harmless outside a room) and closes the socket, and late `created`/`joined` frames are ignored. A rejoin attempt that fails to connect or is `rate_limited` is retried at the next backoff step instead of ending the session.
- Alternatives: ignore stale `connected` events in NetSession only.
- Reason: a join answered after `leave()` used to put the session back in the room ("joined" after "offline"), and the host listed a rider who had left.

#### 2026-10-21 (W2 round 2): host_room(max_peers, preferred_code) and the LAN room list

- Decision: `WlNetSession.host_room(max_peers := 8, preferred_code := "")` sends the preferred code in the relay's `create`; the relay grants it when free and a new code otherwise. A relay that refuses the coded create (`bad_request`, `room_full`, `room_not_found`, `code_taken`) gets one more create without the code (`push_warning`), so a code never stops a host. `host_room_reclaim()` is now `host_room(max, last_hosted_code())`. P1 LAN list for the join page: `lan_rooms() -> Array[Dictionary]` (`{code, name, game_id, address, port, build}`, sorted by code), `signal lan_rooms_changed(rooms)`, `join_lan(room)`, `stop_lan_discovery()`, `is_lan_discovering()`; beacons carry the optional `game` id. Staleness uses the wall clock (`lan_time_func` for tests): beacons are a 1 Hz real-time affair and a private session's poll-count clock runs at any speed.
- Alternatives: keep `host_room(max)` and have the lobby call `host_room_reclaim()` (the lead chose the two-argument form the lobby already calls); retry any failed create without the code (a socket error would then cost a second connect attempt); let `join_lan()` join by code through LanTransport's own discovery (a second listener on the same UDP port, and a 5 s wait for a room already heard).
- Reason: lead W2 round 2 and docs/DESIGN.md known difference 8 (P1 gaps). Both additions change what subclasses may declare: `tests/app/fakes/fake_net.gd` overrides `host_room(max_peers)` and `tests/app/fakes/fake_lan_net.gd` declares `lan_rooms_changed`, so both need a one-line change (requested from app-shell / the lead).

#### 2026-10-21 (W4): A send budget under the relay's rate limit, replaceable frames give way

- Decision: every frame a `WlNetSession` sends spends a token of a bucket refilled at `send_budget_per_s` (50) up to `send_burst` (100). Frames wait in one FIFO queue while the bucket is empty. A snapshot or roster (per recipient), a `lobby_state` and a coalesced `course_grade`/`cadence_band`/`power_band` (per peer and kind) are replaceable: a newer one removes the queued older copy and joins the back of the queue (`stats.superseded`). Every other frame is sent in order and never dropped. A peer that leaves loses its queued frames; a dropped socket empties the queue.
- Alternatives: mirror the relay exactly (60/120: the relay's own pings and clock drift between the two clocks would still trip it); drop low-priority frames instead of queueing; a priority queue that sends requests ahead of older events (reorders the stream games see).
- Reason: W3 NET-4: at 8 riders the documented directive load is about 71 frames a second and the relay silently dropped `request` and `phase` frames. Superseding instead of reordering keeps "a snapshot never overtakes the events sent before it" and "request and phase are never dropped or reordered" (OWNERSHIP C11).

#### 2026-10-21 (W4): A reclaiming host reconciles with joined.peers and resyncs who stayed

- Decision: `RelayRooms` (the in-process relay) now sends `joined.peers` and `joined.rejoin` like `server/relay`, and `RelayClientTransport` keeps them (`joined_peers`, `joined_peers_known`, `rejoined`). When the host reclaims peer 1 after a drop, a connected peer missing from `joined.peers` gets `peer_left(peer, "dropped")`, a listed peer it did not know gets `peer_joined` (and `rejoin_requested` after the start), and every other connected session peer gets the new signal `resync_requested(peer)`. A client re-sends `player_info` (and a wanted ready-up before the start) on `peer_joined` of the host.
- Alternatives: emit `rejoin_requested` for every peer (the GameHost would mark them as awaiting pedaling and resend `session_start`); queue messages while the host is away and flush them on reclaim (stale phases and events would arrive late and out of step).
- Reason: W3 NET-1: after a host drop nothing resent the phases it changed meanwhile, departed peers stayed "connected" with stale power, and a client that joined meanwhile never reached the lobby.

#### 2026-10-21 (W4): WKG lobby_state hides every rider's p_eff_per_w from the other clients

- Decision: in a WKG lobby the `lobby_state` clients receive carries `p_eff_per_w` 1.0 (the 75 kg reference) for every human, the host's own included; the host keeps the real values in its own `lobby`. A client's `lobby_slots()` takes its own entry's value from its own `player_info`. FAIR_FTP (250 / FTP, and FTP is public in the lobby) and RAW (1) are sent as before.
- Alternatives: one personalised `lobby_state` per client (n frames for every lobby change, against the send budget); redact only riders who did not share their weight (needs a new lobby field; a shared weight is still nobody else's business in the lobby).
- Reason: W3 PROF-2 and BRIEF 3.2 ("weight never leaves the machine unless the player opts in; the host receives only what the selected scaling needs"): 75 / p gave every other client the exact weight. Clients never simulate other riders, so nothing on a client needs the value.

#### 2026-10-21 (W4 round 2): A replaceable frame stops being replaced after 0.5 s, so it is never starved

- Decision: a queued replaceable frame is replaced (removed, the newer one joins the back) only while its chain of replacements is younger than `SUPERSEDE_MAX_S` (0.5 s) or an older kept frame of the same key already waits. Otherwise it stays where it is and the newer one queues behind it, so at most two frames of one key wait: the kept one, which goes out when it reaches the front, and the newest, which later ones keep replacing. A clock that went backwards counts as an old chain.
- Alternatives: replace the queued frame in place (fresher content, but the newer snapshot would overtake the events, and a newer coalesced directive the `phase`, queued after the old one's slot); keep the old one and drop the newer (the newest `lobby_state`, band or final snapshot would never arrive); a time limit alone, appending a new frame each time (with 23 replaceable keys under the 8-rider load the demand stayed over the budget and the queue grew without bound).
- Reason: W4r2 R11: under sustained overload every snapshot was replaced before it reached the front, so clients got none for as long as the load lasted (15 of 300 in the first 1.5 s, then a 28.5 s gap). With the rule each key goes out about once per queue drain (snapshots at most 1.35 s apart in `tests/net/test_net_budget.gd`), the backlog stays bounded (about two frames per key plus the never-dropped frames, which stay under the budget), nothing overtakes a frame queued before it, and the newest of each key still arrives.

#### 2026-10-21 (W4 final): No client learns another rider's weight in any scaling mode

- Decision: the `lobby_state` clients receive carries `rider_mass_kg` 75 (the reference) for every human in every scaling mode, on top of the WKG `p_eff_per_w` 1.0; bots keep theirs, the host keeps the real values in its own `lobby`, and a client's `lobby_slots()` takes its own `rider_mass_kg` from its own `player_info` as it already did for `p_eff_per_w`. The GameHost's `session_start` (sdk-runtime's `game_host.gd`, session_start only) does the same per client: one broadcast in FAIR_FTP (no slot carries a weight there), one message per client in RAW and WKG, each with only its own `rider_mass_kg` and `p_eff_per_w`; a rejoin gets its own values back. A client sends `player_info` as the new `WlNetSession.wire_player_info(info, scaling)`: `weight_kg` only in a RAW lobby, `p_eff_by_mode`'s WKG entry (75 / weight) only in a WKG lobby, whoever built the info. The client keeps the full copy locally and re-sends whenever a scaling change changes what goes out, after the `lobby_changed` handlers had their turn, so the app's own re-send is not doubled and a lobby_state echo never loops. `player_info_from_profile()` keeps building that full copy (`weight_kg` and the WKG entry whenever the weight is shared, as before), because the fallback copy `_ensure_local_info()` builds once from the Settings profile is never rebuilt on a scaling change; only `wire_player_info()` decides what leaves the machine. Join case: `join_room()` clears `lobby`, so a client's first `player_info` goes out before it knows the scaling, without a RAW lobby's weight. A client therefore holds a wanted ready (from `set_ready(true)` or a pending ready on connect) until its first `lobby_state`, and holds one asked for by a `lobby_changed` handler until those handlers have run, then sends it after the `player_info` for that scaling; messages from one peer arrive in order, so the host never counts a sharer ready at 75 kg. `set_ready(false)` cancels a held ready (and still sends `ready: false`).
- Alternatives: redact only RAW and keep the FAIR_FTP/WKG lobby as before (the same code path covers every mode at one extra encode per lobby change); a personalised `lobby_state` per client (n frames per lobby change against the send budget); only relabel the opt-in "Share weight with the host" as "with everyone" (the shared weight buys the other clients nothing: they never simulate other riders); leave `weight_kg` in every mode's `player_info` (BRIEF 5.5 allows it with the opt-in, but BRIEF 3.2 says the host receives only what the selected scaling needs). For the local copy: build it per mode and rebuild the Settings fallback on every scaling change too (a second rebuild path for the same result). For the join case: send the first `player_info` with the weight (it would reach a FAIR_FTP or WKG host, against BRIEF 3.2); have the host ignore a ready until it has a `player_info` built for its scaling (app-built info carries no `p_eff_mode`, so the host cannot tell a non-sharer from an info built before the join answer); put the scaling into the relay's `joined` answer (a relay protocol change for one field).
- Reason: W4 DoD finding (BRIEF 3.2 privacy, P0): in a RAW lobby a rider who shared their weight with the host had it broadcast to every other client as `players[].rider_mass_kg` in `lobby_state` and `session_start`. The only consumers of the mass are the host's rules and the rider's own LocalRider. The W4 final review found the join race (a scripted RAW client readied in its `joined` handler and could be started at 75 kg) and the fallback copy losing its weight; `tests/net/test_net_host_reclaim.gd` covers both. Known transient, as for WKG already: right after the host switches an existing lobby to RAW, the host uses 75 kg for a rider who is already ready until that rider's re-sent `player_info` arrives (one round trip); scaling changes do not reset ready flags.

### rides

Short ADRs for `addons/wattlands/rides/`, `server/mock_strava/` and `tools/fit_check/` (BRIEF 3.8, 7.1, 7.5).

- **2026-10-21: Records are sample-and-hold at whole seconds; totals are integrated.** Record k is stamped at UTC start + k and holds the latest values fed at or before that second. Work, distance (from the reported speed) and time-weighted cadence and speed are integrated between `record()` calls with the held values; averages are over elapsed time; maxima are the largest values fed. *Alternatives:* averaging every sample inside each second; work as the sum of record powers. *Reason:* the recorder gets ~1 Hz trainer samples with jitter or 20 Hz ticks, and both give one record per second and the same totals; integration over platform time is exact for the piecewise-constant feed and independent of the feed rate.
- **2026-10-21: Held values expire after 3 s without a fresh sample.** `RideRecorder.hold_limit_s` (default 3 s, the platform's telemetry staleness in OWNERSHIP C20; 0 holds forever): after a gap that long, power counts as 0 W and cadence and speed as absent (no distance) until the next `record()`. Records still come once a second. Non-finite inputs count as 0 W or absent. *Alternatives:* hold forever (the first version); leave staleness to the caller. *Reason:* a caller that feeds per trainer notification would otherwise credit the last power for a whole BLE dropout, inflating kJ and distance on Strava; the recorder can't tell a silent trainer from a steady one, so it bounds the hold itself.
- **2026-10-21: The OAuth callback drops idle connections.** A connection that hasn't sent a complete request within 10 s (`idle_timeout_s`) is closed. *Reason:* browsers open speculative connections that never send anything; eight of them would otherwise fill `MAX_CONNECTIONS` and leave the real callback waiting in the backlog until the 5-minute window ends. Its two page texts come from `content/strings/rides.csv`.
- **2026-10-21: Asking to upload a ride that is already on Strava re-reports its state.** `upload()` doesn't send it again (no new attempt, no `upload_finished`), but emits `upload_state_changed(id, "uploaded"|"duplicate", activity id)` so a results screen that asks after an auto-upload still shows the result. A summary without `ride_id` falls back to the JSON saved next to the FIT. `uploads_busy()` also counts a running connect that will retry a non-empty queue, so a scripted peer that waits for it doesn't quit with its upload still queued.
- **2026-10-21: Pauses stop the FIT timer, not the records.** `set_timer_running(false)` writes a timer stop_all event; paused time counts in elapsed time and not in timer time; records continue (the trainer still runs on platform time). *Alternative:* no records while paused. *Reason:* BRIEF 7.5 checks one record per second over the ride, and a paused rider may still pedal.
- **2026-10-21: A lap at the instant the current lap started renames it.** `start()` opens the first lap; each `lap(label, t)` closes it at t and opens a new one, unless no time has passed in the current lap. Expected laps = lap() calls after the start instant + 1, which equals OWNERSHIP FIT1's formula (lap-true phases + lap_markers + 1 for the cool-down) when the first phase is lap-true (Workout, Siege, Tank and Fishing all start that way); a lap-false first phase adds the opening lap. *Alternative:* a zero-length lap. *Reason:* LocalRider laps phase 0 at the ride's start, and zero-length laps are noise in Strava.
- **2026-10-21: Lap names travel in one developer field.** The FIT lap message has no name field, so the encoder declares developer data (index 0, a fixed application id) with field 0 `lap_name` (string, 32 bytes) and adds it to every lap. *Alternatives:* workout_step messages linked by `wkt_step_index`; no names. *Reason:* `PlanPhase.label` is documented as the FIT lap name; a developer field is the standard way, Garmin's SDK decodes it, and Strava ignores it.
- **2026-10-21: FIT header: protocol byte 0x20 and profile version 2140 (21.40).** *Alternative:* the SDK's 21.218 encoding (21218). *Reason:* 0x20 is protocol 2.0 in the FIT header's nibble encoding; every field we write exists in profile 21.40, and the major*100+minor encoding is read the same way by every decoder.
- **2026-10-21: Fixed local message types, each definition written once.** file_id 0, developer_data_id 1, field_description 2, device_info 3, event 4, record 5, lap 6, session 7, activity 8; little endian; no compressed timestamps. Messages are written in time order (a lap before the records of its end second), then the last timer stop, the last lap, the session and the activity. *Reason:* simplest valid layout; Garmin's SDK decodes it with zero errors.
- **2026-10-21: `save()` never overwrites; `save_as()` writes an exact path.** Two rides that start in the same UTC second get `_2`, `_3` stems. `save_as(path)` (additive) exists for GameSimRunner's `<report dir>/fit/<report stem>_slot<n>.fit`. The JSON summary always sits next to the FIT with the same stem.
- **2026-10-21: The ride UUID comes from the OS's secure random bytes.** `RideRecorder.new_uuid()` uses `Crypto.generate_random_bytes` (RFC 4122 v4). *Alternative:* the seeded RNG. *Reason:* it is an identifier for Strava's `external_id`, not simulation state; it never enters a rule. Callers that need a deterministic id (sim reports) set `ride_id` before `start()`.
- **2026-10-21: HTTP through `HTTPRequest` with redirects followed by hand.** Every request goes through `Locks.network_allows()`; `max_redirects` is 0 and the authorize fetch follows `Location` itself, checking each hop, so a loopback mock can't bounce the client off loopback. After a response the coroutine resumes on the next frame, outside the HTTPRequest's signal, so callers may free the client at once.
- **2026-10-21: Token policy.** Refresh when the access token has less than 10 minutes left and once on a 401; always store the refresh token Strava returns. A 400 from the refresh grant (revoked on strava.com) clears the tokens and reports `error`/`reauthorize`, keeping the client id and secret; 401, 429, 5xx and offline keep the tokens. The client's clock is the system clock (injectable for tests); this is I/O, never the sim path.
- **2026-10-21: Disconnect deletes the secret and tokens even if the revoke fails.** The client id stays in `strava.json` for a reconnect; state detail `revoke_failed` tells the UI. *Reason:* the player asked to disconnect; leaving tokens behind would be worse than an unrevoked grant they can remove on strava.com.
- **2026-10-21: Revoke falls back to deauthorize.** Disconnect calls POST <oauth base>/revoke with HTTP Basic client id/secret and the refresh token as BRIEF 3.8 says; if that endpoint answers 404 or 405 it calls POST <oauth base>/deauthorize (Strava's long-documented endpoint) with the access token in the Authorization header. *Reason:* neither endpoint can be tried against the real Strava in this run; the fallback keeps Disconnect revoking whichever one Strava serves. The mock serves both.
- **2026-10-21: Queue semantics.** Kept in `rides/upload_queue.json`: offline, 401 after a refresh, 429 after the retries, 5xx and processing timeouts (with the upload id, so a retry polls instead of uploading again). Dropped and marked `failed` in the ride's JSON: other 4xx and Strava processing errors (retrying can't fix them) and entries that failed 8 times. Duplicates count as uploaded. The queue never holds a token or the secret. Retried at launch (once connected) and after every connect.
- **2026-10-21: 429 backoff.** `Retry-After` when present, else 15 s doubling per retry (capped at 120 s), at most 3 retries per request; polling backs off the same way. *Reason:* Strava sends usage headers rather than `Retry-After`; three retries spread over ~2 minutes stay inside its 15-minute window without stalling the results screen forever.
- **2026-10-21: `--strava-base=<root>` maps to `<root>/api/v3` and `<root>/oauth`.** WlRides reads it with `CmdLine` before falling back to `Settings.strava_api_base()`/`strava_oauth_base()`, so the mapping is the same whatever Settings does with the flag. The mock serves both trees under one root.
- **2026-10-21: Auto-upload rides on `EventBus.ride_saved`.** `WlRides.save_ride(recorder)` (additive) saves and emits `ride_saved`; WlRides uploads every saved ride without a prompt when the profile's `strava_auto_upload` or `--strava-auto-upload` is set, and queues it until connected. An uploaded ride is never sent twice (its JSON records the upload), so a results screen that also calls `upload()` can't double it.
- **2026-10-21: `--strava-client-id` and `--strava-client-secret` connect at start.** For scripted peers (e2e), WlRides runs the loopback connect deferred from `_ready`, then retries the queue.
- **2026-10-21: The mock rotates refresh tokens strictly.** Every refresh issues a new refresh token and the previous one stops working at once. *Reason:* stricter than Strava (which may return the same token), so a client that keeps an old token fails the tests.
- **2026-10-21: `fit_check` compares each FIT with its summary.** When `<stem>.json` next to the FIT is a RideRecorder summary, record and lap counts and session totals must match (BRIEF 7.5 "session totals equal to the recorder's"). One record per second is checked as floor(elapsed) + 1 records within 1 % (at least 2).
- **2026-10-21: The fallback decoder contract is a flag-and-JSON interface.** `tools/godot.sh --headless --path <repo> -s res://tools/fit_decode/decode.gd -- --json-out=<path> --file=<path>...` writing the JSON in `tools/fit_check/README.md`; `check.mjs` runs the same checks on it. qa builds the decoder; `tools/fit_check/testdata/fake_decode.mjs` is a reference implementation of the contract.
- **2026-10-21 (W4, RS-2): An upload is queued before its first request.** `WlRides._upload()` puts the ride in `rides/upload_queue.json` (last_state "uploading", attempts unchanged) before the first request, `StravaClient.upload_created(ride_id, upload_id)` (additive) stores the upload id as soon as the POST returns (last_state "processing"), and the entry leaves the queue only when the upload is uploaded, a duplicate or a permanent failure. The history shows an upload in flight as "uploading". *Alternatives:* make `App.quit()` wait for `uploads_busy()` (a 429 backoff or a slow POST can take minutes, and the queue covers crashes too). *Reason:* BRIEF 3.8 "keep failed or offline uploads in rides/upload_queue.json for the next launch"; W3 found a ride whose auto-upload was still running at quit neither on Strava nor in the queue. Strava's external_id (the ride id) makes a re-send a duplicate, which counts as uploaded.
- **2026-10-21 (W4, QT-5): `strava_cancel_connect()` stays, documented as what it is.** It cancels a running connect (whose loopback callback waits up to 5 minutes, BRIEF 3.8); Settings has no Cancel item yet, so the doc no longer claims one (requests for lead: the app's Strava page could offer it while connecting).
- **2026-10-21 (W4r2, PROF): the Strava import refuses values outside the profile editor's ranges.** Decision: `strava_import_athlete` applies an FTP only within 50-600 W and a weight only within 30-200 kg (`IMPORT_FTP_MIN_W` .. `IMPORT_WEIGHT_MAX_KG`, the profile screen's FTP field and `AppStyle.WEIGHT_MIN_KG..WEIGHT_MAX_KG`); a value outside is not applied, logged with push_warning and listed in the additive result key `refused` ([{field, value, min, max}]). *Alternatives:* clamp into the range (a 1500 W typo would become a verified 600 W FTP, three times the rider's ceiling basis); keep the "> 0" check (W4r2: FTP 1500 W verified, weight 9 kg). *Reason:* an imported FTP is marked verified and every governor value scales with it, so a value the rider could not have typed must not slip in; the rider can still type it in Profile.
- **2026-10-21 (W4 final, DoD 3.8): offline upload attempts never count toward the retry cap.** Decision: `WlRides._apply_result()` adds an attempt only for a real failure (state "failed": an error response, a Strava processing error or timeout); an "offline" result (no connectivity, including a token refresh that couldn't reach Strava) and a "queued" one (not connected) add none and never drop the entry (`UploadQueue.counts_as_attempt()`, `UploadQueue.UNCOUNTED_STATES`, additive). `UploadQueue.MAX_ATTEMPTS` = 8 stays for real failures: the eighth drops the entry and marks the ride `failed`. *Alternatives:* keep counting offline attempts (a trainer PC without internet lost the automatic retry after 8 launches or connects); a separate, larger cap for offline attempts (still drops a ride BRIEF 3.8 says to keep). *Reason:* BRIEF 3.8 "keep failed or offline uploads in rides/upload_queue.json for the next launch" sets no limit on offline uploads; an offline retry costs one request per launch and can always succeed later, while the cap exists to stop retrying uploads Strava keeps refusing.
- **2026-10-21 (W4 final, DoD 3.8): the scenario tests copy their FITs into build/scenarios/ for the SDK decode.** Decision: the scenario base (`tests/scenarios/lib/scenario_test.gd`, qa's helper, changed for this finding) keeps writing reports and FITs to a per-test temp dir, and in `after_each`, before the dir is removed, copies every `.fit` under it with its same-stem summary JSON to `build/scenarios/<test script>/<test name>/` (`scenario_lib.gd`: `fit_files`, `copy_fits`, a taken name gets `_2`; a failed copy fails the test). The first scenario script of a GUT run clears the folder (`prepare_fit_dir`, a `.gut_run` stamp holding the Godot process id and start time), so later scripts of the run keep their copies and stale FITs of earlier runs are gone. The fit step of `tools/run_tests` already lists `build/scenarios`, so `tools/fit_check` decodes them with @garmin/fitsdk and compares each with its summary (14 FITs, 0 failed). *Alternatives:* write the scenario reports straight into `build/scenarios/` (the tests rely on a fresh, removed temp dir and Paths override per test); clear the folder in each script's `before_all` (each script would delete the previous scripts' copies); drop `build/scenarios` from the fit step (BRIEF 3.8 wants every FIT the tests write SDK-decoded; the GDScript decoder is only the npm-blocked fallback). *Reason:* BRIEF 3.8 "decode every FIT file the tests write with @garmin/fitsdk"; the scenario FITs were checked only by QaFitDecoder.

### sdk-runtime

Short ADRs for `addons/wattlands/sdk/` (WlGameRegistry, PlanValidator, GameHost, DirectiveRouter, HudRouter, IntentRouter, PhaseTracker, SessionStatsTracker, ReportCollector, SdkConformance, GameSimRunner) and `tests/sdk/`. "BRIEF x.y" is `docs/BRIEF.md`; C-numbers are `docs/OWNERSHIP.md` 3.1.

#### 2026-10-21: A host-side refusal refuses the whole directive

- Decision: `DirectiveRouter.request` runs its checks in a fixed order (unknown peer, no phase, cool-down, bad fields, `erg_in_sim_phase`, `outside_range_pct`, SIM-block length, `sim_block_unsafe`, `switch_spacing`, `mode_grade_outside_sim`, `haptic_in_erg`, `haptics_off`) and refuses the whole request on the first failure, with `push_warning`, an `EventBus.directive_applied` record `{refused: true, reason, by: "host"}` and a report entry. Every request gets an id, refused ones too, so the report can join requested and applied records. An ERG target in a SIM phase is refused as `outside_range_pct` (a SIM phase has no ERG range).
- Alternatives: drop only the offending channel and apply the rest.
- Reason: a partial apply hides game bugs (C25's argument); BRIEF 3.6 says "the DirectiveSink refuses anything else". The governor still drops single channels where BRIEF 4.6.6 says so (haptics in warm-up, cooldown).

#### 2026-10-21: The host tracks every rider's ERG<->SIM switches in game ticks

- Decision: `PhaseTracker` follows each rider's effective mode (the phase mode, or SIM during a directive block) and records the game tick of every change: plan phase changes between ERG and SIM, a block's entry, its expiry, and an early exit by a `mode: "ERG"` request. A new SIM block needs 30 s of game time since that tick. The block's own exit is never checked (the bracketing pair, BRIEF 4.5), but it counts for the next block. Block expiry rounds to whole ticks.
- Alternatives: platform time; ask the rider's governor.
- Reason: the host must decide synchronously (`DirectiveResult`), remote governors answer 0.2 s later, and directive expiry already counts game time (paused time can't create a switch).

#### 2026-10-21: course_grade coalescing

- Decision: the first `course_grade` for a rider goes out at once; later ones go out at most every 10 ticks (0.5 s of game time), always the newest value; a value still pending when the rider's phase changes is dropped. Each delivered value gets an id and a report entry (calls coalesced away are not recorded).
- Alternatives: forward every call and let NetSession thin them; a fixed 2 Hz clock.
- Reason: BRIEF 3.6 "coalesced to 2 Hz"; holding a grade across a phase boundary would apply it to the wrong phase.

#### 2026-10-21: Platform events ride in the game event batches

- Decision: the host's `event {seq, events}` batches carry game events and platform events whose `type` starts with `wl.` (`wl.phase_started {peer, index, ended_by}`, `wl.lap_marker {label}`, `wl.finished {reason}`). Clients handle `wl.` events themselves (EventBus.phase_started for other riders, LocalRider.lap, game_finished) and never pass them to `apply_event`. Game event types starting with `wl.` (and `snapshot`) are refused with a warning. Batches flush every second platform tick (10 Hz), before that tick's snapshot.
- Alternatives: separate messages per platform event.
- Reason: BRIEF 3.6 says phase changes go "in an event"; one ordered stream keeps every peer's event order identical and within 10 messages a second (C12).

#### 2026-10-21: Session wire formats

- Decision: `session_start {game_id, game_version, content_id, seed, players: [PlayerSlot.to_dict()], options, scaling, start_tick, session_id, content_overrides}`; `plan {peer, plan}`; `phase {peer, index, start_tick, ended_by}`; `directive {kind: request, id, peer, directive: {...}}`, `{kind: course_grade, id, peer, grade_pct}`, `{kind: cadence_band, id, peer, lo_rpm, hi_rpm}`, `{kind: power_band, peer, lo_w, hi_w}`; `snapshot {game_id, seq, tick, state}`; `roster {riders: {peer: {raw_w, cadence_rpm, ftp_pct, rescue, afk, connected, status, name, slot_index}}}`; `hud {kind, key, args, seconds}`; `results {results}`; `session_end {reason}`; `telemetry {tm}`. The GameHost casts every number it reads and converts peer-keyed dictionary keys itself, so it works whether or not `Protocol.decode` already did.
- Alternatives: flatten the directive fields into the message.
- Reason: `EffortDirective.to_dict()` keeps exactly the game's keys; nesting avoids a clash between the message `id` and the directive's own fields.

#### 2026-10-21: Plans are validated at their worst case per phase

- Decision: a phase whose ERG peak (`PlanPhase.peak_pct()`: target, ramp end, range top) is above 105% (120%) accrues its whole worst-case length (open-ended: `max_s`); a reset run counts best-case lengths (open-ended: `min_s`) of consecutive ERG phases whose peak is <= 90%; SIM phases restart the run. Warm-up checks every ERG value of each phase that starts inside the first 300 s (best-case starts). The mode-switch warning includes the switch into the cool-down. Thresholds are inclusive where BRIEF 4.6.1 says so.
- Alternatives: integrate the ramp exactly.
- Reason: directives may hold the target anywhere in the effective range for the whole phase, so only the worst case is safe (BRIEF 4.6 outranks gameplay).

#### 2026-10-21: replan swaps at the next phase change

- Decision: `ctx.directives.replan(peer, plan)` validates phases 0..current of the current plan plus the new phases as one plan, finalizes it and keeps it pending; the swap happens at the rider's next phase change, where the rider gets `set_plan` (local) or a `plan` message followed by its `phase` message (remote).
- Alternatives: apply at once and restart the current phase.
- Reason: `SafetyGovernor.set_plan` resets channel state; swapping mid-phase would restart ramps and elapsed time. Games replan between phases (BRIEF 3.6).

#### 2026-10-21: Local HUD drawing goes through the session's EventBus

- Decision: HudRouter "draws locally" by emitting this peer's WlEventBus HUD signals (`power_band_changed`, `roster_changed`, `toast_requested`, `banner_requested`, `readout_changed`), which HudLayer reads. The GameHost does not use `HudLayer.local_feed()`. The host's bus carries every rider's power and cadence band; a client's bus carries its own.
- Alternatives: wrap `HudLayer.local_feed()`.
- Reason: HudLayer's contract says it reads the bus; going through both would draw twice. Headless runs need no HudLayer at all.

#### 2026-10-21: Gestures come from the LocalRider, actions from the InputRouter

- Decision: every tick the GameHost drains `LocalRider.take_gestures()` (for `local_intents`, `Telemetry.gestures` and the frame) and `InputRouter.take_actions()` (or `inject_action()`); the router's own gesture queue is drained and ignored. `pause` and `panic` are consumed (SOLO pause toggle, `LocalRider.panic()`); `shift_up`/`shift_down` also call `LocalRider.shift()` and still reach the game. When rendering, the GameHost attaches its rider to the InputRouter (`attach_rider`) for menus and detaches it at the end.
- Alternatives: gestures from the router.
- Reason: one source per input, so no gesture arrives twice (C14, GM8).

#### 2026-10-21: GameSimRunner isolation and paths

- Decision: a run moves `Paths`' process-wide override to a fresh temp root for its duration (every human slot's progress under `slotN/games/<id>/`, the recorders' rides) and `cleanup()` restores the caller's override and deletes the root. FIT files go to `<report dir>/fit/<report stem>_slot<n>.fit` (the GameHost moves `LocalRider.finish_ride()`'s file and its JSON summary there right after saving, so slots never collide), or `<temp root>/fit/sim_slot<n>.fit` without a report. Relative `--report` and `--override` paths resolve against the project folder (the repo root, where the tools run).
- Alternatives: a `rides_dir` option on LocalRider (a frozen API owned by platform).
- Reason: C28 and SIM7 (the user's profile is never touched) without changing another module's API.

#### 2026-10-21: Report schema 1 and the deterministic hash

- Decision: `build_report()` returns `schema, args, game_id, content_id, role, seed, slots, plans, plan_warnings, overrides, end_reason, finished_reason, sim_seconds, game_seconds, max_sim_seconds, events, phases, directives, directives_unmatched, trace, snapshots, max_snapshot_bytes, oversize_snapshots, results, unlocks, clients, fit_paths, meta, deterministic_hash`. `t` is platform seconds in trace rows and `game_t` game seconds; events carry `t` (game) and `pt` (platform); phase `start`/`end` are game seconds. `deterministic_hash` is the sha256 of `JSON.stringify(report, "", true)` without `fit_paths`, `meta`, the hash, and the args `report`, `override_path`, `screenshot_dir`, `at`, `headless`, `sim_speed`.
- Alternatives: hash only events and results.
- Reason: SIM4/SIM5; the games' `check_sims.mjs` read these keys, and the FIT stamp and paths vary between machines.

#### 2026-10-21: Generic time in band uses the eligible time as denominator

- Decision: `SessionStatsTracker` counts ERG seconds with a positive applied target and SIM seconds with a published power band as eligible; `time_in_band_pct` is in-band seconds over eligible seconds. Best 1/5/20-minute power is 0 for a ride shorter than the window; NP below 30 s is the average power. Samples come once per game tick from the frame telemetry (paused time is not ridden time).
- Alternatives: divide by the whole ride.
- Reason: SIM without a band has no target to be in; games with their own definition override the rows (BRIEF 3.7).

#### 2026-10-21: Clients type their options from the manifest

- Decision: a client resolves `session_start`'s `options` and every slot's `options` through the game's manifest (`WlGameRegistry.resolve_option_values`), so an `int` option is an int on every peer even though JSON delivered a float.
- Alternatives: declare option paths for Protocol's int conversion.
- Reason: CTX8/PS14 require identical ctx values on host and clients.

#### 2026-10-21: Results identity and apply_progress once

- Decision: `GameResults.session_id` is `"<game>/<content>/<seed>"` (deterministic for reports); each GameHost applies progress once per session id, so a duplicated `results` message never adds XP twice; a restarted client is a new GameHost and applies it once (C33).
- Alternatives: a random session id.
- Reason: determinism of reports and of the conformance hashes.

#### 2026-10-21: Conformance identifies games in log lines

- Decision: `tests/sdk/test_conformance.gd` runs `SdkConformance.check_game` for `_template` and every registered game inside fixed test functions and prints `conformance: <id> ok|FAILED` per game, with the id in every assertion message. Rider counts are min_players and max_players; with `bots` true that is one human slot plus bots (SOLO), otherwise Autopilot human slots (HOST). Plan validation runs for every content id; the 2-minute runs use `default_content` with seed 4242.
- Alternatives: GUT parameterized tests.
- Reason: GDScript test names can't be generated per game, and GUT's parameter names don't carry the id (TL8 asks for the id in the log).

#### 2026-10-21: Registry additions for fixtures and tools

- Decision: additive WlGameRegistry API: `content_root`/`assets_root` (tests point them at fixtures), `scan_on_ready`, `clear()`, `add_game(m, folder, allow_template)`, `template_manifest()`, `warnings(id)`, `game_dir/content_dir/assets_dir(id)`, `check_option_values()` (tools exit naming the field), and statics `pick_first_run`, `resolve_option_values`, `validate_option_entries`, `coerce_option_value`, `instantiate_manifest`, `normalized`, `parse_json`, `json_equal`, `is_whole`. Fixture games live under `tests/fixtures/games/` (never under `src/games`), all using one fixture GameModule script.
- Alternatives: fixture games under `src/games`.
- Reason: the real registry must never list test games; first-run tests must not depend on which games are installed (B7.1).

#### 2026-10-21: W1 test doubles; the real stack runs in W2

- Decision: W1 tests drive the GameHost with `tests/sdk/helpers/fake_rider.gd` (a LocalRider subclass with a real MockTrainer) and `fake_net.gd`/`fake_net_hub.gd` (a WlNetSession subclass that sends every message through JSON). GameSimRunner takes `rider_factory` and `net_factory` args for them. `tests/sdk/integration/` runs GameSimRunner and SdkConformance over the real LocalRider, governor, NetSession, LoopbackTransport and Protocol; the W1 test command leaves that folder out (no `-ginclude_subdirs`), the full suite includes it.
- Alternatives: wait for W2 to test the GameHost.
- Reason: LocalRider, WlNetSession, LoopbackTransport and Protocol are W0 stubs in this worktree.

#### 2026-10-21: Rendering sessions take the local rider's gestures from the InputRouter (supersedes "Gestures come from the LocalRider")

- Decision: when the GameHost renders (not headless) and has an InputRouter, the local rider's gestures for `local_intents`, `Telemetry.gestures` and the frame come from `InputRouter.take_gestures()`, which merges the attached rider's pedal gestures (BRIEF 3.5, `WlInputRouter.attach_rider`); the GameHost then leaves the LocalRider's own gesture queue to the router. Headless sessions read `LocalRider.take_gestures()` and drain and ignore a router's queue. Bots' gestures always come from their LocalRiders. Actions always come from `InputRouter.take_actions()` plus `inject_action()`.
- Alternatives: the LocalRider's queue in every mode (the earlier ADR).
- Reason: the frozen InputRouter contract makes it the merged source for menus and sessions; if the router collects the rider's gestures by pulling `take_gestures()` itself, a GameHost that also pulled them would race it and lose them. One source per mode, so no gesture arrives twice (GM8, C14).

#### 2026-10-21: The platform completes the results rows

- Decision: after `game.results()` the GameHost calls `complete_rows()`: every rider in `ctx.players` gets a row (the SessionStatsTracker's base row for a rider the game left out, appended after the game's rows), and every row gets the base keys the game did not set. Values the game set are never changed.
- Alternatives: trust the game to start from `GameResults.from_stats`.
- Reason: BRIEF 3.6 promises per-player rows with kJ, %FTP and time in band; the default `GameModule.results()` returns no rows, and the results screen and conformance need one per rider.

#### 2026-10-21: Translations are reference-counted across registry instances

- Decision: `WlGameRegistry` counts how many instances registered each Translation (the resource cache hands every instance the same object) and removes it from the TranslationServer only when the last one clears.
- Alternatives: never remove translations.
- Reason: the conformance test and other tests scan with private registries; clearing one removed the strings the GameRegistry autoload still served (CTX13).

#### 2026-10-21: Rejoin and stale telemetry

- Decision: a dropped peer's stored telemetry is zeroed (power, cadence, speed, P_eff), so after a rejoin the rider shows 0 W and AFK until fresh telemetry shows pedaling. The rejoin `phase` message carries the phase's real `start_tick` and how it began (`ended_by` of the previous phase, `start` for phase 0); a client sets its phase clock from `start_tick` and each snapshot's `tick`, so a rejoined client's PhaseState matches the host's.
- Alternatives: keep the last values until new telemetry arrives; restart the client's phase clock at 0.
- Reason: IF6, NET6 and the HUD timeline after a rejoin.

#### 2026-10-21: A SIM block the governor refuses is taken back

- Decision: when a rider's governor refuses a SIM block the host pre-approved (a local rider's returned record, or a remote rider's ack in `Telemetry.acks`), the host's PhaseTracker drops the block and restores the rider's mode and latest switch, while the block is still the running one.
- Alternatives: keep the host's view until the block's nominal end.
- Reason: otherwise the host would accept `mode_grade` and haptics for a block that never ran and spend the 30 s switch spacing on it (BRIEF 4.5).

#### 2026-10-21: session_start repeats its string ids without "_id"

- Decision: `session_start` also carries `game`, `content` and `session` (copies of `game_id`, `content_id`, `session_id`); clients read those first and fall back to the `_id` fields when they are strings.
- Alternatives: rely on Protocol.decode leaving string `*_id` values alone.
- Reason: `net_messages.gd` says decode converts every `*_id` key to int; a strict implementation would turn `"workout"` into a number and the client could not find its game. The contract clarification is requested; this keeps sessions working either way.

#### 2026-10-21: A confirm that resumes a SOLO pause is the platform's

- Decision: when `confirm` resumes a paused SOLO session it is consumed; the game's `local_intents` never sees it. `pedal_start` that resumes still reaches the game as a gesture.
- Alternatives: pass it through.
- Reason: a rider pressing A to resume must not also buy, cast or pick in the game on the same tick.

#### 2026-10-21 (W2): Loopback HOST runs wait for every client's player_info

- Decision: `GameSimRunner` starts a HOST run (`WlNetSession.mark_started()`) only once the host's lobby lists every human rider, i.e. after each loopback client's `player_info` has arrived.
- Alternatives: start as soon as each client has a peer id (W1); have NetSession admit any connected peer at the start.
- Reason: `mark_started()` fixes the session's players from the lobby, which lists a peer only after its `player_info`; the last client was still in flight, got `session_in_progress` and never rode, so the host's roster showed it at 0 W (qa W1) and HOST runs never reached `complete`.

#### 2026-10-21 (W2): A client GameHost takes the session_start that opened it from its config

- Decision: `GameHost.setup()` in the CLIENT role queues `config.session_start` (the message the app shell received before the GameHost existed) and applies it on the first `step()`, before any later message; a copy that arrives again on the net is ignored because the client configures once. The GameHost keeps connecting to `config.net.game_message` in `setup()`, so `plan` and `phase`, sent right after `session_start`, still reach it.
- Alternatives: have the app shell re-emit `session_start` after `start()`; add a resync request to the protocol.
- Reason: the app shell creates the client's GameHost inside the `game_message` emission of `session_start`, so the GameHost never saw it and stayed in `setup` (qa's real e2e run: both clients stayed in the lobby). `tests/sdk/integration/test_client_session_start.gd` reproduces the app shell's start and failed before this change.

#### 2026-10-21 (W2): The session screen owns pausing

- Decision: the GameHost drops the InputRouter's `pause` action, and its `confirm` while paused, instead of toggling pause on them; `inject_action(&"pause")` and an injected `confirm` still pause and resume (tools, tests, on-screen buttons). A user pause ends only through `resume()`, an injected `pause` or `confirm`; a coasting or `stop_5s` pause also ends with the rider's ride pause (1 s of pedaling), the same rule the session screen applies.
- Alternatives: keep toggling on the router's `pause` (the screen and the GameHost then fight: the screen pauses and opens its menu, the GameHost resumes on the same press).
- Reason: app-shell's W1 request; the session screen calls `pause()` and `resume()` and runs the pause menu, whose confirm must not also resume the session or reach the game.

#### 2026-10-21 (W2): Clients record phase_started for every rider

- Decision: a client's report events get a `phase_started` entry for every rider: its own from its `phase` message, the others from the host's `wl.phase_started` events (the bus already emitted both).
- Alternatives: only the client's own rider (W1).
- Reason: qa's e2e compares event sequences across peers and expects `phase_started` per rider on every peer.

#### 2026-10-21 (W2): content_schemes admit content ids outside `contents`

- Decision: `game.json` may list `content_schemes` (lower-case scheme names, default `[]`). A content id `<scheme>:<value>` with a listed scheme and a non-empty value starts a session although it is not in `contents`; `WlGameRegistry.content_allowed(manifest, id)` is the one check (GameHost setup, `GameSimRunner --content`), the id reaches `ctx.content_id` unchanged on every peer through `session_start`, and the game validates the value when it loads the content. Workout lists `zwo` for `zwo:<absolute path>` (BRIEF 3.7 Workout Director).
- Alternatives: accept any id containing `:` for every game; let Workout list every possible file.
- Reason: game-workout's W1 request; an opt-in per game keeps a typo like `--content=eas:30` an error for every other game.

#### 2026-10-21 (W2): The warm-up window of the PlanValidator is [0, 300) s

- Decision: the warm-up rule covers phases that start in the half-open window [0, 300) s (open-ended phases at `min_s`); a phase that starts at exactly 300 s is outside it. This was already the code (`starts[i] >= 300 - 1e-6` ends the scan) and `test_warmup_allows_65_exactly_and_work_from_300_s` covers it; the doc comment now says so.
- Alternatives: a closed window [0, 300], which would cap every Workout session's first work phase at 65 %.
- Reason: game-workout's W1 request (every Workout session but `e2e` starts its work at exactly 300 s); BRIEF 4.6.8's "first 5 minutes" end at 300 s, and the governor enforces its own run-time warm-up independently.

#### 2026-10-21 (W2): A GameHost steps itself unless told not to

- Decision: `config.realtime` now defaults to true, headless or not: `_process` advances the SimClock by the wall-clock delta × `sim_speed` and steps the session. GameSimRunner, the conformance run and the tests, which call `step()` themselves, pass `realtime: false` (they all did already).
- Alternatives: default to `not headless` (W1); have the app shell's scripted peer call `step()`.
- Reason: the app shell gives a headless scripted peer's GameHost no `realtime` key, so in the real e2e run no GameHost ever advanced: the host printed `running` from `start()`, and each client's GameHost never processed the `session_start` it was handed, so the clients stayed in the lobby (qa W1).

#### 2026-10-21 (W2 round 2): config.platform_pause hands pausing to the app

- Decision: `GameHost.setup()` takes `platform_pause` (bool, default true). True keeps the W2 rule (an injected `pause` toggles, a `confirm` resumes and is consumed, the rider's ride pause ending resumes a pause that was not the rider's own). False: the GameHost never pauses or resumes on actions or gestures, consumes `pause` unused and passes `confirm` to the game; only `pause()`/`resume()` change it. `LocalRider.pause_requested` (the governor's 4.6.4 coasting pause and SOLO `stop_5s`, the pause on not pedaling) pauses in both. The router's `pause`, and its `confirm` while paused, stay dropped in both. `config.realtime` and `config.input_router` keep their keys and meaning; they are now documented in `docs/GAME_SDK.md`.
- Alternatives: make the app strip `confirm`/`pedal_start` from what it hands the GameHost (it can't strip the rider's own gestures headless, and it would still race the GameHost's resume); flip the default (would change GameSimRunner, tools and every test).
- Reason: the app's session screen owns pause and its overlay through `AppSessionInput`; with the GameHost also resuming on `pedal_start`, the two can disagree about who ended a pause. The app passes `platform_pause: false` (app-shell request).

#### 2026-10-21 (W2 round 2): mode SIM during a running block is a confirmation on the host too

- Decision: the host's `DirectiveRouter` opens a SIM block only for mode SIM in an ERG phase while no block runs. The same request while a block runs is a confirmation, as the `DirectiveSink` contract and the governor say: accepted whatever its `duration_s`, no length, safety or spacing re-check, no switch, the block keeps its own end, and the request's other channels apply as inside the block (a haptic subject to the haptic rules: refused in ERG and for an opted-out rider on the host; warm-up and the 15 s cooldown are the governor's). A refused ack for a confirmation never takes the running block back (only the opening request's ack can). `test_sim_block_spacing_and_the_bracketing_exemption` asserted the old refusal of a second mode SIM 0 s after the entry (`switch_spacing`); it now asserts the confirmation, and the 0 s spacing check moved to a new block right after an early exit.
- Alternatives: keep refusing (`sim_block_length` / `switch_spacing`) and document "send haptics inside a block without mode" (docs/TRAINER.md known difference 7); make the governor refuse too (it would contradict the contract every game brief reads).
- Reason: lead W2 round 2. A game that tags its haptics with mode SIM (Tank Crew's hits) lost them on the host though the rider's governor would have applied them.

#### 2026-10-21 (W4): Clients re-apply nothing they already ride; rejoins and resyncs resend bands

- Decision: a client ignores a `plan` identical to the one it rides (compared as `EffortPlan.to_dict()`) and a `phase` for the index it rides with the same `start_tick` (it only catches its phase clock up from the message's new `elapsed_s`). A different plan resets the client's phase to -1, so the next `phase` enters one. Rejoins, resync requests and the host's return (`WlNetSession.resync_requested`) all resend the same set: plan, phase with `elapsed_s`, the latest snapshot, the power and cadence bands the host holds for the rider, and the results if any. A client entering a phase with `elapsed_s` > 0 calls `LocalRider.resume_phase(index, elapsed_s)` when the rider has it, else `start_phase(index)`.
- Alternatives: compute a resume offset on the client from the snapshot tick (the snapshot arrives after the phase); let games republish their bands in `on_afk(peer, false)` (every game would have to, and a cadence band the platform holds would still be lost).
- Reason: W3 NET-2 (every rejoin restarted the governor's phase, the ramp and course following, and wrote an extra FIT lap) and WO-4 (a restarted client lost its Rolling road band for the rest of the phase). The governor cannot yet enter a phase at an offset; that is requested from its owner, and until then a restarted client starts its phase from 0 (downward load, the safe side).

#### 2026-10-21 (W4): Desync warnings come from clients, with an expected-gap exception

- Decision: a client logs `push_warning("GameHost: desync: ...")`, counts it (`desync_count`, the report's `desync_warnings`) and asks for a resync (`telemetry.resync`, at most every 2 s; the host answers at most once a second per peer) when an event batch skips a `seq`, or when two rosters in a row show another phase index for its rider (the roster now carries `phase`). The first gap after the client's own rejoin (net state back to joined) or after the host's return (`peer_joined` of the host) is expected and only recorded. Snapshot `seq` gaps are not checked.
- Alternatives: warn on every gap, outages included (the e2e restart would fail its own "no desync warnings" check); compare a state hash in snapshots (every game would need a canonical hash); check snapshot gaps (the send budget legitimately drops superseded snapshots, and each carries the whole state).
- Reason: W3 NET-5/QT-1: nothing emitted a desync warning, so BRIEF 7.7's e2e assertion could not fail, while lost event batches (an oversize drop, a rate-limited frame) and lost phase messages went unnoticed.

#### 2026-10-21 (W4): A client whose net session ends for good rides its cool-down, then ends

- Decision: on any terminal refusal of its `WlNetSession` (`host_left`, `dropped` after the 120 s rejoin window, `room_not_found`, ...) during a session without results, a client GameHost enters its plan's cool-down locally (unless it is in it already), rides it to its end, then calls `end_session(reason)` and emits `session_finished(null)`. Before it has a plan or a phase it ends at once.
- Alternatives: end at once and let the app put the rider on SIM flat (no cool-down, and the app ended the session only for `host_left`); ride SIM flat until the rider acts (a zero-input rider never gets out).
- Reason: W3 SAF-5: a client whose host vanished held its last interval's ERG target indefinitely. BRIEF 3.1 pillar 4 ("warm-up and recovery built in") and the zero-input rule. The app's session screen still ends a `host_left` session itself at once; it is asked to treat the other reasons alike.

#### 2026-10-21 (W4): Plans over 16 KB go in parts of whole phases

- Decision: the GameHost sends a plan whose message would be over 16 384 bytes as several `plan` messages, each a slice of whole phases with the plan's other fields, numbered `part`/`parts` under a `plan_id`; the client joins them (at most 64 parts; a newer `plan_id` drops older parts) and validates the whole plan as before. `NetMessages` is unchanged (the extra fields are optional).
- Alternatives: a compact plan encoding (still has a limit: the importer accepts 24 h workouts); refuse to start such a session (Workout Director is P0); a generic NetSession fragmentation layer (needs a new message type in the frozen contracts).
- Reason: W3 NET-3: an imported `.zwo` of about 124 phases or more never reached the clients; its plan was dropped as oversize at the start and on every rejoin.

#### 2026-10-21 (W4): results.session_id gets a per-session nonce

- Decision: the host's `session_id` is `<game>/<content>/<seed>/<nonce>`, the nonce being the host's UTC start in milliseconds (hex) plus a per-process counter; `setup()` takes `session_nonce` (default that nonce; `""` keeps `<game>/<content>/<seed>`). `GameSimRunner` passes `""`, so `run_game_sim` reports and their `deterministic_hash` stay reproducible. Clients take the host's id from `session_start`.
- Alternatives: document the id as unique per game/content/seed only (the template's documented apply_progress pattern would keep crediting nothing to a second `--seed` session in one profile); the relay room code (one room hosts several sessions).
- Reason: W3 SDK-5. The id is not game state: it never reaches the rules or `ctx.rng`, so reading the wall clock here breaks no determinism rule.

#### 2026-10-21 (W4): A remote rider's applied record is completed from the routed request

- Decision: the DirectiveRouter keeps each routed remote request (at most 512) until its ack arrives; the GameHost fills the ack's missing `requested`, and `t`, `game_t` and `phase_index` as of routing when the ack has none, so the report's `applied` record has the local record's shape. Only `request` entries get an `applied` record; GAME_SDK 19.1 and Q10 say so.
- Alternatives: send the requested dict back in every ack (more telemetry bytes for data the host already has).
- Reason: W3 SDK-4.

#### 2026-10-21 (W4): ctx.directives and ctx.hud hold their LocalRiders privately

- Decision: `DirectiveRouter.riders` and `HudRouter.riders` became `_riders`, set once through the write-only `bind_riders()`; nothing hands a LocalRider to a game through `ctx`.
- Alternatives: keep them public and rely on the arch gate alone.
- Reason: W3 SAF-4 (defence in depth for the coming game sessions; extending the arch gate belongs to its owner).

#### 2026-10-21 (W4 round 2): GameHost holds its LocalRiders privately behind local_rider(peer)

- Decision: `GameHost.local_riders` became `_local_riders`; the platform and tests read one rider through `GameHost.local_rider(peer) -> LocalRider`, and the `config` the GameHost keeps no longer holds the `local_riders` map. The `local_riders` setup key is unchanged.
- Alternatives: keep the public map and rely on the arch gate flagging `.local_riders` (a game reaches the GameHost through `get_parent()` without naming it); a read-only copy (still hands out the LocalRiders).
- Reason: W4r2 R3 (W3 SAF-4): the GameModule is the GameHost's child, so `get_parent().local_riders[peer]` gave any game a LocalRider whose `setup()`, `set_plan()` and `start_phase()` lift 4.6 rules. The accessor's name is one the no-bypass gate already forbids under `src/games/` (`.local_rider`); the trainer fixer extends the gate for the rest. This is a change to a W0 public field, made at the lead's request; nothing outside the sdk and its tests read it.

#### 2026-10-21 (W4 round 2): A client resumes a phase it enters at the host's elapsed time

- Decision: a client entering a phase from a `phase` message with `elapsed_s` > 0 (a restarted client, a phase that changed while its socket or the host was away, a resync) calls `LocalRider.resume_phase(index, elapsed_s)` with the host's elapsed time, through a `has_method` guard until trainer-core's method is merged (then a direct call), else `start_phase(index)`. FIT laps are `resume_phase`'s (a real phase change laps, a restarted client's first phase does not), so the GameHost writes none itself. A `phase` message for the phase the client already rides (same index and start_tick) still only catches its phase clock up.
- Alternatives: the GameHost writes the lap for a phase that changed while away (trainer-core's `resume_phase` already does, so it would lap twice); send a fresh `start_phase` and patch the governor's clock afterwards (the ramp and course would jump).
- Reason: W4r2 R8 (W3 NET-2): a restarted client's governor started a 300 s ERG ramp over at 0 while the HUD showed the host's 122 s, and `course` profiles started over; the same fallback hit a rejoin or the host's return across a phase change.

### sdk-tools

Short ADRs for `src/games/_template/`, `content/_template/`, `assets/_template/`, `tests/games/_template/`, `tools/run_game_sim` (+ `.ps1`, `tools/sdk/`), `tools/new_game` (+ `.ps1`, `tools/lib/new_game.mjs`), `tests/tools/`, `tools/tests/new_game.test.mjs`, `tools/tests/run_game_sim.test.mjs` and `docs/GAME_SDK.md`.

- **2026-10-21: The template's string keys start with `_TEMPLATE_`.** Its id is `_template`, so the BRIEF 6 rule (upper-case id plus `_`) and the registry's computed defaults (`<ID>_OPT_<OPTION ID>`, `<ID>_CONTENT_<CONTENT ID>`) both give `_TEMPLATE_`, and the conformance test's prefix check passes without a special case. `tools/new_game` rewrites `_TEMPLATE_` and a bare `TEMPLATE_` to `<ID>_`. *Alternatives:* `TEMPLATE_` keys (the task text's spelling), which a generic prefix check would reject. *Reason:* one rule for every game, the template included.
- **2026-10-21: The template seats 1–4 riders with bots, one marker each.** BRIEF 3.6's "one rider pushes a marker" is read per rider. *Alternatives:* `max_players` 1. *Reason:* conformance runs every game at `min_players` and `max_players`, so a multi-rider template exercises HOST, CLIENT, snapshots and events, and a game scaffolded from it starts multiplayer-ready.
- **2026-10-21: Two contents.** `default` is BRIEF 3.6's session (a 300 s warm-up ramp 50 → 65 %, 180 s WORK at 75 % with `range_pct` [75, 80]); `short` is a 2-minute `e2e_content` inside the warm-up limits whose second phase is kind WARMUP (no offset, no nudge), like Siege's `drill`. `first_run_priority` is −1 so a scaffolded game never takes the first run by accident. *Reason:* `game.json` requires an `e2e_content`, and copies must be valid games.
- **2026-10-21: The template's effort nudge comes from the rules, never from Autopilot.** A +5 % "hill" at WORK elapsed 60 s for 30 s, inside `range_pct`, expiring back to the plan; boosts (Autopilot, surge, confirm, the HUD button) only change the marker's speed. *Reason:* BRIEF 3.5: Autopilot never changes effort targets.
- **2026-10-21: The 300-line budget counts the template's `.gd` files plus `game.json`** (296 lines now) and is checked by `tests/tools/test_template_size.gd`, which `tools/new_game` never copies, so scaffolded games may grow. Markdown files (README, HARDWARE_TEST) don't count.
- **2026-10-21: Template unit tests run on recording fakes; integration tests wait for the runtime.** `tests/games/_template/template_fakes.gd` stands in for the sinks and the GameHost's phase clock, so the module is tested in W1 while sdk-runtime builds in parallel. `tests/games/_template/integration/` drives the real registry, validator and `GameSimRunner`; each test is `pending` while those are W0 stubs and runs for real from W2. *Alternatives:* leave them failing until W2. *Reason:* the full suite stays green whatever the merge order, and nothing is weakened once the runtime exists.
- **2026-10-21: run_game_sim's logic lives in `GameSimCli` (`tools/sdk/game_sim_cli.gd`).** `tools/sdk/run_game_sim_main.gd` (extends SceneTree) only calls it on the first frame, once every autoload is ready. `GameSimCli` takes its flag parser, runner factory and environment reader as Callables, so GUT tests drive it with fakes. *Reason:* a SceneTree script can't be unit-tested in-process; the task asks for flag tests with a fake runner.
- **2026-10-21: run_game_sim checks flag names and syntax before `GameSimRunner.parse_args`.** It knows every BRIEF 3.6 flag, refuses unknown flags, missing values, repeats (only `--rider` repeats), a `--rider` count that is neither 1 nor `--players`, non-numeric values and non-JSON `--options`/`--player-options`, and an unreadable `--override` file, naming the flag. Values that need the manifest (option ranges, content ids) stay with `parse_args` and `setup`. *Reason:* a typo like `--player=2` must not silently run a default session; the checks are tested now, while `parse_args` is a stub.
- **2026-10-21: Exit codes 0 / 2 / 3 / 4.** 0 for a completed run with any `end_reason` (the report says which), 2 for bad flags or missing locks, 3 for setup errors (the validator's message included), 4 when the report can't be written; the Godot wrapper's 75 and 124 pass through.
- **2026-10-21: Relative `--report` and `--override` paths resolve against the caller's directory.** The wrappers pass `--caller-cwd=<pwd>`, because Godot changes its working directory to `--path` (checked with a probe: a relative path would land in the repo). MSYS turns `/c/...` into `C:/...` for Godot; PowerShell passes its provider path.
- **2026-10-21: The locks are checked twice.** The wrappers export `WATTLANDS_NO_HW_CONTROL=1` and `WATTLANDS_NO_NETWORK_INTEGRATIONS=1`, refuse `--allow-hw` and `--allow-network` and always add `--mock-trainer`; the entry script refuses to run unless both variables are 1 and `--mock-trainer` is present (BRIEF 1: the game-sim script passes `--mock-trainer` and refuses to run without it). *Reason:* starting the entry script by hand can't bypass the locks.
- **2026-10-21: `tools/run_game_sim.ps1` starts `tools/godot.ps1` in its own `powershell -File` process** through `Invoke-WattlandsNative`. *Alternative:* `& godot.ps1 @args`. *Reason:* `-File` passes every argument untouched (a bare `--`, JSON with quotes); tested with `--options` and `--player-options` JSON from Node.
- **2026-10-21: `--echo-args`.** Prints the flags exactly as the entry script would pass them to `parse_args` (after path resolution) and the lock values, then exits 0 without running. It makes the whole wrapper → Godot chain testable without the runtime and helps debug quoting.
- **2026-10-21: run_game_sim points `Paths.user_dir()` at a fresh temp dir for the run and removes it afterwards** (`isolate_user_dir`), on top of the runner's per-slot temp profiles (OWNERSHIP SIM7). *Reason:* nothing in a simulation can touch the user's real profile, progress or rides.
- **2026-10-21: new_game never refuses over the docs fragments (OWNERSHIP C17).** Only `src/games/<id>/`, `content/<id>/`, `assets/<id>/`, `tests/games/<id>/` and `.github/workflows/game-<id>.yml` (which it writes) trigger the refusal; `docs/{decisions,credits.d,changelog.d}/<id>.md` are created only when missing and never overwritten. *Alternative:* the task text lists the fragments among the refusal targets. *Reason:* Siege's and Tank's G0 may write `docs/decisions/<id>.md` before running `tools/new_game`; C17 resolves this conflict and the task's test list cites it.
- **2026-10-21: new_game's rewriting rules match at word starts only,** in this order: `_TEMPLATE_` → `<ID>_`, `TEMPLATE_` → `<ID>_`, `_template` → `<id>`, `Template` → `<Id>` (PascalCase of the id: `big_fish` → `BigFish`), `template_` → `<id>_`, applied to file contents and names. *Reason:* `test_template_rules.gd` must become `test_<id>_rules.gd` (a plain `_template` replacement gave `testdemo_rules.gd`), and helper names like `_is_template` must survive. The title becomes the id in words (`Big Fish`).
- **2026-10-21: new_game skips `.uid`, `.import` and `.translation` files** (all generated by the import) and strips `uid="uid://..."` attributes from `.tscn`/`.tres`. *Reason:* copied uids would collide with the template's; a copied `.translation` would hold the template's keys until the next import. Exit 1 for a refusal, 2 for a usage error (no id or a bad id), nothing written in either case; writes are planned in memory first and rolled back if one fails.
- **2026-10-21: new_game writes the game's CI workflow** (`tools/run_tests --only=gut --gdir=res://tests/games/<id>`, the script check, Godot 4.7.2 on ubuntu-latest), as OWNERSHIP TL1 recommends; the game session owns and extends it.
- **2026-10-21: GAME_SDK.md marks other modules' details with "W2:".** Where the doc must name a value another W1 builder decides in parallel (the run_game_sim report keys and flag defaults against `tests/sdk/test_sim_report.gd`, HudLayer's CanvasLayer layer and Theme path, widget minimum rects, `intervals`/`noise` profile keys, the fit_check and e2e JSON schemas, the Workout examples), it says so instead of guessing; the lead resolves them in W2.
- **2026-10-21 (hardening review): run_game_sim exits 5 when Godot logged a SCRIPT ERROR during the run.** GDScript ends a function that hits a script error and returns its type's default, so `GameSimCli.run()` (or a broken `setup()`) could hand back 0 and the process would exit 0 with no report or a wrong one (checked with a probe). `GameSimCli.main()` registers a `Logger` (`OS.add_logger`, Godot 4.5+) that counts `ERROR_TYPE_SCRIPT` only, and turns an exit 0 into 5, naming the first error; any other non-zero code wins, and the report is still written when the run got that far. `push_error`/`push_warning` never count: they report bad input. *Alternatives:* exit 0 and only print a warning (a broken game would pass its simulation matrix); detect only an aborted `run()` (misses errors inside the session). *Reason:* BRIEF 7 treats any SCRIPT ERROR as a failure; the task's "exit 0 for any end_reason" still holds for clean runs.
- **2026-10-21 (hardening review): each run first deletes the report and its `fit/<stem>_slot<n>.fit` files at its `--report` path.** *Alternatives:* leave old files (a run that fails at setup, after a tuning change in a G2 balance loop, would leave the previous run's report for `check_sims.mjs` to pass on). *Reason:* a failed run must never look like a passed one. `--echo-args` deletes nothing; only files matching that exact pattern are touched.
