- **2026-10-21 (hardening review): the wrappers pass `--mock-trainer=erg`, not a bare flag.** CmdLine reads a bare flag as the value "true", and autoloads that honour `--mock-trainer=<profile>` (TrainerService, App) would see an invalid spec in a run_game_sim process. `erg` is a valid profile; the riders still ride their own `--rider` profiles.
- **2026-10-21 (hardening review): run_game_sim checks slot lists against the slots** (`--players` plus `--bots` at most `Wl.MAX_SLOTS`; `--autopilot`/`--haptics-off` slots below `--players`; at most one `--ftp` value per slot and one `--player-options` object per human slot) and hands `--unverified` to `parse_args` as a bare flag (`--unverified=false` drops it). *Reason:* these need no manifest, so the CLI can name the flag before any Godot work.
- **2026-10-21 (hardening review): new_game refuses ids whose class names already exist.** Two ids can share a class prefix (`big_fish` and `big__fish` give `BigFish`, `a_1b` and `a1b` give `A1b`) and the id `template` gives `TemplateGame`; the target-path check misses all of these. It scans `class_name` lines under `addons/`, `src/`, `tests/` and `tools/` (skipping `.godot`, `build`, `node_modules`, ...). *Alternative:* forbid `__`, trailing `_` and digit-led segments in ids (narrower than OWNERSHIP TL1's `^[a-z][a-z0-9_]*$`, and still misses `template`).
- **2026-10-21 (hardening review): the template validates every content phase at load** (kind, never COOLDOWN, `target_pct`, `duration_s` > 0, `label`, optional `ramp_to_pct`) and every tuning number, so bad content or a bad `--override` patch is refused with a reason instead of a script error in `build_effort_plans`. Numbers may be JSON floats, GDScript ints or numeric strings. The package test's wall-clock/global-RNG scan skips views, which may seed their own cosmetic RNG from `ctx.seed` (Fishing 3.5); the template still has 298 lines.
- **2026-10-21 (W2): GAME_SDK.md's W2 placeholders are filled from the merged code, and the report section follows `build_report()`.** The report table now lists the keys `GameSimRunner.build_report()` writes and `tests/sdk/test_sim_runner.gd` pins (`trace` with rows `{t, game_t, phase, ...}`, events `{t, pt, type, ...}` with game events under `game_event`, `snapshots` `{seq, tick, sha256, bytes}`, `clients`, `unlocks`, ...); the W1 draft had `traces` and other guessed keys. `--sim-speed` is documented as recorded but not pacing headless runs (they always step as fast as they can), and SOLO report runs as measuring snapshots. *Alternatives:* rename the runner's keys to the draft's. *Reason:* qa's scenarios, Workout's sims and the runner's tests already read the code's keys; the code is the contract's final word.
- **2026-10-21 (W2): `tools/run_game_sim --game=_template` runs the template.** The template is never listed, so the GameRegistry autoload refused it (exit 3, and `tools/tests/run_game_sim.test.mjs`'s real session failed once the W2 runtime merged). For `--game=_template` the CLI hands `GameSimRunner` a private registry with the installed games plus the template (as the conformance test loads it), freed after the run; it is still never listed anywhere. *Alternative:* point the Node test at Workout. *Reason:* the template is the game every session studies first; running it through the real tool is part of that.

#### 2026-10-21 (W4): The template shows course_grade, replan, end_phase and a banner in "short"

- Decision: the template's `default` content keeps the BRIEF 3.6 shape (5-minute warm-up, one 3-minute WORK phase). Its `short` content (the e2e content) adds a 40 s SIM "Rollers" phase fed by `ctx.directives.course_grade`, and at its start a `ctx.directives.replan` of the rest into an open-ended "Finish" that `ctx.directives.end_phase` ends once the rider holds the target after `min_s`. Slot 0 announces each phase with `ctx.hud.banner`. `finished` now waits until every rider is in the cool-down: riders reach it at different times after an open-ended phase, and an earlier `finished` ends the others' phases. The template stays under 300 lines (299).
- Alternatives: put the calls in `default` (breaks the BRIEF's fixed shape); a third content (more lines in game.json and the strings).
- Reason: W3 SDK-7 (BRIEF 3.6: the template "shows every SDK call").

#### 2026-10-21 (W4): A freed GameSimRunner deletes its temp root

- Decision: `GameSimRunner` deletes its `wattlands_sim_*` temp root on PREDELETE too, and `run_game_sim` calls `cleanup()` before freeing the runner; the scenario, Workout sim and template session tests clean their runners up in `after_each`.
- Reason: W3 QT-4: every `run_game_sim` run and the qa scenario tests left a profile dir under OS temp.

#### 2026-10-21 (W4 round 2): The template banners each rider's own phase and reports the group's phase

- Decision: the template sends its phase banner to each human rider's own peer when that rider's phase changes (`banner(key, args, 3.0, peer)`, none to bots), and emits `phase_changed(n)` once every rider reached phase n (the lowest index any rider is in, from the per-rider indexes `_phase_rules` already keeps). The template stays under 300 lines (299 with game.json, `tests/tools/test_template_size.gd`).
- Alternatives: keep slot 0 speaking for everyone and drop the open-ended Finish from `short` (loses the end_phase example); broadcast the banner of the leading rider (still wrong for the others); drop `phase_changed` from the template (Siege and Tank copy the template and do emit it).
- Reason: W4r2 R12: after `short`'s open-ended Finish the riders' phases differ (end_phase vs max_s), so slot 0's "Cool-down" banner reached riders still in Finish for 18 s and nobody got a banner when their own Finish ended. GAME_SDK 10.2 already warns against broadcasting one rider's prompt with the default peer.

### world-kits

#### 2026-10-21: PixelViewport scales in device pixels

- Decision: `integer_scale()` is the whole number of device pixels per art pixel, chosen from the window's real size (the root viewport's stretch transform), so every art pixel is exactly k screen pixels. `art_scale()` (added) is the same scale in canvas units, `letterbox_offset()` is in the control's canvas units snapped to whole device pixels, and `art_rect_to_screen()` returns the viewport's canvas coordinates (what a HUD Control uses). Headless runs measure device scale 1, so there `integer_scale() == art_scale()` (3 at 1920 x 1080). `screen_scale_override` pins the device scale for tests and nested viewports.
- Alternatives: pick the integer scale in canvas units (the 1920 x 1080 `canvas_items` base).
- Reason: the window stretches a 1920 x 1080 canvas. Integer scale in canvas units is pixel-perfect only on 16:9 windows of 720p, 1080p and 1440p; at 1366 x 768, 1536 x 864 or 1600 x 900 it gives uneven 2.13x to 2.5x pixels. Device pixels keep "integer scaling to the window" (BRIEF 3.9) true everywhere, with bars instead. A windowed run at 1280 x 720 reports scale 2, art scale 3, no bars.

#### 2026-10-21: PixelViewport keeps its SubViewport out of the container's own drawing

- Decision: the frozen stub extends `SubViewportContainer`, which draws direct SubViewport children unscaled at the origin. The SubViewport (nearest default filter, no repeat, 2D transform and vertex snapping, 3D disabled, GUI input off) is a child of an internal `TextureRect` that shows its texture at the integer-scaled, centred rect; `_draw()` fills the bars. A `CanvasLayer` at -128 inside the SubViewport clears it to `background_color`.
- Alternatives: `stretch = true` with `stretch_shrink` (forces the container's size to a multiple of the art size and can't letterbox); rendering the SubViewport at full resolution with `size_2d_override` (rotated sprites would lose their pixel look).
- Reason: keeps the frozen class and gives exact letterboxing with one extra node.

#### 2026-10-21: Haze uses Environment depth fog on 4.7.2 Compatibility

- Decision: `SkyAmbient` turns on `Environment` depth fog (`FOG_MODE_DEPTH`, begin 120 m, end 380 m, density 0.55, no sky fog) by default. `SkyAmbient.haze_mesh(lp, camera_origin)` is the one haze helper games call on every `LowPolyMesh`: a no-op while fog is used, `LowPolyMesh.apply_haze()` (vertex colors blended toward the haze color by horizontal distance) when `haze_mode()` is `"vertex"` (forced with `haze_vertex_fallback`, or on an engine without depth fog).
- Alternatives: always blend vertex colors (Siege S3's fallback).
- Reason: a windowed Compatibility render on Godot 4.7.2 (NVIDIA, OpenGL 3.3) showed a red unshaded box 100 m away turn from (1.0, 0.0, 0.0) to (0.58, 0.0, 0.86) with blue depth fog, and to (0.40, 0.0, 0.94) with exponential fog, so fog works there. Fog follows the camera and needs no mesh rebuilds.

#### 2026-10-21: SkyAmbient light energies calibrated for Compatibility

- Decision: defaults sun 0.3 (color `#ffeccc`, 48 degrees up, from the front left of a camera looking toward -Z, orthogonal shadows to 400 m) and flat ambient 0.35 (`#d4d9de`), linear tonemap, SSAO, SSIL, SDFGI and glow off.
- Alternatives: physically sensible energies (sun ~1, ambient ~0.5).
- Reason: on 4.7.2 Compatibility the shadowed directional light is added in a separate pass that blends in non-linear space, so sun 0.9 plus ambient 0.5 turned ochre `#c7a15a` ground into `#ffd65f`. With these values a windowed probe measured lit flat ground `#ca9c4c` (the palette color), the same ground in shadow at about half (`#65532d`) and lit marble `#ede1c6` for `#e8e2d4`. The values are plain properties; a Forward+ scene would raise them.

#### 2026-10-21: Canvas shaders carry modulate through a varying

- Decision: `palette_swap.gdshader` and `sprite_wobble.gdshader` copy `COLOR` (vertex color times modulate) into a varying in `vertex()` and multiply their own texture sample by it.
- Alternatives: `COLOR = sample * COLOR` in `fragment()`.
- Reason: in Godot 4 `fragment()`'s input `COLOR` already includes the texture sample, so that applies the texture twice; a windowed probe showed a swapped blue at (0.0, 0.08, 0.16) instead of (0.0, 0.45, 0.70). With the varying the four slot colors render exactly.

#### 2026-10-21: PaletteSwap uses eight separate key and swap uniforms

- Decision: `key_0`..`key_7`, `swap_0`..`swap_7`, `key_count`, `tolerance`; the helper fills them from ramps (light to dark) and builds slot ramps from `Wl.slot_look()`.
- Alternatives: `vec4` uniform arrays; a lookup texture.
- Reason: every value shows in the inspector and `get_shader_parameter()`; 8 keys cover any ramp the games need (Fishing uses 2).

#### 2026-10-21: MultiMesh custom data always comes with colors

- Decision: `MultiMeshScatter.setup(..., use_custom = true)` also turns on per-instance colors (white unless given). The walk-bob shader writes `ALBEDO = COLOR.rgb` and, like `StandardMaterial3D` with `vertex_color_is_srgb`, decodes the sRGB vertex colors only when `OUTPUT_IS_SRGB` is false.
- Alternatives: custom data alone; always decoding the vertex colors in the shader.
- Reason: on 4.7.2 Compatibility a MultiMesh with custom data but no colors drew wrong vertex colors (brown figures came out green). Compatibility outputs sRGB (`OUTPUT_IS_SRGB`), so there `COLOR` already matches what `LowPolyMesh`'s `StandardMaterial3D` (vertex color as sRGB albedo) shows, and decoding it again darkened it. The guard keeps walking figures matching static props on a linear renderer too.

#### 2026-10-21: PixelViewport also emits `resized` on layout changes at a fixed size

- Decision: when the layout changes while the control keeps its size (the window was resized at the fixed 1920 x 1080 canvas, so only the device scale moved, or `pixel_base` changed), `PixelViewport` emits `resized` after `layout_changed`. A real size change keeps Control's own single `resized`.
- Alternatives: only `layout_changed`, and ask ui-hud to listen to it.
- Reason: the frozen W0 API has no layout signal, so `HudLayer` re-lays out on the control's `resized`. A 1920 x 1080 window shrunk to 1600 x 900 keeps the canvas size but moves the image from scale 3 to scale 2 with a 192 x 108 letterbox; without the extra `resized` the HUD would stay where it was. A windowed 1600 x 900 run reports scale 2, art scale 2.4, offset (192, 108).

#### 2026-10-21: LowPolyMesh is a builder of triangle lists per surface

- Decision: primitives append flat, outward-facing triangles (winding fixed by comparing SurfaceTool's normal `(a - c) x (a - b)` with the face's outward direction) to per-surface arrays; `append()` merges meshes per surface key (material), handling mirrored transforms; `build()` runs SurfaceTool with smooth group -1, vertex colors and the shared vertex-color material, one surface per material. Primitives are centred like Godot's primitive meshes; colors are always explicit arguments.
- Alternatives: committing each primitive to SurfaceTool directly; Godot's PrimitiveMesh classes.
- Reason: ground darkening, haze and merging need the vertices before commit; one surface per material is what keeps a Siege-sized scene within ~250 draw calls.

#### 2026-10-21: Parts animate from _process by default, tick(dt) for sim-driven views

- Decision: `SpinningPart`, `SlidingPart`, `ScrollingChain`, `ParallaxStrip` drift and `MiniatureCameraRig` advance through `tick(dt)`; `auto_process` (default on) calls it from `_process(delta)`. `ParallaxStrip` scrolls only from the `world_x` the view sets. Shaders use `TIME` unless `set_time()` gives them sim time.
- Alternatives: everything driven from sim time only.
- Reason: views are presentation (they only read state), so render-rate animation is fine and needs no wiring; tests, screenshots and replays drive `tick()` for deterministic frames.

#### 2026-10-21: Pixel import preset is validation plus project defaults

- Decision: `PixelImportPreset` documents and checks the preset: required `compress/mode` 0 (lossless) and `mipmaps/generate` false, recommended `detect_3d/compress_to` 0 and no size limit. The project's `[importer_defaults] texture` (lead) gives new PNGs the required half; `write_import_file()` patches or creates a `.import` for folders that need it pinned; filtering is set per CanvasItem (`PixelViewport` makes nearest the default; `apply_to()` elsewhere).
- Alternatives: pixelate writes hand-made `.import` files for every sprite.
- Reason: OWNERSHIP C36; Godot 4 has no per-texture filter setting, and hand-written `.import` files without uids churn on every import.

### ui-hud

Short ADRs for `addons/wattlands/ui/`, `addons/wattlands/audio/`, `tests/ui/` and `tests/audio/` (BRIEF 3.5, 3.9, 4.6.3, 4.6.5, 4.6.8).

#### 2026-10-21: Godot's default font; Atkinson Hyperlegible is a drop-in

- Decision: the W1 build ships no font file. `WlTheme` uses Atkinson Hyperlegible when `addons/wattlands/ui/theme/fonts/AtkinsonHyperlegible-Regular.ttf` and `-Bold.ttf` exist, otherwise `ThemeDB.fallback_font`. Dropping both files in (plus `OFL.txt`, a `docs/deps.d/atkinson-hyperlegible.md` entry with source, version and SHA-256, and a credit) switches the Theme and every kit widget over with no code change.
- Alternatives: download the font from Google Fonts or the Braille Institute release during the build.
- Reason: a file download needs explicit approval, which that session did not have, and BRIEF 3.9 names Godot's default font as the fallback. The readability rules (sizes, outlines, contrast) don't depend on the font.

#### 2026-10-21: Synthesized sound only; no CC0 pack

- Decision: every built-in effect (`ui_move` ... `click`) is synthesized at first use by `SfxSynth` (16-bit mono PCM `AudioStreamWAV`, 22 050 Hz, linear envelopes, a fixed-seed xorshift for noise). Games register their own clips; `synth(..., loop = true)` gives looping clips whose pitch a game may change live.
- Alternatives: a CC0 pack (Kenney and similar).
- Reason: same download rule as the font; BRIEF 3.9 makes synthesis the fallback. Synthesis is deterministic, tiny and silent-safe headless.

#### 2026-10-21: Input router: edge-detected actions, panic hold and chord

- Decision: device actions fire once per press, edge-detected per key, button or stick direction (key repeats ignored), into `take_actions()`, the `action` signal, `EventBus.action_pressed` and `menu_input(action, "action")`. A keyboard binding of `panic` (Backspace) fires after 1.0 s held, timed by `tick(dt)` from `_process`. A controller binding of `panic` (View) never fires alone: it fires at once together with a controller binding of `pause` (Menu), and Menu pressed while View is held gives panic instead of pause. Panic emits `EventBus.panic_requested` at once and queues `&"panic"`. If `pause` has no controller binding, a controller `panic` binding falls back to the 1 s hold.
- Alternatives: View alone held 1 s also panics; polling `Input.is_action_pressed`.
- Reason: BRIEF 3.5 names View plus Menu together; a lone View press mid-ride must not drop the trainer to safe mode. Edge detection keeps stick motion from flooding the queue.

#### 2026-10-21: Gestures never become actions; menus get a mapped `menu_input`

- Decision: gestures from the attached `LocalRider.gesture_detected` go to `take_gestures()`, the `gesture` signal and `EventBus.gesture` only. Menus listen to the added `menu_input(action, via)` signal, which carries device actions and the pedal navigation mapping: `surge` -> confirm and `coast` -> back (only while `pedal_nav_active()`, and `coast` never in ERG), `stop_5s` -> pause (only when `solo` and not in ERG). `pause` and `panic` stay in `take_actions()`; the GameHost filters `WlInputRouter.PLATFORM_ACTIONS` before `local_intents`.
- Alternatives: inject `confirm` into the action queue on a surge.
- Reason: docs/OWNERSHIP.md GM8 and F2.5: a surge must never also arrive as `confirm` in sessions.

#### 2026-10-21: Bindings are plain data in a versioned input.cfg

- Decision: `input.cfg` (ConfigFile at `Paths.input_cfg_path()`) holds `[input] version = 1` and `[bindings] <action> = [{"type": "key", "physical_keycode": ...}, ...]`. `rebind()` replaces the action's bindings of the same family (keyboard or controller) and removes that input from every other action; `add_binding()` adds a second one. A missing file, a wrong version or an unknown action falls back to the project.godot defaults with a `push_warning`.
- Alternatives: store `InputEvent` objects in the ConfigFile.
- Reason: plain data is readable, stable across Godot versions and safe to load.

#### 2026-10-21: PedalScanList timing

- Decision: scanning steps every `scan_interval_s` (default from the profile, 1.6 s) over enabled items. Up, down, left and right move focus and pause scanning for 10 s. With pedal navigation on, ERG auto-confirm picks the highlighted item `2 x enabled items x scan_interval_s` after the last input or pick, so it lands on the item highlighted when the count began (put the recommended item first), and repeats two cycles later if the list stays open. With it off a list never scans or auto-confirms and ignores surge and coast. `coast` never backs out in ERG. A hint line shows how to pick and the auto-pick countdown.
- Alternatives: count cycles of the highlight itself; stop after one auto-pick.
- Reason: BRIEF 3.5 and docs/OWNERSHIP.md PSL2: picks during ERG must happen without input; Siege expects its recommendation on top.

#### 2026-10-21: HUD layout rules

- Decision: `HudLayer` is CanvasLayer 10 with one stack per anchor, 32 px from the edges; top anchors stack downward, bottom anchors upward (the first entry nearest the edge); kit widgets first, then game Controls, the banner area and the toast area. A Rect2i is mapped through `PixelViewport.art_rect_to_screen()` into a fixed holder of exactly that rect, re-laid out on the PixelViewport's `resized` and the window's `size_changed`. Kit widgets shrink to fit (`fit_to`, never above 1.0) from a documented `base_size()`; key numbers (own W, rpm, big readouts) never drop below 48 px, so a rect below a widget's documented `min_rect()` overflows rather than shrinking them. The gear indicator is not a key number (48 px at full size, shrinks), so Fishing's 639 x 252 px zone fits timeline, gauge, cadence, W' and gear with W and rpm at 48 px (tested).
- Alternatives: scale whole stacks by one factor; Godot anchors with grow directions.
- Reason: deterministic, testable layout; BRIEF 3.9's 48 px rule; docs/OWNERSHIP.md HUD2.

#### 2026-10-21: Raw W and %FTP always on screen; W/kg only by opt-in

- Decision: if a spec has no power gauge (and not both a `power_w` and an `ftp_pct` readout), `HudLayer` adds a gauge at bottom_left (`ensure_power_visible`). W/kg shows when the profile opts in: `RiderProfile.show_wkg` if that field exists, else `share_weight`; `HudLayer.wkg_weight_kg` overrides.
- Alternatives: trust each game's spec.
- Reason: BRIEF 3.3 and pillar 2. The profile has no W/kg display flag yet (requested from platform).

#### 2026-10-21: Safety banners

- Decision: the banner area shows the more urgent of rescue and warm-up (rescue > warm-up), each with its own text and color (orange, purple; never color alone), warm-up smaller. Sources are OR-ed: `set_safety_state()`, `update()`'s telemetry (`rescue`) and the bus (`rescue_changed` and `governor_status` for the local peer). Pause has its own symbol (`HudPauseBadge`). `HudSpec.show_safety_banners = false` hides them.
- Reason: BRIEF 4.6.3, 4.6.8; the GameHost can feed whichever it has.

#### 2026-10-21: Shared Theme as a hand-written .tres plus WlTheme constants

- Decision: `res://addons/wattlands/ui/theme/wattlands_theme.tres` (dark panels, 32 px body, 40 px buttons, 48 px line edits, sky-blue focus) and `WlTheme` (sizes, Okabe-Ito based colors, `label_settings()`, `panel_style()`). Kit widgets use LabelSettings with a dark outline so text reads over any game art.
- Reason: games match the kit by setting the same Theme and using the same constants (HUD11).

#### 2026-10-21: Key presses belong to a focused text field

- Decision: while a LineEdit or TextEdit has focus, the router ignores keyboard presses (no action, no Backspace panic hold); releases and controller input still pass, so the View+Menu panic always works.
- Alternatives: let screens unbind keys while typing.
- Reason: typing a room code fires R (swap), P (pause), Q and E (shifts), and holding Backspace to delete would otherwise trigger panic.

#### 2026-10-21: A client's local teammate_status outlives the roster until the roster changes it

- Decision: `HudTeammates.set_status()` (the local feed's `teammate_status`, which on a client draws only there, HUD8) keeps the status over the roster until the roster's own status for that rider changes; then the roster's newer value wins. `HudLayer` keeps the local statuses across a rebuild, minus any the roster has replaced.
- Alternatives: merge once and let the next roster overwrite it (it vanished within 0.5 s, at the 2 Hz roster); local always wins (a host status set later would never show).
- Reason: the latest call or roster update should show, without clocks. sdk-runtime's `HudRouter` overlays client statuses onto the roster itself; both paths agree.

#### 2026-10-21: HudLayer keeps the latest bands

- Decision: `apply_power_band` and `apply_cadence_band` remember the latest band per peer (cleared bands are forgotten) and apply it to gauges and cadence widgets built later, as custom readout texts already were.
- Alternatives: rely on the game publishing after `build()`.
- Reason: a band published in `configure()` (before the GameHost builds the HUD) or before a rebuild would otherwise never show until the game changes it.

#### 2026-10-21: Rebound keys match by physical key

- Decision: `InputBindings.dict_to_event()` builds key bindings with `physical_keycode` only (keycode 0) when a physical code is known, like project.godot's defaults; a key event without one binds by `keycode`.
- Alternatives: keep both codes (Godot then matches by the logical keycode).
- Reason: a rebound key keeps working when the keyboard layout changes, and `same_input()` (which removes an input from other actions) agrees with how actions match.

#### 2026-10-21: Corner toasts and banners wrap within a third of the screen

- Decision: toasts and banners placed at a corner anchor wrap within `HudLayer.corner_text_width()` (a third of the usable width, 610 px at 1080p); the centre stacks keep the room between the corners.
- Alternatives: no limit (a long toast at top_left, Workout's layout, ran under the centre timeline and squeezed it to its 0.25 floor).
- Reason: corner messages must never cover or shrink the centre widgets.

#### 2026-10-21: HudLayer follows PixelViewport.layout_changed

- Decision: besides the PixelViewport's `resized` and the window's `size_changed`, `HudLayer` re-lays out Rect2i slots on the PixelViewport's `layout_changed` signal when it has one (looked up by name, so the frozen stub without it still works).
- Reason: world-kits re-lays out on a stretch-factor change without a resize and emits only `layout_changed`.

#### 2026-10-21: Controls-screen labels come from ui.csv

- Decision: `InputBindings.describe()` takes its words ("Left stick up", "D-pad up", "Key {0}", "View", "Menu") from `UI_INPUT_*` keys; Xbox letters (A, B, X, Y, LB, RB, LT, RT, LS, RS) and engine key names stay as printed. A test checks that every `UI_*` key used under `addons/wattlands/ui` and `audio` is in `ui.csv`.
- Reason: BRIEF 6, user strings live in `content/strings/<module>.csv`.

#### 2026-10-21: Toasts and banners in a small rect keep a readable size

- Decision: in a Rect2i area, toasts show as many as fit (up to 3) at up to 36 px but never below 24 px (`HudToasts.MIN_FONT_PX`), dropping the oldest instead of shrinking; banners never go below 32 px (`HudBanner.MIN_FONT_PX`, warm-up 28 px) and wrap or overflow the rect instead.
- Alternatives: shrink to fit three toasts (a 90 px toast strip gave 16 px text) and banners down to 14 px.
- Reason: the HUD must read from 3 m, and the rescue banner (4.6.3) is safety text.

#### 2026-10-21: Attaching a new rider clears the input queues

- Decision: `InputRouter.attach_rider()` with a different rider than the attached one clears the action and gesture queues; re-attaching the same rider keeps them.
- Alternatives: rely on the GameHost calling `clear_queues()` at session start (sdk-runtime's GameHost does not).
- Reason: nobody drains `take_actions()` in menus, so up to 256 menu presses would otherwise reach the session's first `local_intents` (GM8).

#### 2026-10-21 (W2): Contracts the app shell relies on, stated and tested

- Decision: HudLayer connects `PixelViewport.layout_changed` directly (plus `resized`), tested against the real PixelViewport; `WlInputRouter`'s `action` signal never carries an action made from a gesture (test_the_action_signal_never_carries_a_gesture); a hand-driven `PedalScanList` (listen_to_input off, processing off) moves only through `feed_action`, `feed_gesture` and `tick`, emits `item_chosen` then `item_picked` synchronously, and `set_items()` starts at the first enabled item, so a refresh in place restores the index itself (test_a_list_driven_by_hand); `WlSfx.set_metronome` is idempotent (test_set_metronome_is_idempotent).
- Alternatives: make `set_items()` keep a valid highlight (app-shell's W1 wording).
- Reason: these were documented assumptions on both sides; `AppScreen.refresh_menu()` already restores the highlight, and changing `set_items()` would change ui-hud's tested behaviour for games' shops.

#### 2026-10-21 (W2): HudLayer.reserve_corner for the host's own controls

- Decision: additive `HudLayer.reserve_corner(anchor, size)` / `reserved_corner(anchor)`: a reserved corner's stack moves inward by the reserved height plus a gap and the centre stack of that edge keeps clear of the reserved width. No platform element reserves a corner today.
- Alternatives: let games avoid a corner (every corner may hold game widgets; Workout uses all six anchors).
- Reason: a host-drawn corner element must never cover game widgets.

#### 2026-10-21 (W2 round 2): Toasts and banners run on the session's time

- Decision: `HudLayer.update()` advances toasts and game banners by how far the telemetry's platform time moved (at most 1 s per call); `_process` advances them by frame time only while no session time arrives (a HUD on its own, before the first tick, or after 0.5 s without one). `tick(dt)` stays public.
- Alternatives: frame time only (W1); let the screenshot tool tick the HUD by the skipped time.
- Reason: seen in the W2 screenshots: after tools/screenshot fast-forwarded to 400 s and 675 s the warm-up and interval banners and "Sprint in 10 s" were still up, and at `--sim-speed=8` every toast lasted eight times its sim seconds. Game banners and toasts announce sim-time events (a 10 s sprint warning), so they count sim time.

#### 2026-10-21 (W2 round 2): Corner toasts and banners line up with their side; the phase clock is 48 px

- Decision: additive `HudToasts.set_side()` / `HudBanner.set_side()` (-1 left, 0 centre, 1 right); HudLayer sets the side from the anchor (Rect2i areas centre). The timeline's clock (time left in the phase) is 48 px at full size, like the gear; the timeline's base_size grows to 640 x 162 and min_rect to 380 x 108.
- Alternatives: keep centred toasts in corners; keep the clock at 40 px as a secondary number.
- Reason: in Workout's top-left toast area a short toast sat centred under a longer one, half-way into the screen; the time left in an interval is the number a rider checks most after watts, and BRIEF 3.9 asks for key numbers of at least 48 px.

#### 2026-10-21 (W4): The rescue banner always shows; show_safety_banners hides only the warm-up banner

- Decision: `HudBanner.safety_state()` shows rescue whatever `show_safety` says; `HudSpec.show_safety_banners = false` hides only the warm-up banner (contract doc comment updated, no API change). Rescue wins over warm-up with the flag on; with it off, rescue still shows.
- Alternatives: a separate compact rescue fallback like `ensure_power_visible`; a conformance check that games keep the flag true; keep the opt-out as it was.
- Reason: BRIEF 4.6.3 says the governor shows "rescue", and 4.6 outranks game choices. With the flag off and no local teammate card, only the rescue sound was left. No shipped game turns the flag off, so nothing visible changes for Workout or the template.

#### 2026-10-21 (W4): Trainer control lost shows a notice in the session HUD

- Decision: `HudLayer` links `EventBus.trainer_control_lost` and `trainer_status_changed`. A loss puts up `TRAINER_ANOTHER_APP_CONTROL` ("Another app (Zwift, the Wahoo app) may hold control of the trainer. Close it, then reconnect.") as a trainer notice in the banner area (additive `HudBanner.set_trainer_notice()` / `trainer_text()` / `trainer_label()`: dark panel, orange border, the warm-up banner's smaller size) and plays the "warning" sound, once per loss. It stays up, also across a rebuild and while a retry runs, until `trainer_status_changed` reports `ready`. `show_safety_banners` does not hide it. When `build()` runs on the autoload EventBus (the app's session bus), it also reads `/root/TrainerService` the way it already reads `/root/Settings` (looked up by path, `has_method("status")` guard, no new API) and, if the status is `control_lost`, shows the notice and plays the sound as the signal would have, once: a session that starts while control is already lost (a connect-time 0x05 in the lobby, or a loss in an earlier session never regained) shows it too. A private bus (tests, in-process sims) is never seeded. Only a rendering GameHost builds a HudLayer, so headless peers show nothing.
- Alternatives: a toast (gone after 3 s while the trainer still has no load); `BLE_STATUS_CONTROL_HELD` ("... Close it, then retry.": no retry control exists in the session); clearing on any status other than `control_lost` (a retry's `connected` would hide it before control is back); a session-screen banner in the app shell (ui-hud already follows the session bus, and the app's session bus is the autoload EventBus that TrainerService emits on); for a loss before the session, the GameHost or the app shell passing the trainer status in at build time (a new parameter on a frozen API that every caller must remember) or recording it as a known limitation (the lobby card says so before the start, but the trainer still has no load during the session).
- Reason: BRIEF 4.4 says to tell the player another app may hold control; a Machine Status 0xFF arrives mid-ride, and until now only the menus said so. `TRAINER_ANOTHER_APP_CONTROL` names Zwift and the Wahoo app in the brief's own words and its "reconnect" matches the way back (Settings, connect the same trainer, which calls `retry_control()`).

### app-shell

#### 2026-10-21: Screens built in code on one base, one active pedal-scan menu

- Decision: every screen (`src/app/screens/`) extends `AppScreen` and is built in code, not in `.tscn` files. A screen keeps a stack of `PedalScanList` menus (sub-menus for option values, steppers and the on-screen keyboard) and only the top one is active. Lists get `listen_to_input = false` and `set_process(false)`; the screen feeds InputRouter's actions and gestures to the active list (`feed_action`, `feed_gesture`) and drives its scan timer with `tick(dt)`.
- Alternatives: one `.tscn` per screen; every list listening to InputRouter itself.
- Reason: a screen often shows two lists (a field list and a value list); if each listened, one surge would pick in both. Driving time and input from the screen makes every screen testable headless with injected gestures and exact scan timing.
- Whether a menu scans comes from `InputRouter.pedal_nav_active()` (the rider's pedal navigation setting or Autopilot as the profile default); the title holds its first item while the zero-touch countdown is armed.

#### 2026-10-21: AppServices instead of autoload names in screens

- Decision: screens reach the autoloads only through `AppServices` (App, Settings, TrainerService, NetSession, GameRegistry, Rides, Sfx, InputRouter, EventBus, plus factories for `PedalScanList`, `GameHost` and `LocalRider`). `AppServices.goto()` calls `App.goto()`; if the App did not take the state (its W0 stub), it emits `navigated` and the main scene shows the screen anyway.
- Alternatives: call the autoloads by name everywhere.
- Reason: the W1 worktree only has stubs for those modules; injection lets every flow be tested with doubles, and the same code runs against the real autoloads after W2.

#### 2026-10-21: Zero-touch rules on the title screen

- Decision: "pedaling" is the pedal_start condition (cadence >= 40 rpm or power >= 30% FTP) held continuously, timed by sample time: 5 s with a saved profile, 20 s on the first run. While the countdown is armed the title's menu holds its first item, which is the same quick ride (last-played game, else `GameRegistry.first_run_game()`, with its `zero_input_content`), so a surge also starts it. A keyboard or controller action disarms it for this visit. With a saved profile, a coast after pedaling does too, and the main menu then runs on pedal-scan, so a pedal-only rider can still host, join or open settings. On the first run a coast never disarms it (see "Coasting never takes a pedal-only rider out of a flow").
- Alternatives: a separate splash screen; counting pedal_start gestures; scanning the menu during the countdown.
- Reason: scanning during the countdown would let the start-of-pedaling surge pick a random item; holding the quick ride makes the two zero-input paths agree, and coast gives pedal-only riders the full menu.

#### 2026-10-21: Session seed

- Decision: the host's session seed is `--seed` when given, else `SeededRng.derive(utc_start, "<game>|<content>")` masked to 30 bits. The ride's UTC start stamp is the app's only wall-clock read (`AppServices.utc_now()`).
- Alternatives: `randi()`; a fixed seed.
- Reason: BRIEF 3.6 forbids global RNG and wall clock in rules; sessions still need variety; 30 bits survive JSON's float numbers in `session_start`.

#### 2026-10-21: Who starts a session

- Decision: solo: `pedal_start` (or Start ride) readies and starts at once. Host: Start ride readies the host and starts as soon as every human is ready (`NetSession.all_ready()`) and there are at least `min_players` slots; `pedal_start` only readies, so a room never starts before friends join. Clients ready with `pedal_start` or the Ready item and follow the host's `session_start`. Coast or back on a lobby menu highlights Leave instead of leaving.
- Alternatives: auto-start a hosted room when everyone present is ready.
- Reason: zero-input solo play and hosting (a surge on Start ride) both work, and a rider who stops pedaling in the lobby never drops out of the room.

#### 2026-10-21: Opening the client's GameHost

- Decision: a client opens the session screen on `session_start`, and on a rejoin when the host's `plan`, `phase` or `snapshot` arrives while it is still in the lobby. The GameHost config carries the session_start payload (players, seed, options, scaling) and the key `"session_start"`; the message that opened the session is re-emitted on `NetSession.game_message` right after `GameHost.setup()` and `start()`, so the new GameHost sees it.
- Alternatives: create the client GameHost when the lobby fills; ask the host to resend.
- Reason: messages arrive in one poll; the GameHost is created synchronously in the handler, so only the triggering message would be lost.

#### 2026-10-21: Scripted peer details (end-to-end contract)

- Decision: exit codes 0 complete, 1 error, 2 `--run-seconds` hard stop, 3 refused (including `missing_game`, `version_mismatch`), 4 host left before the results. `WATTLANDS_ROOM` prints the code without the dash (`--join` accepts both). `WATTLANDS_STATE` prints `lobby`, then every GameHost/EventBus session state once per change. Report events: `session_state` {state}, `phase_started` {index}, `game_event` {event_type, event} (host: `GameModule.game_event`; clients: each `event` batch minus platform types), `results` {grade, session_id}; directives deduplicated by peer, id, kind and t; the trace samples the local rider's governor status at 1 Hz of platform time. The host lingers 2 s after its results so they reach the clients. On exit: `App.quit(code)`, then the report and `WATTLANDS_DONE`, then `SceneTree.quit(code)`. A failed or timed-out upload (150 s) or Strava connect (90 s) is an error.
- Alternatives: exit 0 on any end; the App writing the report.
- Reason: the harness needs distinct failures; writing after `App.quit` means nothing overwrites the report.

#### 2026-10-21: The results screen performs the auto-upload

- Decision: with Strava connected and auto-upload on (`Rides.auto_upload_enabled()` or the profile flag), the results screen (and the scripted peer) calls `Rides.upload()` once without a prompt; a state already reported for the ride counts as started.
- Alternatives: Rides uploads on `ride_saved` by itself.
- Reason: one caller means exactly one upload (the e2e test counts them); the results screen already shows the state.

#### 2026-10-21: Option editing in the lobby

- Decision: toggle and choice options and int options with at most 12 values open a value list; longer int ranges open a stepper (−step, +step, ±5 steps). A missing label key shows the option id; a missing choice label key shows the raw value; content labels fall back to the bare id (BRIEF 3.6).
- Alternatives: always a stepper; always a list.
- Reason: a value list is one pick on pedal-scan; 16 values (Siege's wave offset) at 1.6 s each would take too long to scan.

#### 2026-10-21: FTP test runs on the app's rider (P1)

- Decision: the FTP test page builds `AppFtpTest`'s plan (ramp-in, 60 s steps rising 6% of FTP up to min(1000 W, trainer max)), finalizes it with `PlanValidator.finalize(plan, {"ramp_test": true})`, configures the governor with `profile: "ramp_test"` and ticks the rider in fixed 0.05 s steps itself; FTP = 0.75 x best 1-minute power, saved after 10 s unless back.
- Alternatives: a GameModule for the test.
- Reason: games live under `src/games/` and the GameHost has no ramp_test profile; the protocol logic stays pure and tested.

#### 2026-10-21: Recent rooms

- Decision: `Settings` key `recent_rooms` holds up to 8 rooms {code, role, game_id, game_title}, newest first; the join page lists them; a host reclaims its last code through `NetSession.host_room(max, code)` when NetSession takes that second argument.
- Alternatives: a separate file under `Paths.user_dir()`.
- Reason: settings.json already persists through Paths; the call degrades to a new code until net-client adds the argument.

#### 2026-10-21: A client sends player_info only when it changed

- Decision: the lobby keeps the last player_info it sent. On every lobby_state a client rebuilds its player_info (the host's scaling decides `p_eff_per_w`) and sends it only if it differs. Player-option defaults are filled in once per game, so an echo never resets an edited value.
- Alternatives: send on every lobby_state; send only on the first one.
- Reason: a host answers each player_info with a lobby_state broadcast. Sending on every lobby_state looped host and client for a game without player_options (Tank) and would hit the relay's rate limit. Sending only once left a stale `p_eff_per_w` after the host changed scaling.

#### 2026-10-21: Coasting never takes a pedal-only rider out of a flow

- Decision:
  - First run: a coast never disarms the 20 s countdown; stopping only restarts the stretch. A device action still disarms it.
  - On-screen keyboard (code entry, text fields): the first back or coast only highlights Back; a second back, or picking Back, cancels.
  - Session paused by the platform (coasting, stop_5s): the overlay holds Resume and does not scan.
- Alternatives: treat coast as back everywhere (BRIEF 3.5 default).
- Reason:
  - First run: no profile means no scanning menu, so a disarmed countdown left a rider without input stuck on the title.
  - Keyboard: a rider who pauses while scanning six characters would lose the code.
  - Pause: from rest a surge (1.5 s) fires before pedal_start (3 s), so a scanning overlay could pick End ride or Panic when the rider starts pedaling again.

#### 2026-10-21: Autopilot in the lobby, metronome in sessions

- Decision: the lobby has a per-session "My Autopilot" item for the local rider. It starts at the profile's default, goes out in player_info and shows on the rider's card; the profile default is not changed. The session screen starts the Sfx cadence metronome at the preferred cadence when the profile's metronome is on, and stops it when the screen goes. The main scene applies the saved master volume at boot.
- Alternatives: Autopilot only in the profile; leave the metronome to the HUD.
- Reason: BRIEF 3.5 says Autopilot is toggled "in the profile or lobby". Nothing else turned on the metronome or the saved volume, so those settings would have done nothing. Both calls are idempotent if ui-hud also makes them.

#### 2026-10-21: Zero-touch hold after the launch visit needs a fresh start

- Decision: the launch visit to the title counts a pedaling stretch that is already running when the title opens. Later visits (back from a ride, the results or a menu) count the 5 s hold only after one sample without pedaling. A coast after that old stretch does not disarm the countdown. `AppServices.title_visits` counts the visits in one app run.
- Alternatives: arm the countdown only on the launch visit; arm it the same way on every visit.
- Reason: riders keep spinning after the cool-down, so a rider who picks Continue would otherwise be put into a new ride 5 s later. Arming only at launch would break "hold pedal_start 5 s on the title" for later visits.

#### 2026-10-21 (W2): Sessions hand ticking to the GameHost through set_external_tick

- Decision: `AppServices.set_menu_ticking(on)` calls `WlTrainerService.set_external_tick(not on)` (the platform's name for the requested `set_menu_ticking`). The session screen, the scripted peer and the FTP test call it as before.
- Alternatives: ask platform for an alias `set_menu_ticking`; keep the duck-typed call.
- Reason: the duck-typed call found no method on the real service, so TrainerService and the GameHost both ticked the session rider on two clocks.

#### 2026-10-21 (W2): The GameHost runs on wall time in every app run

- Decision: `AppSessionLauncher.build_config()` sets `config.realtime` (params `realtime`, default true): the session screen passes its `auto_process`, the scripted peer its own. Headless app runs (scripted e2e peers) therefore advance the GameHost's SimClock in `_process` like windowed ones; tests drive it by hand.
- Alternatives: step the GameHost from the screen's `_process`.
- Reason: the GameHost's default is `realtime = not headless`, meant for `GameSimRunner`; a headless app session never stepped.

#### 2026-10-21 (W2): A client's GameHost gets the message that opened it

- Decision: right after `GameHost.start()` the shell re-emits on `NetSession.game_message` what made it create the client's GameHost: `params.replay` (a rejoin's plan/phase/snapshot) or else the host's `session_start` (`AppSessionLauncher.replay_messages`). The client's GameHost is still created inside that `session_start` emission, so the `plan` that follows it in the same poll reaches it directly.
- Alternatives: the GameHost reads `config.session_start` (asked of sdk-runtime in W1; its `_on_session_start` ignores a second copy, so both can coexist).
- Reason: a GameHost connects to `game_message` in `setup()`, after the `session_start` emission it was created in already went past it, so clients never left the lobby.

#### 2026-10-21 (W2): SOLO and HOST sessions start after the tick that triggered them

- Decision: in the real app (screen `auto_process` on) the session screen builds its overlay at once and creates the GameHost with `call_deferred`; CLIENT sessions and tests start synchronously.
- Alternatives: harden `LocalRider.tick()` against `setup()` re-entering from its own gesture or sample signal (requested of platform as well); defer every navigation.
- Reason: Start is usually picked by a surge or the zero-touch sample, both emitted inside `LocalRider.tick()`; setting that rider up for the session there let the rest of the tick record menu time into the new ride (RideRecorder "time went backwards", the first seconds of the FIT lost).

#### 2026-10-21 (W2): The session screen owns InputRouter.solo

- Decision: the session screen sets `InputRouter.solo = (role == SOLO)` at the start and back to true when it leaves.
- Alternatives: the GameHost sets it (ui-hud's W1 note; sdk-runtime's GameHost does not).
- Reason: `stop_5s` must map to pause only in a solo session (BRIEF 3.5); the shell already owns the session's lifetime.

#### 2026-10-21 (W2): Scripted peers end the same way whichever clock runs out

- Decision: the scripted peer sets `App.run_seconds_exit_code = 2` and finishes through its own `finish()` on `App.quitting` (report, DONE line, exit 2 for `run_seconds`).
- Alternatives: ask platform to stop counting `--run-seconds` for scripted peers.
- Reason: `WlApp` also counts `--run-seconds` and quits on its own; without this its quit (exit 0, fallback report) could win the race.

#### 2026-10-21 (W2): App tests drive the real PedalScanList

- Decision: `tests/app/fakes/fake_scan_list.gd` is the real `PedalScanList` plus read-only probes (`ids`, `text_of`, `index_of_id`, `is_disabled_id`, `picks`); `FakeRegistry.add_game` is `add_fixture`. Screens set `sounds = false` on their lists and play the UI cues themselves.
- Alternatives: keep the W1 double under private names.
- Reason: the double redeclared members of the real class and no longer compiled; the real list now exists and the screens should be tested against it.

#### 2026-10-21 (W2): The GameHost reads input through the session screen

- Decision: the session screen hands its GameHost an `AppSessionInput` as `config.input_router`: the router's actions without `pause` (the screen pauses and resumes) and, while the overlay menu is open, without anything but `panic` (which always passes); gestures unchanged; `detach_rider()` at the session end re-attaches TrainerService's menu rider.
- Alternatives: let the GameHost own pause (then the overlay's confirm both picks an item and resumes); ask sdk-runtime to stop toggling pause (app-shell's W1 request, not taken).
- Reason: with the real GameHost a P press paused (screen) and resumed (GameHost, next tick), and a confirm on the overlay both picked the highlighted item and resumed; after a session the router had no rider, so results and menus lost pedal gestures.

#### 2026-10-21 (W2): Overlay above the HUD

- Decision: the session screen puts its pause overlay in a CanvasLayer at `HudLayer.LAYER + 10`.
- Alternatives: keep them in the screen's canvas (drawn under the HudLayer CanvasLayer).
- Reason: seen in a windowed run on the real GameHost: HUD widgets and toasts drew over the overlay.

#### 2026-10-21 (W2 round 2): The FTP test page drives the platform's FtpTest

- Decision: `AppFtpTestScreen` starts `FtpTest.run_on(rider, Settings, TrainerService)` after checking `FtpTest.build_plan()` with the PlanValidator (`ramp_test`), sets the rider up as a recorded ride (`ftp_test`), ticks it in fixed 0.05 s steps while menu ticking is handed over, and saves the ride when the cool-down ends, the rider ends it or the page is left, before handing the rider back. `src/app/ftp_test.gd` (`AppFtpTest`) is removed. The page follows FtpTest's flow: the 10 s save countdown runs as the cool-down starts (Save, Keep my old FTP), then End the cool-down; `back` cancels a running test without a result, keeps the old FTP during the countdown and ends the cool-down after it. While the test runs its menu doesn't scan and surge and coast pick nothing; stopping pedaling is FtpTest's coasting rule and keeps the result.
- Alternatives: keep the app's own protocol class; count down on a result page after the cool-down.
- Reason: two implementations had drifted (the app's ended on a cadence that never arrived, saved results from the ramp-in, and ignored rescue's own end); BRIEF 3.2 has one protocol. A hard ERG effort can read as a surge, which must not cancel or panic the test.

#### 2026-10-21 (W2 round 2): W/kg opt-in on the profile page

- Decision: the profile page has a "Show my W/kg in rides" toggle for `RiderProfile.show_wkg` right after "Share weight with the host"; it is a normal pedal-scan field and saves at once.
- Alternatives: tie W/kg to `share_weight`.
- Reason: BRIEF 3.3 shows W/kg only if the rider opted in; seeing your own W/kg doesn't need your weight to leave the PC.

#### 2026-10-21 (W2 round 2): The session overlay dims the HUD

- Decision: the session overlay gets a full-screen scrim (black, 60%) under it in the top layer, and its panel is opaque (97%); an empty detail line is hidden. A test seam `AppSessionScreen.render_headless` builds the view and HUD under GUT's headless display server. The lobby's message line starts hidden.
- Alternatives: the 82% panel alone; a status-like panic label in the HUD's own colors.
- Reason: seen in tools/screenshot --app-screen=session as a client: the panic overlay's title ran into the sprint banner showing through the panel. In the solo lobby an empty message line pushed the content down.


#### 2026-10-21 (W4): A client's governor works from the rider's own profile (W3 SAF-1)

- Decision: `AppSessionLauncher.build_config` overwrites a CLIENT's own slot's `ftp`, `ftp_verified`, `haptics_on` and `haptic_intensity` with the rider's profile (`apply_own_profile`) before `LocalRider.setup`, on both the `session_start` and the rejoin (`lobby_state`) path. HOST and SOLO keep their lobby slot, which comes from their own `player_info`.
- Alternatives: trust the host's echo (an honest host only rounds the FTP); clamp the host's values to a range.
- Reason: BRIEF 3.3 ("ERG targets always come from each rider's own FTP") and 4.6.7 (each client validates locally). A host that sent FTP 5000 made the client's floor 1500 W; the host also removed the 0.85 unverified factor and turned opted-out haptics back on. The core's own check in `LocalRider.setup` and `SafetyGovernor.configure` is the trainer and core fixers' part.

#### 2026-10-21 (W4): --sim-speed only drives a MockTrainer session (W3 SAF-3)

- Decision: `AppSessionLauncher.effective_sim_speed(device, requested)` gives the requested speed only for a `MockTrainer`; any other device runs the session clock at 1 and logs a warning. It is a local copy of the rule; the trainer area adds the TrainerService helper for its menu clock, and the launcher can switch to it when that lands.
- Alternatives: refuse to start; ignore the flag only above 1.
- Reason: every governor time rule runs on platform time, so x4 meant 120 W/s slew and 8 writes/s on a real trainer (BRIEF 4.5, 4.6.2), and x0.25 stretched rescue and coasting.

#### 2026-10-21 (W4): Settings make the trainer safe before the link drops (W3 SAF-8)

- Decision: Disconnect, connecting another trainer and "use the mock trainer" first shut the menu rider's governor down (`LocalRider.shutdown()`: SIM flat, then Stop) and wait `SAFE_DRAIN_S` (0.5 s, the grace App.quit gives a real trainer) of screen time before the link drops; with a MockTrainer, no rider, a link that isn't ready or during a session they act at once. Leaving the page mid-wait drops the link at once (the rider is already safe). After Disconnect the shut-down menu rider is set up again (as TrainerService's menu reset does), because the manager hands the same `FtmsTrainer` back on the next connect and TrainerService then keeps its rider; without that the governor would stay dead and App.quit would skip it. After a switch the old rider stays shut down, so its commands never reach the new trainer.
- Alternatives: wait for the trainer area's TrainerService method (not merged when this round ran; the page has one helper, `make_trainer_safe_then`, to swap); drain by watching FtmsClient (the app never touches it, BRIEF 4.6.11).
- Reason: BRIEF 4.6.9 "on disconnect: try SIM flat, then Stop". Proven on the emulated KICKR CORE (`tests/app/test_settings_pages.gd`); the effect on a real trainer is a hardware check.

#### 2026-10-21 (W4): Network notice on the session screen (W3 NET-6)

- Decision: the session screen draws a banner in its top layer (above the HUD): on a client `NET_HOST_DROPPED` from `peer_left(host)` until `peer_joined(host)` or `host_left`, and `NET_STATE_RECONNECTING` while its own NetSession is reconnecting (HOST and CLIENT). Sending `leave` when the host's window closes is the net area's part.
- Alternatives: a HUD toast (gone after a few seconds while the wait lasts up to 120 s); the pause overlay (the host's absence doesn't pause the client's ride).
- Reason: BRIEF 5.5; clients sat 120 s with no notice.

#### 2026-10-21 (W4): Menus follow the rider's trainer mode and never ERG-auto-pick (W3 ZIH-6)

- Decision: `AppScreen.tick` passes the local rider's trainer mode (`local_trainer_mode()`: the InputRouter's attached rider; the session screen's own rider) to the active menu with `PedalScanList.set_local_mode`, without `follow_local_mode`. In ERG the hint reads "keep pedaling" and a coast doesn't back out.
- Alternatives: `follow_local_mode` (it also turns on ERG auto-confirm, which would auto-pick End ride or Panic in the ride menu).
- Reason: BRIEF 3.5 (coast never means back in ERG); the old hint told an ERG rider to stop pedaling, which only triggered the coasting floor and a pause.

#### 2026-10-21 (W4): The first-run profile starts from the profile as it is (W3 PROF-3)

- Decision: both first-run paths (the 20 s rule and the form) copy the current profile (`AppFirstRunScreen.first_run_copy`) and set the name ("Rider" or the typed one) and `created`; the FTP is 200 W unverified (CP = FTP) unless it is already verified, which only an FTP test finished before the profile existed can do.
- Alternatives: copy only the Settings fields (haptics, Strava, pedal navigation, audio) onto a fresh profile.
- Reason: BRIEF 3.2, 4.6.6: a haptics opt-out made in Settings before the first ride must hold; a verified FTP is more accurate than the 200 W default.

#### 2026-10-21 (W4): One launch visit of the title (W3 PROF-1)

- Decision: the main scene records the state it showed before (`AppServices.previous_state`); a title built over a title (the App's deferred `boot()` shows it a second time at launch) is not a new visit.
- Alternatives: skip the App's second `goto("title")`; derive "fresh start" from the previous state alone.
- Reason: the launch visit counted twice, so a rider already pedaling at launch never got the 5 s start (BRIEF 3.5). Checked in the real app: `--mock-trainer=const:w=150,rpm=85 --run-seconds=25` with a saved profile now starts Workout and saves the ride.

#### 2026-10-21 (W4): Imperial units edit the weight in pounds (W3 PROF-6)

- Decision: with `units` imperial, the profile and first-run weight fields show and step in whole lb (67-440 lb, inside the 30-200 kg range) and store kg; changing units rebuilds the field list. The weight is the only measured value the app shows the rider.
- Alternatives: remove the units choice; document it as stored only.
- Reason: BRIEF 3.2 lists units; a visible choice that changed nothing was a defect.

#### 2026-10-21 (W4): Results: stats under the grid, W/kg, unlock names (W3 ZIH-1, PROF-5, ZIH-8)

- Decision: each rider's custom stats are a wrapped line under the rows grid ("Ada · ..."), the grid keeps its seven columns, and the side column with the summary line expands and wraps. When the rider opted in (`show_wkg`) an "x.x W/kg average" line follows the summary, computed from the local weight and never sent. Unlock ids show `<GAME_ID>_UNLOCK_<ID>` when the game has that string (as content labels use `<GAME_ID>_CONTENT_<CID>`), else the id.
- Alternatives: a W/kg grid column (would need every rider's weight); truncating the stats.
- Reason: a real Workout row made the grid 2599 px wide and pushed Upload, Continue and %FTP off a 1080p screen (BRIEF 3.3, 3.8, 3.9).

#### 2026-10-21 (W4): Controls rows show one key and one pad binding (W3 ZIH-3, ZIH-4)

- Decision: a Controls row reads "Action: <first key>, <first pad binding>" with InputBindings' short labels; the content column lists every binding of every action. Leaving the page (coast, back, the Back item, leaving Settings) ends a capture, and the controller's B button cancels one as Esc does (so B can't be captured as a binding, like Esc).
- Alternatives: a two-line row with a detail line (14 rows would no longer fit 1080 px in height); truncating with an ellipsis.
- Reason: the long `as_text()` rows were 2635 px wide; a capture left armed rebound the next key or button pressed on Settings.

#### 2026-10-21 (W4): Typing a room code needs no click (W3 ZIH-7)

- Decision: on the join page, the first key press of a code character (letters and digits, Crockford rules) while nothing has keyboard focus goes into the code field, which then takes the focus; arrows, Enter, Esc and Space keep their actions, and the on-screen keyboard keeps working for the controller and pedal-scan until the rider types.
- Alternatives: focus the field when the page opens (the screen then blocks every routed action but back and confirm, so the controller could no longer move the on-screen keyboard).
- Reason: APP_JOIN_HINT says "Type the host's code"; keys went nowhere until the field was clicked.

#### 2026-10-21 (W4): A rider's own .zwo files in the lobby (W3 WO-1)

- Decision: for a game whose game.json lists the `zwo` content scheme, the host and solo lobby's content picker lists the game's contents and then every `*.zwo` in `Paths.user_dir()/workouts/` (created on demand) as `zwo:<absolute path>`, labelled by file name (`APP_CONTENT_ZWO`) and pedal-scan selectable; the page names the folder (`APP_LOBBY_ZWO_HINT`); the session preview shows the importer's clamps. The interactive lobby also takes `--content=<id>` for the game it opens, and a scripted host accepts any id `WlGameRegistry.content_allowed` accepts. A `zwo:` id whose file is gone falls back to the default content.
- Alternatives: a typed path field (long paths on the on-screen keyboard); only fixing the docs.
- Reason: BRIEF 3.7 makes .zwo import (Workout Director) P0; no app screen could produce a `zwo:` id, and HARDWARE_TEST.md 6 asks for `--content=zwo:<path>`.

#### 2026-10-21 (W4): Strava FTP and weight import in Settings (W3 PROF-4)

- Decision: Settings -> Strava adds "Connect with profile access (for the import)" (`Rides.strava_connect_with_profile`), two toggles (FTP on, weight off by default) and "Import from Strava now" (`Rides.strava_import_athlete`, then the profile is saved); a status line names what was imported.
- Alternatives: import during the normal connect.
- Reason: BRIEF 3.8 P1 asks for an opt-in import; profile:read_all is only requested when the rider asks for it.

#### 2026-10-21 (W4): The rides history re-uploads rides already on Strava (W3 RS-3)

- Decision: picking a ride whose upload state is uploaded or duplicate calls `Rides.reupload(ride_id)`; any other ride goes through `Rides.upload()`.
- Reason: BRIEF 3.8 P1 "Rides history with re-upload"; `upload()` only reports the stored state of an uploaded ride, so a ride deleted on Strava could not go up again.

#### 2026-10-21 (W4r2): App.quit ends the tree, not the app shell (W4r2 R9/R10, W3 NET-6)

- Decision: `AppServices.quit` calls `App.quit`, then `last`, and calls `SceneTree.quit` only when the App is not quitting inside the tree (`app_ends_tree()`: a test double or a private App). A window close re-enters it through the main scene's "quitting" branch before App.quit has left the room; it now does nothing more, so App.quit leaves the hosted room, shuts the riders down and ends the tree itself after `QUIT_GRACE_S`. The "quitting" branch also freezes the current screen (`AppScreen.freeze()`: input disconnected, `_process` off on the screen and every node under it, a GameHost included), so during the grace only the TrainerService ticks the shut-down riders. `quit_hook` now stands in for `SceneTree.quit` alone.
- Alternatives: main.gd ignores "quitting" while App quits (leaves the scripted path, where AppServices.quit followed App.quit with an immediate `SceneTree.quit`, unfixed); `leave()` the screen on quitting (hands ticking back and re-sets the menu rider up before App.quit shuts it down).
- Reason: `SceneTree.quit` ends the process at the end of the frame, so the grace timer App.quit started afterwards never fired: no `host_left` reached the relay's linger and the governor's SIM flat and Stop (BRIEF 4.6.9) had no time to go out with a real trainer. `tests/app/test_quit_chain.gd` drives the real main.gd, AppServices and WlApp on a hosting loopback session.

#### 2026-10-21 (W4r2): The results page fits 1080p by measured fit steps (W4r2 R16, R26)

- Decision: the results page never scrolls, so it is laid out to fit. The menu column holds the menu, the upload status and the host-left notice; the content column the grade, the local rider's summary and new unlocks, and the rows grid; the custom stats lines, the team line and "Ride saved" run the full page width below both columns (two lines for a real Workout row instead of four). While the page is taller than the screen (checked one frame after each layout change, also after a longer upload status), it takes the next fit step: a compact top row (grade at FONT_NUMBER, XP beside it), then only the local rider's custom stats (every rider's kJ, W, %FTP, NP, time in band and XP stay in the grid; each rider's own screen has their stats), then FONT_SMALL grid rows, then caps with an ellipsis (stats line 3 lines, team 1; then 1 or 2 lines everywhere). Pages with more than 3 rows start at the own-stats step. Real Workout stats (10 intervals, 4-digit sprint peaks, 16-character names, the Strava URL, unlocks, W/kg, the host-left notice) fit uncut from 1 to 8 riders; only stats as long as a 200-interval .zwo are cut. `tools/screenshot --app-screen=results --app-riders=<1..8>` shoots the real screen.
- Alternatives: a ScrollContainer (pedal-scan and zero-input riders can't scroll); fixed tiers by rider count (a long .zwo or a long status line still overflows); truncating every stats line to one line (loses the local rider's own numbers).
- Reason: W4 WO-2 made each stats line twice as long; from one rider the upload status (BRIEF 3.8) and with two or more the ride-saved line fell below a 1080 px screen.

#### 2026-10-21 (W4r2): Results stats keep the game's order (W4r2 R18)

- Decision: `AppResultsScreen.stats_text` lists custom and team stats in the Dictionary's insertion order, which is the order the game added them; Protocol encodes without sorted keys, so clients show the host's order.
- Alternatives: a fixed key order in the app (the app can't know a game's keys); sorting (showed "Best 1 min, Best 20 min, Best 5 min").
- Reason: BRIEF 3.7 lists best 1-, 5- and 20-minute power in that order; WorkoutResults adds its stats in BRIEF order.

#### 2026-10-21 (W4r2): Settings use TrainerService's safe unlink (W4r2 R6, R7; supersedes the W4 SAF-8 entry above)

- Decision: Settings' Connect, Disconnect and "use the mock trainer" call `TrainerService.connect_trainer(address)`, `disconnect_trainer()` and `use_mock()`, as the platform's SAF-8 decision says. The service shuts the rider down and drops the link only once the control point has sent SIM flat and Stop (up to its 3 s timeout); picking the trainer that is already linked connects at once and leaves the menu rider alone; the next ready trainer gets a new menu rider. The page's own fixed `SAFE_DRAIN_S` wait, its `_rearm_menu_rider()` and its "leaving the page drops the link at once" are gone: the wait belongs to the service and outlives the page. The page follows the service's connection manager (none with the mock) and shows "Making the trainer safe…" while `is_unlinking()`.
- Alternatives: keep the page's wait and only skip the same address (still too short for a retried write).
- Reason: a KICKR answering SIM flat later than 0.5 s lost the Stop (BRIEF 4.6.9), and re-picking the linked trainer left the menu rider shut down for good.

#### 2026-10-21 (W4r2): A Strava import outlives the Settings page (W4r2 R20)

- Decision: `AppSettingsScreen.import_from_strava()` is a static coroutine that awaits `Rides.strava_import_athlete()` and then saves the profile and emits `profile_changed`; the page only shows the result. Leaving the page while GET /athlete runs still saves the import.
- Alternatives: import into a copy and apply it from the page (leaving the page would drop the import the rider asked for).
- Reason: Rides writes the values into the live profile while it waits, so a page freed mid-request left an imported, unsaved profile in memory.

#### 2026-10-21 (W4r2): The Controls content column follows a rebind (W4r2 R28)

- Decision: ending a capture on the Controls page refreshes the content column (`all_bindings_text()`) as well as the rows; the unused `binding_text()` helper is removed (W4r2 R32/R44).
- Reason: InputBindings.rebind can take an input away from another action, so the list of every binding went stale until the page was reopened.

#### 2026-10-21 (W4r2): Esc and the arrows keep working on the join page after typing (W4r2 R27)

- Decision: while the code field has the keyboard focus, Esc and the four arrow keys take the focus off it in the lobby's `_input` (which runs before the InputRouter autoload's) and go on unhandled, so the router routes them: Esc backs out (the first highlights Cancel, the second leaves), the arrows move the on-screen keyboard and the room lists. The next code character takes the focus back (ZIH-7). Backspace and Enter stay with the field.
- Alternatives: let WlInputRouter route these keys while a LineEdit has the focus (every text field in the app would lose its caret keys); never focus the field (the ZIH-7 typing needs it).
- Reason: WlInputRouter drops key presses while a LineEdit has the focus, so after one typed character a keyboard rider could neither back out nor reach Recent or LAN rooms.

#### 2026-10-21 (W4fin): Controls get a Reset to defaults row; Esc and B still cancel a capture

- Decision: the Controls page's first row is Reset to defaults. It opens a Keep / Reset confirm with Keep highlighted, so a stray surge or Enter changes nothing; back, a coast or Keep returns unchanged. Reset calls `InputRouter.reset_bindings()` and then `save_bindings()`, which puts every project.godot default back (Esc and B on `back` included) and writes input.cfg. Esc and B keep cancelling a capture. A one-line help above the bindings list says so and names the Reset row, and the capture prompt reads "Esc or B cancels". Reset comes first: it is on screen when the page opens and the first row the scan highlights. The action rows and Back run below a 1080p screen and scroll into view as they are highlighted (next entry).
- Alternatives: let Esc and B be captured, with a timeout or an on-screen Cancel row as the only way out. A controller-only rider would lose the B-to-cancel habit that every other page keeps, and a pedal-scan rider would wait out a timeout. Reset with no confirm: one surge on the highlighted row would wipe every custom binding. Reset as the last row: the longest scan from the top of the page, and below the screen until the page scrolls.
- Reason: BRIEF 3.5 makes Esc and B the defaults for back and says everything is rebindable. `InputBindings.rebind` removes same-family inputs, so once back moved off Esc or B nothing in the UI could put them back, short of deleting input.cfg.

#### 2026-10-22 (W4fin): The Settings menu column scrolls and keeps the highlighted row in view

- Decision: Settings puts its menu column in a vertical ScrollContainer (`AppScreen.scroll_menu_column`, opt-in, so no other screen changes). On every highlight change (scan, keyboard or controller) and whenever the menus' layout changes, the column scrolls just far enough to show the whole highlighted row, plus the scan hint under the last row. A pushed or uncovered menu starts at its top. Settings hides its empty footer line, so the column reaches the bottom margin. Pages that fit (the root, Trainer, Haptics, Audio, Network, Pedal UI) never scroll and look as they did. Controls (Reset, 14 action rows, Back and the hint, about 1360 px) scrolls. Strava's 11 rows also ran past the bottom margin, with the scan hint below 1080 px, so Strava scrolls a little when Back is highlighted. The Trainer page scrolls too once a scan finds enough trainers.
- Alternatives: split Controls into two pages (menu keys and game keys). That adds a level to every rebind and leaves Strava and a long Trainer list as they were. A smaller row height or font on Controls only would go below the 40 px menu size (DESIGN 10.3) for one page. Scrolling inside `PedalScanList` would change ui-hud's list for the one app screen that needs it.
- Reason: BRIEF 3.9 says "every screen works with pedal-scan". Back sat at y=1394 on Controls, so the scan highlighted rows nobody could see.

#### 2026-10-21 (W4fin): The session's Autopilot turns pedal-scan on

- Decision: `scanning_enabled()` in the lobby, the session screen and results also counts this rider's Autopilot for the session. The lobby uses `my_autopilot`, and toggling it re-applies scanning to every open menu at once. The session uses its own slot's `autopilot` in the GameHost config (the lobby toggle, or the profile default for a quick start) and passes it to results as `params.autopilot`. It adds to `InputRouter.pedal_nav_active()` and never removes from it: switching Autopilot off for one session keeps scanning on when the profile default or pedal navigation says so. An auto-pause still holds Resume without scanning.
- Alternatives: make the router aware of the session's Autopilot (an API change in ui-hud's frozen router); let the session's Autopilot also switch scanning off (a profile-default Autopilot rider who switched it off with pedals could lose the scan with no device to fall back on).
- Reason: BRIEF 3.5 says "with Autopilot or pedal navigation on", and Autopilot is "toggled in the profile or lobby". Only the profile default reached the router, so a rider with pedal navigation off who switched Autopilot on in the lobby got menus that didn't scan.

### app (pedal navigation, forms, Strava text, branding)

- **2026-10-24: Pedal navigation is opt-in.** The Settings toggle "Pedal navigation" (`RiderProfile.pedal_nav`, default off) is copied into `PlayerSlot.pedal_nav` for the local rider by the session launcher. Off, menus and in-game pick lists never scan, never auto-confirm and ignore surge and coast; on, or with Autopilot as the profile default, nothing changes. `InputRouter.pedal_nav_active()` decides for menus, `PedalScanList.pedal_nav` for lists. *Alternative:* keep pedal-scan on until a keyboard or controller is touched. *Reason:* a stray surge or a coast must never pick or back out for a rider who did not ask for pedal control.
- **2026-10-24: Zero-touch start and first-run creation stay.** Holding `pedal_start` on the title and the first run's 20 s of pedaling are start rules, not menu picks, and a new rider has no way to opt in first. Siege's Overdrive keeps its surge: it is gameplay, not a menu. Siege's shop follows `slot.pedal_nav`; its zero-input fallbacks (Autopilot picks and routing) cover a rider who neither pedals into a pick nor presses a key.
- **2026-10-24: One surge graph, under the page.** `WlSurgeGraph` shows the last 5 s of power sampled at 10 Hz and the threshold from `GestureRecognizer.surge_threshold_w()` (read-only, `max(1.3 x 10 s mean, mean + 40 W)`). Each app page owns one, shown with pedal navigation and hidden in ERG, where the surge reads flywheel speed. *Alternative:* one graph per list (a long list pushes it off screen).
- **2026-10-24: Every form scrolls its menu column.** `make_form_page` (Profile, Settings, first run) and the Rides, lobby and picker pages use `scroll_menu_column`, so the highlighted row, and the last rows, are always reachable by keyboard, controller and scan.
- **2026-10-24: The Strava text comes from the game's results.** `Rides` keeps the `GameResults` of the session until its ride is saved, writes `ride_tag`, `ride_lines`, the game title and the content title into the summary JSON (so a re-upload from the history has them), and `activity_name` and `activity_description` read the summary only. Title `WATTLANDS: <game> - <ride_tag>` (content title when the tag is empty, no " - " when both are); description: the game's lines, moving time, distance, average and normalized power, average cadence, work, "Recorded with WATTLANDS". No heart rate, no image (the Strava API takes none). The recorder's summary gains `np_w`.
- **2026-10-24: The product name shown to players is WATTLANDS.** Strings, the window title (`config/name`), FIT product name, Strava texts and doc prose. Identifiers, paths, `WATTLANDS_*` variables and the `Wl` prefix keep their spelling.

### art-pipeline

Short ADRs for BRIEF 3.10. "fal docs" means fal's public model and platform API pages, read (never called with a key) on 2026-10-21.

- **2026-10-21: Request size = nearest aspect, about 1 MP, within the base price.** Among sizes valid for fal (16 px multiples, 655,360 to 8,294,400 px, 3840 px edge, 3:1), `generate.mjs` picks the smallest aspect error, then (within 0.05 %) the size nearest 1,048,576 px, never above 1536 × 864 worth of pixels unless nothing fits. *Alternatives:* always the minimum pixel count; always the maximum. *Reason:* every sprite between 1:3 and 3:1 lands within 1 % of its aspect (Tank checks 2 %, Fishing 5 %) at the $0.07 base price, with enough resolution for area averaging. Beyond 3:1 the request is 3:1 and `--dry-run` lists it; `tile: true` makes that the intended segment.
- **2026-10-21: Endpoint limits from fal docs.** Both GPT Image 2.5 endpoints state 16 px multiples, a 3840 px edge, 3:1 and 655,360–8,294,400 px; `endpoints.json` keeps BRIEF's 3840 px / 3:1 fallback in `defaults` for endpoints that don't. `mock_fal` checks fal's limits independently, so a wrong `endpoints.json` fails the tests with a 422.
- **2026-10-21: Prices.** Sunburst high is $0.07 up to 1536 × 864 and scales by pixel count above (BRIEF). Low and medium are priced as high (never under it); xhigh, max and auto (which may reach max) scale by flare's listed quality ratios. Flare uses its listed per-size table: the dearest row up to the smallest row that holds the request, plus $0.002 of prompt text. Money is summed in integer $0.0001 units. *Reason:* conservative estimates never let the budget stop undercount.
- **2026-10-21: Billed amounts.** fal documents no per-request cost in the queue, result or history responses (its usage API is aggregated and needs billing permissions). `generate.mjs` records a billed amount only when a response carries `cost_total` at the top level or under `billing` or `metrics`; `mock_fal` reports it under `billing` when configured. The spend total and the budget stop take max(estimate, billed) per request. *Alternative:* poll the usage API with request tags. *Reason:* delayed, needs extra key permissions, and can't attribute single requests.
- **2026-10-21: Free paths before paying.** Per sprite (unless `--reroll`): the local cache for the identical endpoint and input; a stored image URL, or resuming an unfinished request from its stored `status_url`; then fal's history (`/v1/models/requests/by-endpoint?expand=payloads&status=success&start=<now − 24 h>`, following `next_cursor`), matching every field of our input against `json_input`. A history that can't be read is a warning, then the run pays. *Reason:* BRIEF's recovery after a failed download, plus crashes between submit and manifest write; never blocking art on a missing `models:requests:read` permission.
- **2026-10-21: Statuses and spend.** A request fal accepted counts as paid even if it later fails (`failed`, `timeout`, `download_failed`); a `submit_failed` (no request id) does not. Submits are never retried automatically (a retry could pay twice; the history lookup finds a submit that did succeed). Status polls tolerate 5 consecutive errors; downloads are tried 3 times.
- **2026-10-21: Budget stop.** Workers take sprites in art.json order; each reserves its estimate before submitting and adds any billed excess when known. The first sprite that would pass `--budget` stops all further payment (free paths continue), its id and the rest are listed, and the run exits 3. With `--concurrency=1` the paid set is fully deterministic. Default concurrency 4. *Reason:* "halts before the run's estimated spend would pass" read literally; a cheaper later sprite does not sneak in after a stop.
- **2026-10-21: Selection.** Each run selects, per processed sprite, the result for its current input (new, recovered or cached), so the newest wins by default and returning to an earlier input re-selects its cached result.
