"Newest" is the newest request: free paths are tried newest request first, so a paid result newer than the cached one (a `--reroll` whose download failed) is recovered and selected instead of the older cached result; an unfinished newer request gets a single status check, not a full wait, when a cached result exists. `--select` marks a result `selected_by: "user"`; a cache-only run keeps that choice, a new or recovered result replaces it, and re-fetching an image that was already `ok` (after a cache wipe) does not count as new. A `--select` choice whose raw image is missing is fetched again from its stored URL by the next run that includes the sprite. `--select` edits only the manifest and can't be combined with `--only`, `--reroll` or `--dry-run`. *Reason:* Tank's flare-then-sunburst flow and Fishing's keep-the-draft flow both work without extra flags.
- **2026-10-21: The art lock and hosts.** The lock is checked right after argument parsing, before any file or socket. `--mock` must be a loopback URL; in mock mode `FAL_KEY` is never read and requests carry `Authorization: Key mock`. Real mode contacts only https `*.fal.run`, `*.fal.ai`, `*.fal.media`, sends the key only to `queue.fal.run` and `api.fal.ai`, follows redirects by hand (GET only, at most 3, re-checking the host), and redacts the key and each `:`-separated part of it from every message. The source never names the network lock variable (a test scans for it). When the client refuses to fetch a result image (a host outside that list), the run stops paying at once (no download retries, no further submits, exit 1): fal bills on completion, so every further sprite would be money spent on an image the tool may not fetch. *Alternative:* allow the image's host. *Reason:* BRIEF 3.10 allows only fal's hosts; a human decides whether to widen the list.
- **2026-10-21: Tests never set `WATTLANDS_ALLOW_ART`.** BRIEF 7.1 asks every child to run without it, and `test/helpers.mjs` `runGenerate()` enforces the rest: every child gets `--mock`, except refusal cases, which must run under a preload that makes `net`, `tls`, `http(s)`, `dns` and `fetch` throw and log (the "refusal before any socket" test). The "permission set, no key" refusal and `--dry-run`/`--select` without `--mock` run `main()` in the test process with a plain env object (never `process.env`) and a `fetch` that throws and records; the real-mode transport (auth hosts, allowlist, redaction) is tested on `FalClient` with an injected fetch and a dummy key passed in code.
- **2026-10-21: Exit codes.** 0 done, 1 some sprites failed, 2 usage or art.json error, 3 budget stop, 4 art lock refusal. Failures win over a budget stop.
- **2026-10-21: art.json is strict.** Unknown keys are errors (except `notes` and keys starting with `_`), ids and part names are `[a-z0-9]` words joined by single underscores, `mirror` needs `tile`, and every problem is listed at once. `anchor` defaults to the sprite's `align`; named anchors use the align names. *Reason:* a typo such as `backround` would otherwise silently generate the wrong art and cost money.
- **2026-10-21: Pixelation maths.** Trim at alpha ≥ 16/255; area averaging with exact fractional coverage, separable, premultiplied alpha, in sRGB-encoded values (dark outlines stay dark); alpha ≥ 0.5 becomes opaque, everything else `(0, 0, 0, 0)`; quantization to the nearest palette color in Oklab (Ottosson's matrices), ties to the lower index; optional 4×4 Bayer dither (±16 sRGB units) before quantizing. Fit offsets: left/top flush at 0, right/bottom flush at the edge, centre rounded to a whole pixel. A tile strip is assembled at source resolution (segments butt together, every second one flipped with `mirror`) and then resampled once. *Alternatives:* `Image.resize` (not area averaging); linear-light averaging (thins dark outlines). *Reason:* exact, deterministic and fast enough in GDScript (a 1 MP result takes well under a second).
- **2026-10-21: Outputs.** Parts are written as `<sprite id>__<part>.png` (the id rule forbids `__`, so names can't collide), plus `sprites/sprites.json` with every sprite's size, anchor (art pixels), parts and source request; files are rewritten only when their bytes change. Part files of a processed sprite that `art.json` no longer lists are removed (a stale part in an old palette would fail a game's palette-only test); nothing else in `sprites/` is ever deleted. Pixelate warns, and still proceeds, when the selected result's endpoint, prompt or background differs from `art.json`. *Alternative:* refuse such results. *Reason:* Fishing keeps flare drafts on purpose; Tank needs to notice a draft still selected after the sunburst switch.
- **2026-10-21: Pixel import preset (switchable, for W2).** `docs/OWNERSHIP.md` C36 relies on `project.godot`'s `[importer_defaults]` (lossless, no mipmaps), which already holds. As the W1 task asked, `ArtPixelate.apply_import_preset()` also writes a minimal `.import` with the preset (`compress/mode=0`, `mipmaps/generate=false`, ...) next to each PNG that has none, and corrects only those keys in an existing one, keeping its uid. Godot completes the minimal file on import (checked: `tests/art/fixtures/import/`). W2 switches by setting `ArtPixelate.import_preset_mode = IMPORT_PROJECT_DEFAULTS` (no files) or pointing `apply_import_preset()` at world-kits' `PixelImportPreset`.
- **2026-10-21: The end-to-end check is a Node script outside `node --test`.** `tools/art/pixelate_e2e.mjs` runs `generate.mjs` against the mock and then `pixelate.gd` through the wrapper, twice. It is not under `tools/art/test/` because `node --test` there must not start Godot (BRIEF 7: no Godot from inside another test process, and the node suite runs without Godot in CI). The lead can add it as a `tools/run_tests` step after GUT.
- **2026-10-21 (W2): Pixel import preset = project importer defaults only.** `ArtPixelate.import_preset_mode` now defaults to `IMPORT_PROJECT_DEFAULTS`: pixelate writes plain PNGs and no `.import` files, and `run_game()` first checks `PixelImportPreset.check_project_defaults()` (world-kits) and stops with an error if `project.godot`'s `[importer_defaults] texture` lost `compress/mode` 0 or `mipmaps/generate` false. `IMPORT_WRITE_FILE` stays as an opt-in for a folder that must pin the preset; nothing uses it by default. *Alternatives:* keep writing hand-made `.import` files (two mechanisms that can disagree); point pixelate at `PixelImportPreset.write_import_file()` (still per-file). *Reason:* OWNERSHIP C36 names one mechanism, the lead's importer defaults; world-kits' preset needs nothing more for 2D sprites (its recommended `detect_3d/compress_to` only matters for textures used in 3D, which no game does), and the check makes a lost default fail loudly instead of silently importing mipmapped sprites.
- **2026-10-21 (W4): Everything written is redacted; the key is trimmed (W3 ART-W3-1, ART-W3-2).** `saveManifest()` takes the run's redactor and runs it over the whole file text, and the `.art_cache` result JSON goes through it too, so nothing fal sends back (a job error, a result body, an image URL) can carry the key into a committed file. `main()` uses `FAL_KEY` trimmed, the way `fetch` sends it, and `makeRedactor()` also knows the trimmed key and its trimmed `:`-parts. Tested by running `main()` in real mode in a child process under the no-network preload against a fake fal that echoes the header (`test/key_echo.test.mjs`, `test/helpers/key_echo_run.mjs`); the child's environment never has `FAL_KEY` or `WATTLANDS_ALLOW_ART`, and `main()` gets a plain env object. *Alternative:* redact each field that came from fal (easy to miss one).
- **2026-10-21 (W4): A submit whose answer was lost keeps its budget share (W3 ART-W3-3).** Only a submit error that proves fal took nothing (an HTTP 4xx or 5xx answer, or the client refusing to send) releases the sprite's share and is `submit_failed`. A dropped connection, a timeout or an unreadable 2xx body is `submit_unconfirmed`: `paid: true` with no request id, counted in `spend.total_usd` on its own, and the next run adopts it when fal's request history shows a request with the same input (it then gets that request id and counts once). *Alternative:* look the request up in the history at once (the job may not have finished). *Reason:* BRIEF 3.10 "stops before the run's estimated spend passes --budget"; fal bills a queued job when it completes. If fal never queued it, the manifest over-counts one estimate, which stays on the safe side.
- **2026-10-21 (W4): An unusable --select choice gives way (W3 ART-W3-4).** A run keeps a `--select` choice only while its status is `ok`; one that could not be fetched again gives way to the usable result the run has, with a warning naming both. *Reason:* the documented invariant (exactly one usable selected row per sprite); a stale choice made pixelate skip the sprite for good.
- **2026-10-21 (W4r2): A submit that never left is not money spent, and the run stops submitting (W4r2 R24).** `FalClient` records the transport error code on `FalError` (undici's `cause.code`, or the first inner error of an `AggregateError`) and `notSent` when that code proves the request never left this machine: no connection (`ECONNREFUSED`, `ENOTFOUND`, `EAI_AGAIN`, `UND_ERR_CONNECT_TIMEOUT`, unreachable network or host) or a failed TLS handshake (certificate errors, `ERR_SSL_*`; HTTP goes out only after the handshake). Such a submit is `submit_failed` with its budget share released, like an HTTP error, and the run stops submitting (`netStop`, exit 1, the unsubmitted sprites named): the next submits would fail the same way. A reset, a closed socket or a timeout after connecting stays `submit_unconfirmed` (ART-W3-3). *Alternative:* stop after any transport failure (a single flaky reset would end a long run). *Reason:* an offline run marked every sprite paid for good, since fal's history never had them, and `spend.total_usd` (which the game briefs subtract from their caps) over-counted.
- **2026-10-21 (W4r2): A demoted --select choice is fetched again and selected (W4r2 R25).** When a `--select` choice gives way because its image could not be fetched (ART-W3-4), the row keeps `"reselect": true`; every later run that includes the sprite fetches its stored image URL again for free and selects it (`selected_by: "user"`) once that works. `--select` also accepts a paid result whose image is not fetched yet (`download_failed`, or another recoverable status, with an image URL): it is marked `reselect` and the current usable selection stays until a run fetches it. A user's `--select` of a usable row, or a new or recovered result for the sprite, drops any waiting choice. *Alternatives:* select a `download_failed` row at once (pixelate would have no usable result meanwhile). *Reason:* the warning and the docs told the user to `--select` the old result again, which `--select` refused and no run made possible.

### workout

Short ADRs for the reference game Workout (`src/games/workout/`, `content/workout/`, `assets/workout/`, `tests/games/workout/`; BRIEF 3.7).

- **2026-10-21: Sessions are explicit phase lists in JSON, validated strictly at load.** Each content id has `content/workout/sessions/<id>.json` with every phase written out (no repeat syntax), or a `zwo` path. Unknown keys, a game-written COOLDOWN, `lap` other than true, a missing label, FREE phases in ERG and a missing 5-minute 50 → 65% warm-up ramp (unless `warmup_only`, which e2e uses) are rejected with one `push_error` listing every problem. *Alternatives:* a compact repeat syntax; building plans in code. *Reason:* the files read like BRIEF 3.7's table, the schema test compares them line by line, and a typo can't silently change a training plan.
- **2026-10-21: Easy 30's main set is 360 / 30 / 330 / 30 / 330 / 30 / 90 s.** The three FREE sprints start at minutes 6, 12 and 18 of the 20-minute main set and are their own SIM flat phases with laps. *Alternative:* sprints as directive SIM blocks inside one ERG phase. *Reason:* plan phases get FIT laps, the timeline shows them, the PlanValidator checks their switches, and Workout needs no directives at all.
- **2026-10-21: Workout sends no directives.** The plan does all the trainer work (ERG targets, SIM sprints, the course). No `cadence_band` either. *Reason:* the reference game should prove the plan path; every directive is a chance for a refusal the e2e gate would flag.
- **2026-10-21: In band.** ERG: power within ±5% of telemetry's `applied_target_w` (before the governor reports one, or while a ramped switch still has the trainer in SIM, the planned target × 0.85 for an unverified FTP). Rolling road (SIM WORK): power within the session band, 76–105% FTP. FREE sprints: cadence ≥ 60 rpm. Rescue, AFK and a dropped connection count as out of band and nothing else (a stagger). The warm-up counts toward the score; the platform's cool-down doesn't. *Alternative:* a few seconds of grace at each interval start. *Reason:* BRIEF 3.7 defines the band literally; the governor's slew and the mock's 2 s lag cost a compliant rider well under 10%.
- **2026-10-21: The group grade includes bots.** The group score is the average session time in band over every slot, so a solo ride with bots grades the whole group. Each row also carries its own grade in `stats`.
- **2026-10-21: Sprint scoring uses SprintRule's threshold with Workout's own streak counter.** A sprint is a hit once power stayed ≥ 1.2 × FTP for 3 s in a row; rows report the peak, the best streak and the time above. *Alternative:* `SprintRule.update/held_s/best_s`. *Reason:* SprintRule is a W1 stub here; `threshold_w()` already works, and window 0 (instantaneous) is exactly the per-step check, so the result is the same once SprintRule lands.
- **2026-10-21: Power bands on the gauge.** Rolling road shows 76–105% FTP; a FREE sprint after the first 300 s shows 120–160% FTP (MockTrainer's `erg` profile then rides the band centre, so bots sprint); a sprint inside the warm-up (e2e) shows none, so the rider stays easy there. Entering an ERG phase clears a band Workout set; the platform's cool-down clears the rest (OWNERSHIP 3.1 C27), so Workout sends nothing in the cool-down.
- **2026-10-21: Rolling road's course.** Three seeded sine waves (wavelengths 1.5–3, 3.5–7 and 8–15 km) sampled every 250 m over 50 km, normalized to exactly −2…+6% and rounded to 0.1%. The seed lives in the session file, so every rider and peer gets the same road. 50 km covers 30 minutes at 1000 W of P_eff on the steepest descent. *Alternative:* a random walk. *Reason:* smooth rolling hills, both limits reached, deterministic.
- **2026-10-21: Ride statistics.** Rows start from `GameResults.from_stats(ctx.stats, players)`; any value the platform reports as 0 is filled from Workout's own `WorkoutPowerStats` (kJ, average and normalized power, best 1/5/20-minute power from 1 Hz averages of the per-step telemetry). *Reason:* results stay correct whether or not the SessionStats tracker is wired, and tests can check them with synthetic frames.
- **2026-10-21: XP and progression.** XP = kJ + session time in band (%) + 10 per sprint hit. `ctx.progress` keeps totals (rides, kJ, XP, time in band, sprints hit), a level per 1000 XP, the best grade and cosmetic pennant unlocks (first ride, 5 rides, a grade S, 10 sprints, 5000 kJ); the newest unlock is the equipped pennant the view draws for the local rider.
- **2026-10-21: Snapshots are all ints and carry the timeline every 20th time.** Rows hold distance and course distance in decimetres, speed in cm/s, flags, time in band in permille, power and cadence; `int_fields` declares `t`, `p`, `pt`, `r[][]` and `tl[][]`. The timeline (`tl`, `tll`) goes out with the first snapshot and every 20th after it. *Reason:* a client can't always load the content itself (a `zwo:<path>` file exists only on the host), and a rejoining client gets only the latest snapshot.
- **2026-10-21: Imported .zwo phases are post-processed, then validated again.** Every phase gets `lap = true` and a label (`WORKOUT_LBL_BLOCK`, "Block n"), an imported Cooldown block becomes RECOVERY (the platform appends the real cool-down), and a SIM flat FreeRide becomes a FREE phase (cadence band, sprint scoring). The result goes through the same phase rules as a session file. Tests inject a parser while the platform importer (WorkoutImport, renamed ZwoImport in W4) was a W1 stub; `tests/games/workout/integration/` runs the real one.
- **2026-10-21: Workout's own parallax layer.** `WorkoutParallaxLayer` tiles a procedurally drawn texture and scrolls it by a world x the view sets. *Alternative:* world2d's `ParallaxStrip`. *Reason:* `ParallaxStrip` had no W0 stub to code against, so a reference would not parse in W1; the swap is a small change once world-kits lands.
- **2026-10-21: on_rescue and on_afk hold until their false call (hardening review).** The callbacks set flags on the rider that `WorkoutRules` ORs with telemetry's `rescue`, `afk` and `connected`, so a rider the platform marks AFK (a drop, a rejoin before fresh pedaling) or rescued is out of band and drawn that way until the platform clears it, even if telemetry doesn't say so yet. *Alternative:* telemetry flags only (the first build overwrote the callback's effect on the next step). *Reason:* BRIEF 3.6 makes the callbacks the platform's word on staggers; nothing but time in band is taken away either way.
- **2026-10-21: In Rolling road the ride view moves riders on the trainer's applied SIM grade (hardening review).** `WorkoutRules` integrates each rider's `SpeedModel` on telemetry's `applied_grade_pct` while the trainer reports SIM in a course phase (the course grade under the rider only until then), and counts course distance from the phase start. *Alternative:* the raw course grade at Workout's own distance (the first build). *Reason:* the rider's own speed model follows `course_profile` by P_eff on the applied grade (difficulty-scaled, gears included); on the raw grade Workout's riders fell kilometres behind over 30 minutes of climbing, so the hill on screen no longer matched the grade on the trainer.
- **2026-10-21: Imported block labels are plain text.** "Block n" comes from `WORKOUT_LBL_BLOCK` when the strings table is registered and from a literal fallback otherwise (a headless run), never the bare key, because the label is also the FIT lap name.
- **2026-10-21: Ride view scales.** Riders and the course profile at 2 m per art pixel (the local rider at x 200), the course elevation exaggerated 2.5 px per metre, parallax at 3 px per metre at factor 1, and the interval blocks at 0.5 px per plan second (about 21 minutes across the screen). Pennants are polygon lists drawn at 1.5 × their base size (pole heights staggered by slot so a bunch stays readable), and the picker card rasterizes the same shapes at 2 ×. `src/games/workout/tools/render_view.gd` renders the view from a snapshot fixture (windowed) for a look at the art.
- **2026-10-21 (W2): The ride view uses world2d's ParallaxStrip; sprints use the real SprintRule.** Supersedes "Workout's own parallax layer" and "Sprint scoring uses SprintRule's threshold with Workout's own streak counter". The five backdrop strips are `ParallaxStrip`s (top edge at the old `top_y`, `cover_width` 640, no drift, scroll snapped to whole art pixels) and `WorkoutParallaxLayer` is gone; `WorkoutSprintTracker` feeds `SprintRule` (window 0) on the sprint's own clock and reads `held_s()`/`best_s()`, keeping its own total of time held. *Alternative:* keep Workout's stand-ins. *Reason:* both platform classes exist now (B 3.9, SH1); the view scrolls the same way (rounded instead of floored, under one art pixel) and the sprint results are unchanged (the tests that pinned them pass as they are).

#### 2026-10-21 (W4): Rolling road normalizes its course over an 8 km window

- Decision: `WorkoutCourse` takes an optional `window_m` (a multiple of `step_m`, at most `length_m`). The road is generated over [0, window_m], normalized to exactly span the session's grades there, and repeated back and forth (mirrored, so no grade jumps at a seam) up to `length_m`. Rolling road uses 8 km, which even a 120 W P_eff rider passes in the 30-minute phase.
- Alternatives: shorter wavelengths over 50 km (the extremes still land wherever the waves put them); repeat the window without mirroring (a grade jump at every seam).
- Reason: W3 WO-3: normalized over 50 km, the first -2 % point lay at 36 km, beyond every rider's 30 minutes, so the road ridden spanned about -1..+6 %.

#### 2026-10-21 (W4): Only a FREE phase of at most 30 s is a sprint

- Decision: `WorkoutSession.is_sprint(phase)` is a FREE phase of at most 30 s (BRIEF 3.7's sprints). Only sprints get the 120-160 % band, the sprint banner and countdown and SprintRule scoring. A longer FREE phase (an imported FreeRide) is free riding: no band, `WORKOUT_BANNER_FREE`, no sprint result; its time in band stays cadence >= 60 rpm.
- Alternatives: a `sprint` flag in session files (a .zwo has no such flag); a 60 s limit (a 60 s FreeRide is not a sprint either).
- Reason: W3 WO-6: a 10-minute FreeRide told the rider to hold 120-160 % FTP for 10 minutes.

#### 2026-10-21 (W4): Imported ERG blocks below the floor ride the floor

- Decision: `WorkoutSession.from_zwo` raises an imported ERG block (target or ramp end) below 30 % FTP to 30 % and lists it with the importer's clamps (`WORKOUT_CLAMP_FLOOR`) instead of rejecting the file.
- Alternatives: accept 0 % in Workout's own re-check and leave it to the governor's floor (the preview would show a 0 % block the rider never rides).
- Reason: W3 WO-5 and BRIEF 3.7 ("clamps each block to 4.6 ... listing each clamp"): a single `Power="0"` rest rejected the whole workout. A floor clamp in the importer itself belongs to its owner.

#### 2026-10-21 (W4): A long timeline goes in snapshot parts; results show best powers

- Decision: a timeline of more than 120 phases travels in parts (`tlo`, `tln`) on consecutive snapshots at the start of each 20-snapshot cycle; a client sets its timeline once every part is in. `WorkoutResults` adds the best 1-, 5- and 20-minute power (only for windows the ride covered) and every sprint's peak to each row's display stats.
- Reason: W3 NET-3 (a 400-block .zwo pushed the timeline snapshot past 16 KB) and WO-2 (BRIEF 3.7 lists those results; the results screen draws only the row's stats beyond its fixed columns).

#### 2026-10-21 (W4 round 2): The preview and the strip tell a free ride from a sprint

- Decision: `WorkoutSession.describe_phase` previews a FREE phase longer than 30 s as a free ride (`WORKOUT_PREVIEW_FREE_RIDE`, "Free ride, flat, virtual gears") and keeps "Free sprint" for sprints; `WorkoutArt.phase_color` gives it the new `ZONE_FREE` (a muted grey-green) and the ride view's strip draws it low (55 % on the strip's scale) and hatched, like the course's "no fixed target" block, where a sprint stands at 130 % in `ZONE_SPRINT`.
- Alternatives: the endurance colour (reads as an ERG target the rider must hold); leave the strip and fix only the text.
- Reason: W4r2 R15: after WO-6 the HUD, the rules and the results treated a long FREE phase as free riding, but the lobby still previewed the built-in zwo_sample's 60 s FreeRide as a "Free sprint" and the strip drew it as one.

#### 2026-10-21 (W4 round 2): A floor-raised block is relabelled and listed like the importer's clamps

- Decision: `_raise_to_floor` renames a block whose label ends in its old range (the importer's labels do: "Steady 0%" becomes "Steady 30%", "Ramp 0-20%" becomes "Ramp 30%"), turns a ramp raised to the floor at both ends into a steady block, and writes the clamp line in the importer's shape: "Block 3 (Steady 0%): 0% to 30%, the trainer's 30% FTP floor" (block number from the phase's `zwo_block` meta, else its position; just "Block 3: ..." when the label is the block label).
- Alternatives: keep the importer's label and add the floor to the banner text only (the FIT lap name would still say 0 %).
- Reason: W4r2 R17: the preview, the in-ride banner and the FIT lap name said "Steady 0%" while the target was 30 %, and the ramp line read "0 -> 20% -> 30 -> 30% FTP".

- **2026-10-21 (W4 final): A client's ride view interpolates the other riders and blends its own.** On a client, `WorkoutGame.apply_snapshot` records every rider's distance per snapshot in `WorkoutSnapHistory` (8 entries, keyed by the snapshot's tick × 0.05 s, the host's game time; a gap over 1.5 s starts it over). `WorkoutRideView.advance(delta)` (called from `_process`) runs a render clock on the frame delta behind the newest snapshot by `interp_delay()`: the history's mean snapshot spacing, clamped to 0.1–1.4 s. That is 0.1 s at 10 Hz (NetSession's documented `host_time() - 0.1`) and about 1 s under the 8-rider load, where snapshots reach a client about once a second; the 1.4 s cap stays under the 1.5 s resync, so a growing delay never sends the clock back. The clock catches up softly (τ 0.25 s) when a late snapshot held it, jumps only when 1.5 s off, never passes the newest snapshot and never goes back; the other riders are drawn linearly between the two snapshots around it. The local rider (and the camera, parallax and road with it) is drawn from its own prediction: it moves by its predicted speed each frame and blends toward the client state's distance (the host's latest plus `client_predict` since) with τ 0.3 s, never moves backwards, stops when the state has not moved for 0.15 s (a pause), and jumps only when the error passes 10 m. Rules, snapshots and the host/SOLO view are unchanged; the state still takes each snapshot in full. *Alternatives:* call `NetSession.snapshot_pair_at(host_time() - 0.1)` (a game has no NetSession handle in `GameContext`, and contracts are frozen); blend inside `WorkoutSnapshot.apply`/`WorkoutRules.predict` (changes the rules state and the snapshot tests that pin it; the error is only visual); keep snapping (BRIEF 5.1 asks for soft reconciliation); a fixed 0.1 s delay (under the 8-rider load the other riders waited at the newest snapshot for about 0.9 s of every second, then hurried on); the longest recent spacing instead of the mean (fewer waits when spacing varies, but up to 1.4 s of delay at any load). *Reason:* DoD finding confirmed[13], BRIEF 5.1: "clients predict their own rider locally and reconcile softly toward the host", and GAME_SDK 12's "interpolate everything else between snapshots". Before, a client's markers and background snapped to every 10 Hz snapshot.

### fishing

Turbo Reel's ADRs are in `docs/decisions/fishing.md`; the v0.3.0 ones:

- **2026-10-27 (v0.3.0): The boat is a rigid body on the water.** Decision: `FishingBoat` simulates surge, heave and pitch with forces (turbo thrust 150 × P_eff / (u + 6 m/s), hull drag tuned to 15 m/s × (P_eff / 250 W)^0.6 in displacement, a displacement hump, line tension) and force-driven spring-dampers, plus a planing latch (on at 11.9 m/s, about 70 % FTP-equivalent; off under 11.3 m/s) that cuts drag for a 7 % faster cruise; the prop never rises above 0.2 m depth. *Alternatives:* the scripted trim spring with kicks; a pose table by speed. *Reason:* the owner wants a boat that reacts to pedalling exactly; poses, wake and tow follow from one model the view reads through the snapshot.
- **2026-10-27 (v0.3.0): Big-fish surges as an ERG haptic pull.** Decision: a run or jump by a fish of 30 kg or more requests `haptic_pull` +50 % FTP for 2 s (haptics on, not in the first 300 s or a stagger, one per 15 s) and shows `F_SURGE` for 2 s either way; the sims and the e2e check want each pull applied or dropped only for haptics off, warm-up, the haptic cooldown or an unsafe rider, and trace rows with `haptic_pull` may sit above 105 % in a long phase. *Alternatives:* a SIM haptic grade (not in an ERG phase); a target change (a ride target, not a feel). *Reason:* the owner-approved BRIEF 4.6.6 amendment; the governor bounds and spaces it.

### qa

Short ADRs for `tools/e2e/`, `tools/screenshot`, `tools/screenshot.ps1`, `tools/qa/`, `tools/fit_decode/` and `tests/scenarios/` (BRIEF 2, 3.6, 3.8, 7.4, 7.5, 7.7, 7.8).

#### 2026-10-21: One Godot lease for the whole e2e run, peers through tools/godot.sh with the lease token

- Decision: `tools/e2e/run.mjs` takes one lease of 3 slots with `acquire({lease: 3})` from `tools/lib/godot_lock.mjs` before the first game and releases it after the last. Every peer, the restarted client and the GDScript FIT decoder fallback run through `tools/godot.sh` with `WATTLANDS_GODOT_LEASE_TOKEN=<handle.token>`, so none of them queues again. Each peer gets its own `--log-file <peer dir>/godot.log` (Godot 4.7.2 supports it) besides its own `--profile-dir`.
- Alternatives: one lease per game; starting the Godot binary directly.
- Reason: BRIEF 2 asks for one lease for all the harness's Godot instances; starting Godot directly would duplicate the wrapper's env, user-dir, timeout and `-d` rules (docs/decisions/tooling.md). A per-peer log file keeps three processes that share the checkout's `.userdata` from writing one `godot.log`.

#### 2026-10-21: Same event sequence = the shared events, compared without arrival times

- Decision: events of types that are a peer's own view (session and app states, peer churn, uploads, rides, HUD messages, roster, snapshots; `PER_PEER_EVENT_TYPES` in `tools/e2e/lib/checks.mjs`) are left out; every other event (`phase_started` per rider, game events, `game_phase_changed`, `results`) must be identical on the host and `client1` after removing arrival-time keys (`t`, `wall_t`, ...) and the recording peer of a `results` event, numbers equal within 1e-6 relative. `final_state` is compared within 2 % (or 0.01) without the per-peer keys (`local_peer`, `fit_path`, `distance_m`, `phase_index`, ...). The restarted client must hold a tail of the host's sequence that ends with the results, after at most one replayed `phase_started` for its own rider.
- Alternatives: an inclusion list of event types; comparing raw events.
- Reason: game event types are open-ended (each game names its own), so only an exclusion list works for "any registered game"; arrival times differ by design. A rejoined client cannot have the events from before its restart; allowing more than one leading replay would hide gaps.

#### 2026-10-21: Strict log scanning

- Decision: any `SCRIPT ERROR`, `Parse Error`, `Debugger Break`, `Ignoring script` or line starting with `ERROR:` / `USER ERROR:` (push_error output) in a peer's stdout, stderr or Godot log fails the run, including the killed client's output before the kill; `WARNING: ... desync` lines and `rate_limited` anywhere (peers and relay) fail too.
- Alternatives: only `SCRIPT ERROR`; an allow-list option.
- Reason: BRIEF 7.7 says "no script errors" and lists push_error output; an allow-list flag would be a way to weaken the gate (BRIEF 1, 7).

#### 2026-10-21: The auto-upload peer is client1, with the mock's default test client

- Decision: exactly one peer, `client1` by default (`--auto-upload=`), gets `--strava-client-id=12345 --strava-client-secret=mock-client-secret --strava-auto-upload`. The harness lists the mock's uploads at `GET /admin/uploads` (other common admin paths are tried when that answers 404) before stopping it, and expects exactly one; the auto-upload peer's report must show one upload in state `uploaded` or `duplicate`, the others none. The secret is masked in logs and the report.
- Alternatives: the host uploads; dummy credentials.
- Reason: `client1` is never killed, so its upload runs once, from a client (the host path is covered by the rides tests). `server/mock_strava` checks the client secret on the token exchange, and these are its documented test credentials, never a real app's.

#### 2026-10-21: Kill and restart

- Decision: the victim is `client2`. It is killed `--kill-after` (25) seconds after it prints `WATTLANDS_STATE running` (or `session`, `playing`, `started`, `warmup`), with its whole process tree (`taskkill /T /F` on Windows; on POSIX SIGTERM to its process group first, since the lock runner puts Godot in a group of its own and kills that tree on SIGTERM, then SIGKILL), and restarted after `--restart-delay` (3) seconds with the same `--profile-dir` and code. The reconnect passes when it prints the same `WATTLANDS_PEER` id again, is not refused with `session_in_progress`, exits 0 and gets the results.
- Reason: BRIEF 7.7 and ARCHITECTURE "Scripted peers"; a SIGKILL to the peer's group alone would orphan Godot on Linux.

#### 2026-10-21: Duration excludes the cool-down as observed

- Decision: the 6-minute limit applies to the game's wall time minus the cool-down, measured from the host's `WATTLANDS_STATE cooldown` line to its next state line (or `--cooldown-s`, 180, when the host never reports it).
- Reason: BRIEF 7.7 excludes "the appended 3-minute cool-down"; measuring it avoids crediting a slow cool-down to the game.

#### 2026-10-21: A test seam instead of mocking Godot

- Decision: `--peer-cmd`, `--relay-cmd`, `--strava-cmd` and `--fit-check-cmd` replace the four external programs; `tools/e2e/fixtures/` holds stand-ins that speak the same contracts and inject faults, so `node --test tools/e2e/test` exercises the whole orchestration (lease, room code, kill and restart, every check, `e2e_check.mjs`, exit codes) in about 40 s without Godot. Fixtures live outside `test/` because `node --test` treats every file under a `test/` folder as a test.
- Alternatives: unit tests of the pure parts only.
- Reason: the orchestration is where an e2e harness breaks; the real path was also smoke-run against Godot 4.7.2, the real relay and the real mock Strava from their builders' worktrees.

#### 2026-10-21: tools/screenshot is a shim over one Node core

- Decision: `tools/screenshot` (bash) and `tools/screenshot.ps1` both run `tools/qa/screenshot.mjs`, which validates the flags, always adds `--mock-trainer` (refusing non-mock values, `--allow-hw`, `--allow-network`, `-d` and `--headless`), removes `FAL_KEY` and `WATTLANDS_ALLOW_ART`, and starts Godot windowed through `tools/godot.sh` or `tools/godot.ps1` with `--resolution 1920x1080 -s tools/qa/screenshot_main.gd`. On Linux without `DISPLAY` or `WAYLAND_DISPLAY` it wraps the run in `xvfb-run` (with `LIBGL_ALWAYS_SOFTWARE=1`) when installed, else prints `screenshot: skipped` and exits 0; `WATTLANDS_SCREENSHOT_NO_DISPLAY=1` forces the skip. The Godot side skips the same way on the headless display server.
- Alternatives: two parallel shell implementations.
- Reason: the same pattern as `tools/run_tests` (one tested core, identical behaviour in both shells).

#### 2026-10-21: Screenshot modes and names

- Decision: game mode (`--game`, `--at`) drives `GameSimRunner` with `headless = false`, steps as fast as possible (in 50 ms slices between frames) to `--settle` (1 s) before each `--at`, then one fixed step per 0.05 s of frame time, and saves the window's frame (HUD included, the host's view) as `<game>_<content>_<players>p_<at>s.png` in `build/screenshots/<game>/`. `--scene=<res path>` saves `<folder>_<scene>.png` (so `world2d_demo.png` and `world3d_demo.png`) in `build/screenshots/scenes/` after `--frames` (30) frames; `--app-screen=title|picker|lobby` starts the main scene, switches `TrainerService` to a mock and calls `App.goto`, saving `app_<screen>.png` in `build/screenshots/app/`. The driver sets a throwaway `Paths` override in `_init` (before the autoloads start) unless `--profile-dir` is given, and deletes it on exit. Exit 0 when every shot was saved, 1 when one failed or none was taken, 2 on bad flags.
- Reason: BRIEF 3.6 and 7.8, docs/OWNERSHIP.md TL2 and C13; the user's real profile is never touched.

#### 2026-10-21: The FIT decoder reports structure and Wattlands checks separately

- Decision: `QaFitDecoder` (`tools/fit_decode/lib/fit_decoder.gd`) decodes any FIT file generically (header and file CRCs, definition and data messages, compressed timestamps, both byte orders, developer fields, chained files) and keeps structural problems in `decode_errors`; `errors` adds the Wattlands checks (file_id type activity, first message file_id, records with non-decreasing timestamps, one session with sport 2 and sub_sport 58, `num_laps` matching, laps with start and duration, one activity, `--expect-laps`). Missing device_info or timer events are warnings. `decode.gd` prints a text summary or `--json` (one line; Godot prints its own header first, so `--out=<file>` is the clean way to read it), and for `tools/fit_check/check.mjs` it implements rides' fallback contract (`--file=`, `--json-out=`, decode errors only, Unix times, exit 0), documented in `tools/fit_check/README.md`.
- Alternatives: decode only the fields the encoder writes; one error list.
- Reason: an independent decoder that only knew the encoder's layout would share its bugs. check.mjs applies its own checks to the fallback's output, so that output must carry decode errors only.

JSON schema of `decode.gd --json` / `--out` (version 1):

```
{decoder: "wattlands-fit-decode-gd", version: 1, ok, failed, files: [{
  path, ok, errors: [String], decode_errors: [String], warnings: [String], is_fit, integrity,
  header: {size, protocol_version, profile_version, data_size, data_type, header_crc, header_crc_ok},
  crc_ok, segments, messages: {name: count}, file_id: {type, manufacturer, product, serial_number, time_created},
  records, record_span_s, record_gaps, record_timestamps_monotonic, has_gps, first_record_ts, last_record_ts,
  laps: [{index, name, start_time, start_s, duration_s, timer_s, distance_m, work_kj, avg_power_w, max_power_w, avg_cadence_rpm, max_cadence_rpm}],
  session: {start_time, timestamp, sport, sub_sport, elapsed_s, timer_s, distance_m, work_kj, avg_power_w, max_power_w,
            avg_cadence_rpm, max_cadence_rpm, avg_speed_mps, max_speed_mps, normalized_power_w, num_laps, first_lap_index} | {},
  sessions, activity: {timestamp, timer_s, num_sessions, type, event, event_type} | {}, activity_count,
  events, timer_starts, timer_stops, device_infos}]}
```

FIT timestamps (`start_time`, `*_ts`) are raw FIT seconds there; `start_s` is relative to the session start.

#### 2026-10-21: Scenario tests live in tests/scenarios and are W2 gates

- Decision: the BRIEF 7.4/7.5 scenarios are GUT tests in `tests/scenarios/workout/` that drive `GameSimRunner` tick by tick through its frozen API and read each tick's `SafetyGovernor.status()` and MockTrainer samples on the rider's own platform clock (`GameHost.local_rider(peer)`; the map itself became private in W4 SAF-4). They decode each FIT in process with `QaFitDecoder` (no second Godot process) and compare it with `RideRecorderApi.summary()`. The decoder's and the helpers' own tests live in `tests/scenarios/unit/` so the full suite collects them; the W1 test command runs only that folder, because GameSimRunner, Workout, the governor and the recorder are stubs in this worktree. Report keys are read through one tolerant accessor file (`tests/scenarios/lib/scenario_lib.gd`) that matches sdk-runtime's report (`plans`, `phases`, `trace` keyed by peer id as strings).
- Rules encoded: compliant rider (erg 1.0, verified FTP 200, one bot) per built-in session: time in band >= 90 %, grade A or S, warm-up limits on every tick, no duration limit ever latching and an independent count of time above 105 % and 120 % FTP; coasting: floor `max(30 % FTP, 50 W)` reached within 3 s +/- one tick of the first not_pedaling sample, pause (SOLO) or AFK (two humans, HOST) within 10 s +/- one tick, for a `zero` rider and one who stops after 60 s of pedaling; rescue: one 8 s drop to 30 rpm per ERG phase (`at_target_pct=60` where a ramp crosses 60 %), rescue exactly when the applied target was >= 60 % FTP at the collapse, never a rescue without a collapse; a tree pause keeps samples and control responses flowing; a sample `.zwo` imports with listed clamps and plays inside them; FIT1 lap count (lap-true phases that started + lap markers + 1 for the cool-down).
- Reason: BRIEF 7.4 and 7.5 need tick precision (3 s plus one 0.05 s tick), which the 1 Hz report trace can't give.

#### 2026-10-21: Hardening review (W1)

- **A rejoin's replayed phase may arrive late.** The relay forwards broadcasts to a client as soon as it is back, before the host has handled the rejoin and sent its `phase`, so the replayed `phase_started` can follow a few live events. `matchTail` now accepts at most one replayed `phase_started` for the rejoined rider among its first 8 events (`REPLAY_WINDOW`), and only when the host emitted that same event. *Alternative:* only a leading replay (the first version), which would fail a correct reconnect by timing alone. *Reason:* the check is about missing or reordered shared events, not about when a replay lands.
- **The upload check reads the mock's verdict.** Besides exactly one upload, `strava_upload` requires what `server/mock_strava` reports for it at `/admin/uploads`: `fit_valid` true, `data_type` fit, `trainer` 1 and no `error`, and the auto-upload peer's activity id must equal the mock's. Keys the mock doesn't report are not checked.
- **Lap names come from the field_description.** `QaFitDecoder` maps developer fields to names with the `field_description` messages and takes the lap name from the one called `lap_name`, instead of assuming developer index 0, field 0; without a description a lap has no name, as with the FIT SDK. Every timer event that isn't a start counts as a stop, matching the SDK model in `tools/fit_check/check.mjs`.
- **The decoder is checked against the FIT SDK on real files.** `tests/scenarios/unit/fixtures/` holds two FIT files the rides encoder wrote in W1 (`rides_ten_minute.fit`, a copy of rides' `tools/fit_check/testdata/gdscript_ten_minute_ride.fit`, SHA-256 6af82f30ab06435dcc29f2401fd63142f4b82c7a9d87cb264badbd36715d52bf; `rides_paused_dropout.fit`, the output of rides' `test_paused_ride_with_a_dropout_for_fit_check`, SHA-256 af1c832da4d537e179796c6b4945454964911e9bbd66bfaa9543d1634e94e888) and `rides_fit_sdk.json`, what `@garmin/fitsdk` 21.218.0 decoded from them through rides' `testdata/fake_decode.mjs` (the fallback contract's shape). `test_fit_decoder_vs_sdk.gd` requires `rides_contract()` to equal it field by field. *Alternative:* qa's own writer only. *Reason:* the writer and the decoder share one author; the SDK is the reference `check.mjs` uses, so the fallback must judge a file the same way.
- **Stricter W2 scenarios.** The session scenarios also require the governor's warm-up to be on for the first 300 s after the first pedaling sample (2 s margin) and the 150 % and long-phase 105 % caps on every tick; Workout's own `.zwo` session (found through `content/workout/sessions/<id>.json` with a `zwo` key) is played end to end through `GameSimRunner` and its clamps must appear in Workout's session preview. The tree-pause scenario checks the system nodes BRIEF 4.1 names (GameHost, LocalRider, SafetyGovernor, MockTrainer), not `GameSimRunner`, which is the test's driver, and proves the pause is real with an ordinary pausable node. The scenarios use the GameRegistry autoload, never a private registry, and make sure the game strings are registered before reading a preview (`ensure_game_strings`): a freed `WlGameRegistry` instance removes the shared Translation resources the autoload also added, so later lookups return raw keys (reported to sdk-runtime). Run against an overlay of every W1 branch (21 Oct 2026 heads), all thirteen W2 scenarios (90 tests with the unit tests) pass after these two test-side fixes.

#### 2026-10-21 (W2 round 2): `--app-screen=session` shoots the app's real session screen

- Decision: `tools/screenshot --app-screen=session [--app-role=client|host|solo] [--app-game] [--content] [--at] [--app-overlay=menu|panic|none]` runs the main scene windowed and opens a real `AppSessionScreen`: as a client, the app's own lobby screen joins a room that an in-process headless host (`WlNetSession` + `GameHost` on a `LoopbackTransport` hub shared with the app's NetSession) opens, readies on `pedal_start` and follows the host's `session_start`, so the HUD is the one a client builds from that message; as a host, an in-process headless client joins the app's lobby and runs a CLIENT GameHost from the app's `session_start`. Shots at each `--at` (the app session's platform seconds, default 30) plus one with the ride menu or the panic overlay open. The logic lives in `tools/qa/screenshot_session.gd` (`QaScreenshotSession`).
- Alternatives: GameSimRunner windowed (its clients are always headless and it has no overlay); a scripted peer (`AppScriptedScreen` has neither).
- Reason: BRIEF 7.8 and the round-2 check of a CLIENT HUD built from `session_start`, with the ride menu or panic overlay above the HUD, need the app's session screen itself.

#### 2026-10-21 (W4): The e2e harness's relay command follows the Node version (W3 PKG-8)

- Decision: the default `--relay-cmd` is `node --experimental-strip-types server/relay/src/main.ts` on a Node without default type stripping (22.6-22.17, 23.0-23.5) and `node server/relay/src/main.ts` otherwise (`defaultRelayCmd()`); `--relay-cmd` still overrides it. `tools/e2e/test/relay_cmd.test.mjs` starts the real relay as Node 22.12 would (`--no-experimental-strip-types` first) and shows the old default fails there.
- Alternatives: raise the minimum Node to 22.18 (BRIEF 2 names 22.12).
- Reason: BRIEF 5.3 asks for the flag on 22.12-22.17; the harness never passed it. `tools/run_tests`' relay node-suite step needs the same (the lead's file).

#### 2026-10-21 (W4r2): tools/screenshot --app-screen=results (W4r2 R26)

- Decision: `--app-screen=results [--app-riders=<1..8>]` opens the app's real results screen through `App.goto("results", ...)` with a Workout-like GameResults from `tools/qa/screenshot_results.gd` (`QaScreenshotResults.params`): ten intervals, best 1/5/20 min, four-digit sprint peaks, 16-character names, two unlocks, a real-length FIT path, and the host-left notice from two riders on, so the shot is the tallest page a rider can get. It prints the page height, the fit level and the lowest line, and saves `app_results_<n>p.png`.
- Alternatives: run a whole session to its results (about 30 minutes of sim time per shot).
- Reason: the results page must fit 1080p for 1 to 8 riders; a windowed screenshot shows what the headless layout test measures.

## The v1 run (from 31 Oct 2026)

Merged from the platform fragments in `docs/decisions/`. The games' decisions of this run stay in their fragments: `fishing.md`, `siege.md`, `tank.md` and `workout.md`, and `audio-fishing.md`, `audio-siege.md`, `audio-tank.md` and `audio-workout.md`.

### gate

- **2026-10-31: A cap file, not only an env var.** `machineCap(dir)` returns `WATTLANDS_GODOT_LOCK_CAP` if set (with its existing error on a bad value), else the positive integer in `<lock dir>/cap`, else the computed default (4 or 2). `node tools/lib/godot_lock.mjs set-cap <n>` writes the file atomically, and `set-cap 0` deletes it. A bad file is ignored with one stderr note per content, so a typo can't stop the machine. *Alternative:* set the env var in every shell. *Reason:* every waiter computes its own cap, and under strict FIFO one shell at cap 4 stalls everyone queued behind it until held drops to 3. A file is one value for the whole machine, whichever checkout a shell runs from, and waiters re-read it every poll. The default with no file is unchanged. Every checkout needs this code before the cap goes above 4, so it lands on main first.
- **2026-10-31: `machineCap` reads the cap of the dir `acquire` was given,** not always the machine's. A temp lock dir (tests, a gate's node lane) never picks up the machine's file. The one existing test that read the real lock dir now uses a fresh one; what it asserts is unchanged.
- **2026-10-31: `WATTLANDS_USERDATA_DIR` replaces `<checkout>/.userdata` for non-export runs,** in both wrappers. Empty counts as unset, and a relative value resolves against the caller's directory, since Godot changes directory to `--path` and would otherwise see a different folder. Export runs still keep the real user dir, because they need the installed templates. *Alternative:* one checkout per concurrent run. *Reason:* Godot's default file log (`user://logs/godot.log`) and `user://` writes collide between concurrent runs in one checkout, which gate shards are.
- **2026-10-31: A display sub-lock of 4 for screenshots,** in `<lock dir>/display`, taken in `tools/qa/screenshot.mjs` before the Godot slot, with its own fixed cap that no env var or cap file moves. Dry runs, skips and refusals take none. It waits 1800 s by default, or `--lock-timeout` if given (so a caller who set a short wait gets it here too), then exits 75 with the same message as the Godot lock. *Alternative:* let windows fill the Godot cap. *Reason:* at cap 16 that could mean 16 windows on one GPU and desktop. Isolation between windows is already sound, so this guards resources, not correctness. Slot holders never wait for the display lock, so the two can't deadlock. No Godot process starts in its tests: `main()` takes a `run` stand-in.
- **2026-10-31: `tools/gate` next to `tools/run_tests`, not instead of it.** Lanes run each step's existing verdict code (`run_tests.mjs --only=<step>`, `evaluateGut`) concurrently. `tools/run_tests` keeps its behaviour with no flag, and CI and the Final checkpoint still run it literally. *Alternative:* parallelism inside `run_tests`. *Reason:* the literal serial gate stays the fallback for everything, and a gate bug can't change what `tools/run_tests` says.
- **2026-10-31: A lane passes only on positive evidence.** `laneVerdict` needs exit 0, the pass line in the last summary, and a PASS row for each selected step (import may WARN). A SKIP row fails, and so do "gdtoolkit unavailable, skipped" and both workflow-check skip strings. That is stricter than `run_tests`, where those skips pass. *Reason:* a stray nested env or a missing tool must not read as green.
- **2026-10-31: `fast` runs all of GUT in 8 shards and skips only sim and e2e of untouched games.** A shard runs under `evaluateGut` with `narrow: true`, then `aggregateGut` requires every file in some JUnit, none in two, and the sum at or above max(local, tracked floor). A group never splits, so the writers of one `res://build/<dir>` stay in one process. `strict` keeps one GUT process, because only it catches cross-file state leaks. *Alternative:* touched games plus a smoke set. *Reason:* all of GUT costs about 2.5 min in shards, so skipping saves little and loses coverage.
- **2026-10-31: `check` with no `--ws` on `main` or `queue*` drops rule 2 (`lead_only`), and `never_edit` stays.** This extends SPEC_GATES 6.1, through a new manifest key `lead_branches`. *Alternative:* add `--ws` to every queue command. *Reason:* `strict` checks `<last floor commit>..HEAD` on the queue, a range holding the lead's own `project.godot`, `CHANGELOG.md` and floor commits, so rule 2 would always fail there. Everything on those branches was landed by the lead. `fast` and `strict` also take `--ws`, `--allow` and `--allow-pin`, passed to their check and written to the stamp, because a `fast` in the queue for a branch that needs an exception must be able to say so.
- **2026-10-31: `tools/lib/safety_pins.json` is denied to the `gate` workstream,** although its `also` covers `tools/lib/**`. *Reason:* otherwise a `tooling/*` branch could weaken a pinned file and re-pin it in the same diff, and "only the lead re-pins" would hold only by habit. A lead branch can still commit it. This is stricter than SPEC_GATES 6.1.
- **2026-10-31: Pins are checked against HEAD and, for uncommitted pinned paths, against the work tree.** The pin lists live in the manifest (`safety_pin_globs`, `append_only_pins`), because `pin-safety` has to know them before any `safety_pins.json` exists. This branch ships no pins: `tools/run_game_sim` is pinned and G1 edits it, so `pin-safety` runs after landing (SPEC_GATES 8.5). `pin-safety` refuses a dirty tree, so it can't bless uncommitted work. A new file under a pinned glob isn't a mismatch: adding a governor test strengthens BRIEF 4.6.
- **2026-10-31: A merge revert skips the area rule too, not only the total rule.** SPEC_GATES 6.4 words the exception for the total only, but a revert also shrinks an area with no `Test-Removal:` trailer, and section 3 requires that revert to land. The tracked floor still applies.
- **2026-10-31: The CR rule reads the git diff only, not untracked files.** `.gitattributes` has `* text=auto eol=lf`, so `git add` normalises an untracked file, and flagging its working copy would be a false red after gdformat on Windows. The other added-text rules do read untracked files.
- **2026-10-31: The lock and art rules also cover extension-less scripts directly under `tools/`** (`tools/run_game_sim`, `tools/gate`), which are bash. SPEC_GATES 6.5 lists only extensions, which would let a flag into exactly those scripts. Stricter, never looser.
- **2026-10-31: `aggregateGut` also fails on a file in two JUnits,** since a file run twice would pad the total toward the floor. SPEC_GATES 2.4 doesn't ask for this.
- **2026-10-31: `matrix` imports first, and a `--scenarios` run stamps `partial`.** *Reason:* with `stamp-ok` skipping `fast`, a fresh queue merge could reach the matrix without an import. A subset of scenarios must not pass `stamp-ok --matrix` as the whole matrix. `matrix` also removes `<ID>_SIMS_OUT` from the script's env, since it deleted that directory's default.
- **2026-10-31: `wt-gc` keeps a worktree at the exact base tip, and any with a live gate,** although SPEC_GATES 4 says merged and clean goes. *Reason:* a builder who has just started from main is both of those with no commits yet, and would lose its worktree at a checkpoint. Landed branches arrive by `--no-ff`, so their tips are never the base tip.
- **2026-10-31: Audio's manifest paths also hold `docs/{decisions,changelog.d}/{tank,workout}.md`.** SPEC_GATES 6.1 has only the `audio*` fragments, but EXECUTION_DAG section 1 says `audio/tank` and `audio/workout` append to those files after the game lands, and rule 3 would fail them. Siege and Fishing stay out, because audio writes only their credits.
- **2026-10-31: `gate_weights.json` comes from a monolith run on this tree** (4834 tests, 979 s of JUnit time, 122 s per shard at 8 shards), not from `wt/siege-hud/build/junit.xml` as SPEC_GATES 2.4 says, because that worktree no longer exists. `tools/gate floor` refreshes it at each checkpoint.
- **2026-10-31: An append-only pin is a position rule, not a subsequence test.** The pinned lines stay in order with nothing between them. Added lines go after the last pinned line of the body, before its final `return`, and can't leave the function or empty what it collected (`return`, `throw`, `process.exit`, `.length =`, `.splice`, `.pop`, `.shift`), except in a branch guarded by a string the pinned lines never name. Two exceptions serve SPEC_TANK's recipe: comments and a one-line `probs.push(...)` go anywhere, since neither changes control flow; and the manifest's `append_only_anchors` accept only such a guarded branch right above three named lines (`directiveProblems`, `appliedProblems`, `classify`), which is what Repairs needs. *Alternative:* the critic's plain "only before the final return". *Reason:* that would have refused the Tank builder's own `if (kind === 'repair')` lines and S5's added push, none of which can switch a rule off. Checked against the live `wt/tank-sims` working copy: it passes. SPEC_GATES 6.3's subsequence wording let a first-line `return` through.
- **2026-10-31: Pins follow what a checker rests on.** `pin-safety` pins the named functions, every top-level function and arrow const they reach, and the thresholds they read. It does not follow through a table, or `CHECKS` would pull in every check of the file. `CHECKS`, `SCENARIOS` and `NODE_SUITES` are tables: entries stay unchanged and new ones are welcome, a repeated key fails (a later key replaces a pinned one), and so does a line that changes the table at run time. `result` and `pick` may move to `tools/lib/sim_report.mjs` as SPEC_CLEANUP's C8 plans: the pin follows the import. *Reason:* with `checkS4` pinned but `humanPeers`, `TOTAL_GRADE_MAX_PCT` or `CHECKS` free, one small edit outside the pinned text switches off every BRIEF 4.6 sim assertion. *Known limit:* this reads JavaScript by lines; it stops an agent in a hurry, not a determined author.
- **2026-10-31: Two partial file pins.** `run_tests.mjs` is hashed without its `NODE_SUITES` statement, so A-BASE can add `tools/audio` (E12). `settings.gd` has line pins on its grade and difficulty ranges and the inverted-range rule instead of a file hash, so audio's `kit` can add its bus defaults. *Alternative:* take `settings.gd` out of audio's paths. *Reason:* SPEC_AUDIO needs that file, and only those lines are BRIEF 4.6.
- **2026-10-31: A pins file must match the manifest at the commit it names.** `check` recomputes every pin at `pinned_at` and compares, so `{"files":{}}`, a dropped glob or a rewritten hash fails, on `main` and `queue` too and whatever `--allow` says. A pins file that history once held and HEAD lacks fails. *Reason:* rule 2 was the only guard, and it does not run on lead branches. This branch still ships no pins, because `tools/run_game_sim` and the gate's own files are pinned and "only the lead pins". Before the first `pin-safety`, `check` says "not pinned yet" as a note, since failing would redden every branch and the acceptance run. `floor` refuses without pins, so the window can't pass a checkpoint. A new file under a pinned glob isn't a mismatch: adding a governor test strengthens BRIEF 4.6. It stays unpinned until the next `pin-safety`. This supersedes the earlier ADR's "no pins at HEAD is a note" for a file that has existed.
- **2026-10-31: A matrix passes on the checker's own rows.** The check phase needs at least one PASS row, no FAIL, in a full matrix no SKIP, and the manifest's `matrix_required_rows` (the rows holding BRIEF 4.6 rules) PASS. Every `<ID>_*` variable is removed from the script's and the pool's env. `tools/run_game_sim` refuses a `WATTLANDS_SIM_PLAN` with no file (exit 2), and the gate creates the file and removes it after each phase, so a value left in a shell can't turn a real run into exit 0 with no report. *Reason:* `TANK_SKIP_CHECK=1` in a shell gave a green `check_sims` row, and a scenario with all its reports missing reads as SKIP. *Evidence:* Siege's 37 reports in `wt/siege-perf` give 9 PASS and 0 SKIP. Tank's and Fishing's full matrices were read, not run.
- **2026-10-31: A `fast` that narrowed anything is partial.** `--lanes`, a `--base` that is not main's commit, a `--ws` other than the branch's own, any `--allow` or `--allow-pin`, and a `--games` that leaves out a game the branch touches. A wider `--games` narrows nothing. The stamp lists the reasons, and `stamp-ok` also refuses a dirty project, a stamp with a missing field, no GUT total, or a matrix with no evidence. `changedSince` uses `--no-renames`, so moving a platform file into a game selects the platform.
- **2026-10-31: `fast` with no `--lanes` always runs `aggregateGut`,** so no GUT process is a red row, never a pass by absence. `listGutFiles` throws on a read error other than a missing `tests/`.
- **2026-10-31: Strict's check starts before the last floor commit, and the floor answers to the highest value in HEAD's history.** *Alternative:* the previous value only. *Reason:* starting at the floor commit itself made base and head equal, so a commit lowering the floor and deleting tests compared clean; and 100 -> 50 -> 60 passes a previous-value test. The one past fall (4461a59, 4592 -> 4283) sits below today's 4834, so this reads green on the real history. A lead who must lower the floor changes this rule on purpose.
- **2026-10-31: The quiet rerun is for budget tests only.** It runs only when every reason is a plain test failure and every failed testcase measures wall time (`Time.get_ticks_usec` in its body or in a function of its file it calls). A SCRIPT ERROR, a crash or a non-budget failure keeps its first verdict. `stamp-ok` shows a rerun and accepts it, with the test names. *Alternative:* move budget tests to their own files. *Reason:* those files belong to Tank and Siege, and reading the failed case gives the same guarantee today without touching them. Moving them later stays a request.
- **2026-10-31: Test counts follow what GUT collects.** Only `tests/**/test_*.gd` counts, so a rename out of the pattern reads as lost tests, and a helper file can't pad the count. `strict` compares its JUnit suites with `listGutFiles` in a `gut:files` row.
- **2026-10-31: `evaluateGut` reads JUnit's failure count.** Exit 0 with `failures="3"` is red. This is stricter than BRIEF 7's list, not looser, and a healthy run never differs: GUT exits non-zero on a failure. It lands now because `gut_gate.mjs` gets pinned next. It also changes `tools/run_tests`' verdict in that one case, so it is the one exception to "only additive flags".
- **2026-10-31: More pins than the critic listed:** the gate's own `gate.mjs`, `gate_core.mjs` and manifest, and all of `addons/wattlands/trainer/**` rather than a list. *Reason:* a pinned rule is only as strong as the code reading it, and the rest of the trainer stack is what cleanup's `deny` already keeps out. After `pin-safety`, a gate fix needs the lead's re-pin.
- **2026-10-31: No GUT config reaches a gate.** `stepGut`, the scaffold step and `gutLane` pass `-gconfig=`, and `check` fails on any `.gutconfig.json`, tracked, untracked or ignored, whatever `--allow` says. *Reason:* GUT loads `res://.gutconfig.json` by default, and one with `failure_error_types` or `unit_test_name` turned failing tests green in `strict`, `fast` and `tools/run_tests` alike. *Evidence, real GUT in a scratch project:* with `{"unit_test_name":"test_passes"}` it ran 1 of 2 tests and exited 0; with `-gconfig=` it ran 2 and exited 1. `-gconfig=` is the one change to the literal `tools/run_tests` here, and it changes no verdict on a tree with no such file.
- **2026-10-31: A file's JUnit count may not fall below its source count,** in `aggregateGut` and in strict's `gut:files` row. *Reason:* GUT collects inner classes only with the `Test` prefix, so moving a failing test into `class Helper` kept the file in the JUnit with one test less, and only the floor's slack was left. *Evidence:* the last real JUnit on this tree (358 suites) has no file below its source, and none above. `Test*` inner-class suites count for their file. Nothing narrows on purpose in a gate, so there is no exception.
- **2026-10-31: Pins reach the helpers, enforcers and pass-rule tools, whole-file only where nobody else writes.** Whole: `tests/scenarios/lib/{scenario_lib,scenario_test}.gd`, `tests/core/helpers/{core_test_util,fakes}.gd`, `tests/trainer/{unlocked_ftms_client,fake_kickr_transport}.gd`, `tests/sdk/helpers/session_kit.gd`, `tests/app/app_test_base.gd`, `core/{app_state,ftp_test}.gd`, `ui/input_router.gd`, `sdk/{sdk_conformance,report_collector}.gd`, `tools/e2e/run.mjs`, `tools/e2e/lib/game.mjs`, `tools/fit_check/check.mjs`. Line pins: the clamps in `settings.gd` (`kit`'s file, audio's paths), the panic dispatch in `game_host.gd` (2500 lines), `skip_warmup` in `session_launcher.gd` (audio's `src/app`) and `game_sim_runner.gd`. *Alternative:* whole-file for all, as the critic's list could read. *Reason:* a whole pin on a file a builder is writing reddens that builder. *Evidence:* no writer of the whole-pinned files in EXECUTION_DAG section 1 or any spec, and no live worktree on 2026-10-31 had changed any of them. Cleanup's planned helper moves (`result`, `pick`) already go through `append_only_moves`. If a batch does move a whole-pinned helper, `check` says "is gone" and the lead re-pins.
- **2026-10-31: The manifest states each game's whole matrix: every checker row and a minimum of runs.** `matrix_required_rows` now lists every row (Tank S1-S7, S9-S13, FIT; Siege S1-S7, S9, S10; Fishing ALL, R1-R6, W1-W3, D, FIT), `matrix_min_runs` holds today's plans (37, 37, 15, deduplicated by report). A plan below the minimum fails the `plan` row, a game the manifest names with no `run_sims.sh` fails (exit 1, no stamp), a `--scenarios` subset answers to neither and stays partial, and `stamp-ok` compares both again. Each script also has line pins on its seeds default, its checker call, its `set -euo pipefail` and, for Siege, its `exit "$status"`. This supersedes the earlier ADR's safety-rows-only list. *Reason:* a hard-coded `--only=S4,S5` or `seeds="1"` in a game's own script read as a full matrix, with `partial` false. *Evidence:* the real plans give 37, 37, 15, and `wt/tank-sims`' live `run_sims.sh` keeps every pinned line. Both are floors, so Tank v4's S14-S18 and about 100 runs pass; **the lead raises Tank's rows and minimum when T-FINAL lands** (the manifest is lead-only).
- **2026-10-31: Floor and pin history read `git log --full-history`.** `highestFloor`, the pins-deleted rule and `strictBase`. *Reason:* plain `git log -- <file>` simplifies away the side of a merge that holds the evidence: after `git merge -s ours main`, a floor raise on main and a pins file main added both disappeared, and all pin rules went quiet. The highest floor in the real history stays 4834.
- **2026-10-31: The merge-revert exemption needs a real merge.** The commit's message must say `This reverts commit <sha>`, the sha must be a merge in HEAD's history, and its diff, compared as plain added and removed lines per file, must be the reverse of that merge's diff to its first parent, so a revert after later commits moved other lines still counts. The subject alone proved nothing: a branch with one commit titled `Revert "Merge nothing"` deleted a test file with no trailer. A revert that needed conflict resolution no longer matches and takes `Test-Removal:` instead.
- **2026-10-31: The quiet rerun allows exit code 1 only.** GUT exits 1 on failed tests; any other code is a crash or a kill, which a rerun must not wash out.
- **2026-10-31: The test-total rule covers Node suites.** `check` counts `test(` and `it(` in the files `node --test` runs (`NODE_SUITES`, `tests/games/<id>`) at the merge base and at HEAD. The total may fall only in a merge revert or with `Test-Removal: node <reason>`. A suite that held tests and holds none needs `Test-Removal: <suite> <reason>`, since `run_tests` reads it as SKIP. Other shrinking suites are notes, because tests move between them. `test.skip(` does not count, so skipping reads as a fall. *Alternative:* no trailer, as for GUT's total. *Reason:* Node tests have no floor to answer to, and a cleanup deleting a dead tool and its tests is legitimate, so the trailer puts the reason in front of the lead instead of stopping the batch.
- **2026-10-31: Known limits of the gate.** The threat model is a hurried agent reaching for green, not a determined author, so these stay open, each found by the critic and not fixed:
  - Append-only pins read JavaScript by lines. A comment-wrapped statement on one line (`/* x */ return ...; /* y */`), a computed or spread entry in `CHECKS` (`['S4']: fake`, `...{S4: fake}`), a top-level `checkS4 = fake`, `Array.prototype.push = ...`, `Object.defineProperty(CHECKS, ...)` and a side effect in a plain push's guard (`r.target_pct++`) all pass `checkPins`.
  - Line pins are a subsequence: a guard added above a pinned line, or a pinned line left in dead code, passes.
  - `lead_branches`' `^queue` also matches `queue-of-mine`: such a branch skips rule 2, and its stamp is not partial.
  - `isBudgetTest` tags the whole test, so a logic assert failing in a test that also measures time (`test_crowd_draws_150_enemies_in_one_batch`) gets the quiet rerun.
  - A stamp does not record which gate code wrote it. `--project=<dir>` runs this checkout's `run_tests.mjs` against another tree, so a stale or edited gate can stamp a tree that `stamp-ok` accepts, and `stamp-ok` does not check `mode`.
  - A matrix's floors count runs and rows, not arguments: a script keeping its run count with changed compliance, crews or padded report names still passes.
  - Node counts read source: a test made in a loop counts once, `{ skip: true }` is not seen, and `tools/run_tests` itself still reads an empty suite as SKIP.
  - The source count reads `func test_` by line, so one inside a multi-line string would be a false red. None exist today.
  - All pin rules are inactive until the lead runs `tools/gate pin-safety`; `floor` refuses until then.

### audio-dsp

Short ADRs for the audio pipeline (`tools/audio`, `assets/_shared/audio`). "SPEC" is the run's SPEC_AUDIO.

#### 2026-10-31 A loop file carries a guard frame; `edit/loop_end` stays -1

- Decision: a loop of N frames is written as N + 1, the last a copy of frame 0, with `edit/loop_end=-1`.
- Alternatives: N frames with -1, as the SPEC's skeleton implied (measured on 4.7.2: Godot stores `frames - 1`, a period one frame short that drops the last frame); N frames with `loop_end=N` (Godot keeps it, but its resampler then reads one frame past the loop end into the zero padding, a small tick each wrap).
- Reason: the period is exactly N and the read past the end lands on frame 0, in PCM and in QOA. `test_audio_assets.gd` holds it through the real importer. Manifest entries carry both `frames` and `loop_frames`.

#### 2026-10-31 A loop's seam goes at the quietest step of its cycle

- Decision: after the crossfade the file is rotated to start where the sample step is smallest.
- Alternatives: leave the seam where the crossfade ends. On noise that pair is 2 sigma past the median step about one time in 25, per channel, and A11 can't be waived, so a few games' beds would fail by chance.
- Reason: a seam no louder than any other pair of samples. A cyclic signal loses nothing by rotating.

#### 2026-10-31 The crossfade adapts to the head and tail's correlation

- Decision: fade gains `u / sqrt(u² + v² + 2ruv)`, with r measured over the crossfade. No per-sound setting.
- Alternatives: equal power (measured: +2.5 dB across the seam of a tone in phase); linear (a dip on noise); a recipe flag that each builder has to get right.
- Reason: constant power for any r, with nothing to choose.

#### 2026-10-31 `warning` and `rescue` play at -16 LUFS momentary, past the UI row's -17

- Decision: as played, both prompts read -16. `HudLayer._play`, the only path they have to `WlSfx`, plays at -4 dB and `hud_layer.gd` is frozen, so the files carry it: file peaks -8 and -10.6 dBFS, file momentary -12. `simulate()` plays a prompt at -4 dB too (`PROMPT_VOICE_DB`, pinned to `hud_layer.gd` by a test), D5 reads that, and A2 holds the prompts as played to -23..-15 and every other UI sound's file to -23..-17.
- Alternatives: -17 and under, the SPEC's row. Measured as played on the fixture, -16 reads D5 +7.83 LU over a -19.31 LUFS mix, +6.53 over -18.13 and +6.03 over -17.67, so -17 would read about +5.5 at the SPEC's own -18 target. Raising the prompts further to cover A1's whole window (-16 LUFS needs about -14 as played) was also rejected: a louder alarm for a mix no bank should aim at.
- Reason: D5 is the safety row and cannot be waived; BRIEF 4.6 outranks a loudness target. A sound here is over the row only by 1 dB, and only for the two prompts. The cost: D5 caps a game's mix at about -17.6 LUFS, inside A1's window, and the scorecard says so to the builder.

#### 2026-10-31 Ticks under 100 ms and the metronome are outside the UI loudness row

- Decision: `ui_move` (50 ms, about -27 momentary) and `click` (12 ms) are not metered. `coin`, `hit`, `boom` and `whoosh` play on Sfx (SPEC 1.4), so the UI row does not apply to them either.
- Alternatives: raise `ui_move` by 4 dB so a 400 ms meter reads -23.
- Reason: a 400 ms block reads a 50 ms tick as silence plus the tick. SPEC 4.5 asks for a soft tick, and raising it makes it louder than a confirm to the ear. `shared.test.mjs` names the excluded sounds, so a new one must be placed.

#### 2026-10-31 The preview's limiter runs 0.25 dB under the kit's ceiling

- Decision: `offline_mix` limits sample peaks to -1.25 dB. The kit's `AudioEffectHardLimiter` stays at -1 dB (SPEC 1.4).
- Alternatives: -1 dB, which read -0.85 dBTP on the fixture, a failure of the unwaivable A1 from intersample peaks alone.
- Reason: A1's -1 dBTP is a true-peak target and the limiter is a sample-peak one. Godot's true peak behind its own limiter is not measured here.

#### 2026-10-31 A3 is measured with a longer window and a floor

- Decision: spectral flux with a 2048 window, hop 512, from the second revolution on, as stdev over mean plus 2 % of the frame magnitude.
- Alternatives: stdev over mean on a 512 window, which passed a steady 55 Hz saw: the window was shorter than its period, and the loops' opening dominated the stdev.
- Reason: A3 exists to catch a monotone rumble. A drone now reads about 0.2 and fails; the fixture's engine reads 1.3.

#### 2026-10-31 Additions to the schema, all of them read only by the generator or the preview

- Decision: `per_variant`, `pitch`, `width`, `xfade_s`, layer `dur`, `glide_s`, swept filter `f`; `preview.others[].lead` and `.rpm_by` (a Tank track, driven by speed), `preview.loops`, `preview.seed`, `preview.checks`, `preview.waive`. `audio_manifest.json` is keyed by file stem and adds `sound`, `variant`, `loop_frames`, `preroll`, `bytes`, `loop`, `locked`, `compress`.
- Alternatives: requests to the lead from each game builder later. The SPEC's list has no way to give variants different pans, which it asks for (gulls left and right), nor to run a machine on hull speed in the preview.
- Reason: game builders do not edit `tools/audio/lib/`. `AudioBank` ignores keys it does not read.

#### 2026-10-31 A third import proof fixture

- Decision: `import_proof_locked` joins the SPEC's two, plus the bank and its manifest under the same prefix.
- Alternatives: read the pre-roll guarantee off `edit/trim=false` alone.
- Reason: SPEC's point is that a locked file's pre-roll survives import, so the test reads the 80 ms of silence out of the imported data.

### audio-kit

#### 2026-10-31: CrankClock lives in addons/wattlands/audio/, verbatim from the run's errata

- **Decision:** `CrankClock` is `addons/wattlands/audio/crank_clock.gd`. No `drive`, `fraction` or `MAX_DT_S`.
- **Alternatives:** `addons/wattlands/sim/` from a separate contract commit, which was dropped.
- **Reason:** one class, one place. A second `class_name CrankClock` fails parse.

#### 2026-10-31: a bank never plays on Master, and no duck touches Master or UI

- **Decision:** `AudioBank.validate` rejects a bank's bus or duck on Master and a duck on UI; `AudioBuses.duck` refuses both with a warning. A game that registers its own `warning` or `rescue` in `WlSfx` still plays on Master.
- **Alternatives:** trusting the bank's author, as the schema's plain "unknown bus" rule would.
- **Reason:** BRIEF 4.6: no sub-slider or duck can hide a safety prompt. The rule is enforced where the data enters.

#### 2026-10-31: validate accepts a bus that exists on the server

- **Decision:** a bank's bus is known if it is one of `AudioBuses.ALL` or exists on `AudioServer`.
- **Alternatives:** the bench puts a capture effect on the real Machines bus.
- **Reason:** the jitter bench machine sits on its own temporary, muted bus, so the bench never sounds on the rider's speakers and never touches a real bus.

#### 2026-10-31: `variant` from CadenceVoice and AmbienceBed is a running count

- **Decision:** the pure classes count firings per sound; `AudioBank.stream` wraps the count by the sound's `variants`.
- **Alternatives:** handing the variant counts to the pure classes, whose `_init` signature is fixed by the spec.
- **Reason:** the pure classes stay free of the bank, and round-robin needs no RNG.

#### 2026-10-31: additions to the kit's spec, all additive

- `CadenceVoice`: `gated` (S2 counts fired + dropped + gated) and `planned_to()`.
- `LayeredLoop`: `close(dt)`, `layer(i)`, `all_off()`. A machine's loops close at `rpm <= STOP_RPM` whatever its layers say (S4), also without `min_rpm`.
- `AmbienceBed.trim_db`: the kit's crossfade (old bed down at 40 dB/s, new one up from -30 dB) and pause drop.
- `GameAudioKit`: `preroll_on` (the bench's baseline), `voiceless`, test accessors. A stroke that may not steal anything is dropped and counted.
- `AudioBank`: `duplicate_keys(text)` reads the raw JSON, because `JSON.parse` keeps only the last of a repeated key. A locked sound must be PCM (its seek must be exact).
- A cue's `duck` runs only for `lead` true: someone else's shot does not duck my machine.

#### 2026-10-31: the jitter bench misses with Godot's delta smoothing, and passes without it

Superseded: `docs/decisions/audio-sync.md` replaces the call below to report the miss and keep the strokes on `revs`.

Measured on WASAPI, 48 kHz, 59.94 Hz display, with other Godot runs on the machine; residual p99 / stddev in ms, limits 12 / 4, thresholds unchanged. All slopes hold (at most 0.056 %, limit 0.1 %), all onsets found, no stroke late or dropped.

| rpm | engine delta, smoothing on (the exit code) | real clock | engine delta, smoothing off | no pre-roll seek |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 60 | 9.05 / 3.83 | 5.85 / 3.15 | 5.69 / 2.99 | 17.15 / 6.93 |
| 90 | 12.11 / 5.30 | 5.61 / 3.08 | 5.15 / 3.05 | 19.78 / 7.89 |
| 110 | 14.18 / 6.88 | 5.70 / 3.40 | 5.52 / 3.16 | 18.48 / 7.86 |
| 140 | 12.07 / 5.34 | 5.73 / 3.08 | 6.06 / 3.20 | 19.61 / 7.77 |

- **Cause:** at 59.94 Hz Godot's delta smoothing hands out 16.667 ms and carries the rest, then pays it back as a whole vsync. `revs` integrates that delta, so it has a sawtooth of up to one vsync against real time, and a run looks good or bad according to where the jumps fall in it (a 60 rpm run measured 6.27 / 3.18 once and 18.75 / 6.71 once). What remains without it, stddev about 3.1, is a uniform spread over one 512-frame mix step (10.67 ms, stddev 3.08): the spec's own bound.
- **Decision:** the kit keeps the spec's strategy, horizon and formula. The miss is reported with these numbers; the thresholds stay. S1 and S3 hold against `revs`: the stroke and the picture share the sawtooth.
- **Alternatives:** `application/run/delta_smoothing=false` in `project.godot` passes every rpm, but it is the lead's file and changes every game's frame delta, so it is the lead's call. Feeding the bench only the real clock would have passed and hidden what a game actually does, so the "engine" run decides the exit code and the "real" run is a diagnosis.

#### 2026-10-31: contract critic's fixes

- **A safety prompt is never stolen.** `WlSfx`'s round-robin skips a player on Master (only `warning` and `rescue` play there). If all eight hold a prompt, a new prompt gets a player past the cap and an effect gets none: a prompt always sounds, and the pool grows only in that case. Alternatives: a reserved player (one prompt only, and it would shrink the pool for everyone).
- **A pause closes `loops` entries** even while the adapter still calls `set_loop`. Spec 2.5 lists only machine loops, but views keep running under a pause, so its literal text would leave a drag scream at full level. Alternative: gating in each adapter, which would need `_paused` public or its own bus connection.
- **A cue duck without times takes spec 5's 10 ms and 350 ms** (`DUCK_ATTACK_S`, `DUCK_RELEASE_S`), the preview's defaults. Alternative: both validators requiring the keys, which touches the dsp's file for no gain.
